| `Integrate` | fixed | 54 | batch 4: indefinite integrals (verified by differentiating back), definite, improper and multiple integrals (the last specification is innermost); options Assumptions, GenerateConditions (Automatic/True give ConditionalExpression[value, cond] when the result needs a parameter condition, e.g. Integrate[x^a, {x, 0, 1}] -> ConditionalExpression[1/(1 + a), Re[a] > -1]; False gives the generic value) and PrincipalValue -> True (Cauchy principal value, Integrate[1/x, {x, -1, 2}, PrincipalValue -> True] -> Log[2]); SymPy convergence conditions are rewritten in Wolfram form (Abs[Arg[a]] < Pi/2 -> Re[a] > 0); integrals that diverge to +-Infinity give that limit, indeterminate (oscillating/cancelling) ones raise; HeavisideTheta and DiracDelta integrands are supported; integrals SymPy cannot do stay unevaluated |
| `InverseSeries` | added | 3 | InverseSeries[s] / InverseSeries[s, y] for a power series s = a1 x + a2 x^2 + ... + O[x]^n about 0 with a1 != 0: the series of the inverse function to the same order (InverseSeries[Series[Sin[x], {x, 0, 5}]] -> x + x^3/6 + 3 x^5/40 + O[x]^6); the result is checked by composing back |
| `Limit` | fixed | 15 | batch 4: Limit[f, x -> a] (two-sided: Indeterminate when the one-sided limits differ), Direction -> "FromAbove" / "FromBelow" / -1 / 1 / "TwoSided", Assumptions, limits at +-Infinity, lists of functions, and multivariate limits Limit[f, {x, y} -> {a, b}] (continuity after cancellation, Indeterminate when two paths disagree, otherwise a clear error); oscillating limits raise (use MaxLimit/MinLimit); legacy rejected every option |
| `MaxLimit` | added | 4 | upper limit (limsup) MaxLimit[f, x -> a] with Direction and Assumptions: the larger one-sided limit, or A + \|B\| for f = A + B Sin[g] / A + B Cos[g] with g -> +-Infinity (MaxLimit[Sin[1/x], x -> 0] -> 1); other oscillating functions raise |
| `MinLimit` | added | 3 | lower limit (liminf) MinLimit[f, x -> a]; see MaxLimit (MinLimit[Sign[x], x -> 0] -> -1) |
| `Normal` | fixed | 138 | batch 4 adds: Normal[series] drops every O term structurally (also the inner ones of multivariate series) and evaluates the remaining polynomial; associations and other heads as before (batch 1) |
| `O` | added | 3 | order term O[x]^n / O[x - x0]^n / O[1/x]^n of Series results (stays symbolic); Normal removes it; SeriesCoefficient, InverseSeries and ComposeSeries read it |
| `Product` | fixed | 7 | batch 4: as Sum (finite, symbolic and infinite products via SymPy; parametric products give the generic value, ConditionalExpression with GenerateConditions -> True) |
| `RSolve` | fixed | 10 | batch 4: RSolve[eqns, a[n], n] -> {{a[n] -> ...}} and RSolve[eqns, a, n] -> {{a -> Function[{n}, ...]}}; linear recurrences with constant or polynomial coefficients and initial conditions (SymPy rsolve); every solution is substituted back into the recurrence and the initial conditions; free constants are C[1], C[2], ...; systems raise |
| `RSolveValue` | added | 4 | RSolveValue[eqns, a[n], n] gives the solution value, RSolveValue[eqns, a, n] a pure function, RSolveValue[eqns, expr, n] the value of expr (e.g. a[10]); checked like RSolve |
| `Series` | fixed | 27 | batch 4: Series[f, {x, x0, n}] with Laurent and Puiseux terms, expansions at +-Infinity in powers of 1/x (O[1/x]^(n+1)), the O term always present (Series[1/x, {x, 0, 2}] -> 1/x + O[x]^3; previously dropped), expansions about x0 grouped in powers of (x - x0) (O[x - x0]^k); several variables Series[f, {x, x0, nx}, {y, y0, ny}] expand in x with coefficients that are series in y; threads over lists; functions SymPy cannot expand raise |
| `SeriesCoefficient` | added | 9 | SeriesCoefficient[f, {x, x0, n}] (negative n for Laurent series, x0 may be Infinity: coefficient of (1/x)^n), several variables {x, x0, nx}, {y, y0, ny}, and SeriesCoefficient[series, n]; a symbolic n gives Piecewise[{{general term, n >= 0}}, 0] when SymPy finds a formal power series (checked against 10 Taylor coefficients); a coefficient beyond a given series' order is Indeterminate |
| `Sum` | fixed | 21 | batch 4: as before ({i, n}, {i, a, b}, {i, a, b, di}, {i, {values}}, several iterators, first outermost; divergent sums to +-Infinity give that limit, other non-convergent sums stay unevaluated), plus parametric series that SymPy sums under a convergence condition: Wolfram's generic value by default (Sum[x^k, {k, 0, Infinity}] -> 1/(1 - x); previously a recursion error) and ConditionalExpression[value, cond] with GenerateConditions -> True |
| `SumConvergence` | added | 7 | SumConvergence[f, n]: True/False for the convergence of Sum[f, {n, k, Infinity}] (SymPy's ratio, root, integral, comparison, alternating-series tests); the p-series family 1/n^p and n^p r^n with a parameter gives the Wolfram condition (Re[p] > 1, Abs[r] < 1); undecided cases stay unevaluated |

### calculus extras (W06)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Antihermitian` | added | 3 | Antihermitian[{s1, s2}]: sign change under conjugate transposition; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `Antisymmetric` | added | 12 | Antisymmetric[{s1, ..., sn}]: sign change under odd permutations; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `ArrayExpand` | added | 4 | ArrayExpand[expr]: expands symbolic-array expressions built from MatrixSymbol/VectorSymbol/ArraySymbol (rank <= 2) and Symbolic*Array with Plus, Times, Dot, Transpose, Inverse, MatrixPower, Tr, Det: distributes Dot over Plus, Transpose[a.b] -> Transpose[b].Transpose[a], Transpose[a + b] -> ..., Inverse[a.b] -> Inverse[b].Inverse[a] (SymPy matrix expressions); explicit arrays are expanded elementwise; anything else raises |
| `ArraySimplify` | added | 7 | ArraySimplify[expr]: simplifies symbolic-array expressions (Transpose[Transpose[a]] -> a, a.Inverse[a] -> identity, a.SymbolicIdentityArray[{n}] -> a, a.SymbolicZerosArray -> zeros, Transpose[a] -> a for MatrixSymbol[.., Symmetric[{1, 2}]] and -a for Antisymmetric) via SymPy matrix expressions; explicit arrays are simplified elementwise |
| `ArraySymbol` | added | 4 | ArraySymbol["A", dims] / [.., dom] / [.., dom, sym]: a symbolic array (inert); rank-1 and rank-2 ArraySymbols take part in ArrayExpand / ArraySimplify like VectorSymbol / MatrixSymbol |
| `Arrays` | added | 3 | Arrays[{d1, ..., dk}] / Arrays[dims, dom] / Arrays[dims, dom, sym]: the domain of arrays of the given dimensions; inert; malformed specifications raise |
| `Asymptotic` | added | 12 | Asymptotic[f, x -> x0]: the leading term of the asymptotic expansion (verified: f/term -> 1); Asymptotic[f, {x, x0, n}]: the expansion to order n; SeriesTermGoal -> k: the first k terms (Taylor, Laurent or Puiseux at finite x0; at Infinity down to x^-n, with exponential/power prefactors and Stirling-type expansions of Gamma, Factorial, Binomial, HarmonicNumber via LogGamma); unexpandable input raises |
| `AsymptoticDSolveValue` | added | 4 | AsymptoticDSolveValue[{ode, ics}, y[x], {x, x0, n} \| x -> x0]: the power-series solution of a (linear or nonlinear) ODE at an ordinary point x0 (Taylor coefficients by repeated differentiation of the solved equation; missing initial values become C[1], C[2], ...); AsymptoticDSolveValue[{ode, ics}, y[x], x, {e, 0, n}]: regular perturbation expansion in a parameter (each order solved with DSolve, residual checked); singular points, singular perturbations and systems raise |
| `AsymptoticEqual` | added | 3 | AsymptoticEqual[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = Theta(g): \|f/g\| bounded above and away from 0 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticEquivalent` | added | 3 | AsymptoticEquivalent[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f ~ g: f/g -> 1 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticExpectation` | added | 3 | AsymptoticExpectation[expr, x \[Distributed] dist, a -> a0 \| {a, a0, n}]: the asymptotic expansion in a parameter of the exact Expectation (computed by Expectation, then expanded); raises when the expectation has no closed form |
| `AsymptoticGreater` | added | 3 | AsymptoticGreater[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = omega(g): \|f/g\| -> Infinity as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticGreaterEqual` | added | 3 | AsymptoticGreaterEqual[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = Omega(g): \|f/g\| bounded away from 0 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticIntegrate` | added | 3 | AsymptoticIntegrate[f, x, x -> x0 \| {x, x0, n}]: asymptotic expansion of an antiderivative (SymPy's antiderivative checked by differentiation, else term-wise integration of the expansion of f, constant 0); AsymptoticIntegrate[f, {t, a, b}, {p, p0, n}]: expansion of a parametric integral (exact integral checked numerically, else term-wise integration of the expansion in p (finite p0, each term finite; the result is checked against numerical quadrature near p0)); divergent term-wise integrals raise |
| `AsymptoticLess` | added | 3 | AsymptoticLess[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = o(g): f/g -> 0 as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticLessEqual` | added | 3 | AsymptoticLessEqual[f, g, x -> x0]: True when f = O(g): \|f/g\| bounded as x -> x0 (decided by the limit of \|f/g\|; oscillating ratios with finite bounds are decided where the bounds suffice), False otherwise; unevaluated when the limit cannot be decided or depends on parameters |
| `AsymptoticProbability` | added | 3 | AsymptoticProbability[pred, x \[Distributed] dist, a -> a0 \| {a, a0, n}]: the asymptotic expansion in a parameter of the exact probability (Probability, or 1 - CDF / CDF for one-sided bounds of continuous distributions); e.g. the Gaussian tail Erfc[a/Sqrt[2]]/2 ~ E^(-a^2/2)/(a Sqrt[2 Pi]); no closed form -> error |
| `AsymptoticProduct` | added | 3 | AsymptoticProduct[f, {k, a, n}, n -> Infinity \| {n, Infinity, m}], AsymptoticProduct[f, {k, a, Infinity}, {p, p0, m}] and the indefinite AsymptoticProduct[f, k, {k, Infinity, m}] (= Product over 1..k-1): from SymPy's closed form (Gamma / rising factorials, checked numerically) expanded with Stirling's series (Product[k, {k, 2, n}] ~ Sqrt[2 Pi] n^(n + 1/2) E^-n); no closed form -> error |
| `AsymptoticRSolveValue` | added | 3 | AsymptoticRSolveValue[{eqn, ics}, y[n], n -> Infinity \| {n, Infinity, m}]: asymptotics of the solution of a recurrence: first-order linear homogeneous recurrences are solved as C[1] times a closed-form product (Gamma functions, verified); others via RSolveValue (verified by substitution); each free constant's part is expanded separately (Stirling / exponential scales; Fibonacci -> GoldenRatio^n/Sqrt[5]); parameter (perturbation) forms and recurrences without a verified closed form raise |
| `AsymptoticSolve` | added | 4 | AsymptoticSolve[eqn, {y, y0}, {x, x0, n}]: the branch of y(x) through (x0, y0) as a Taylor series (implicit function theorem, requires D[eqn, y] != 0 there; Newton iteration on truncated series, residual checked); AsymptoticSolve[eqn, y \| {y}, {x, x0, n} \| x -> x0]: every branch, from SymPy's closed-form roots expanded in Puiseux series; systems raise |
| `AsymptoticSum` | added | 5 | AsymptoticSum[f, {k, a, n}, n -> Infinity \| {n, Infinity, m}]: asymptotics of a partial sum, AsymptoticSum[f, {k, a, Infinity}, {p, p0, m}]: expansion of a parametric infinite sum, AsymptoticSum[f, k, {k, Infinity, m}]: the indefinite sum (the tail convention F(Infinity) = 0 for convergent sums, else Sum over 0..k-1); computed from SymPy's closed form (checked numerically against direct summation) with Euler-Maclaurin expansions of Hurwitz zeta / generalized harmonic numbers; no closed form -> error |
| `C` | added | 3 | C[i] is the i-th constant of integration generated by DSolve, RSolve, AsymptoticDSolveValue and GreenFunction-related solvers (inert; C[i] + C[i] = 2 C[i]) |
| `CaputoD` | added | 8 | CaputoD[f, {x, alpha}]: the Caputo differintegral I^(m-alpha) D^m f (m = Ceiling[alpha]) with lower terminal 0; powers in closed form (constants and x^k with k < alpha give 0; symbolic alpha gives a Piecewise for polynomial terms); other f with numeric alpha via the defining integral, checked against quadrature; threads over lists |
| `Casoratian` | added | 4 | Casoratian[{y1, ..., yk}, n] = Det[{y_j[n + i]}] (i = 0..k-1), simplified; Casoratian[..., n, True] gives the matrix; the equation form Casoratian[eqn, y, n] raises (its normalisation constant is not determined by the equation) |
| `ContourIntegrate` | added | 13 | ContourIntegrate[f, z ∈ cont]: complex contour integral; closed contours (Circle, Disk, Rectangle, Triangle, Polygon boundaries, counterclockwise) of meromorphic f by the residue theorem (poles found exactly); open contours (Line, HalfLine, InfiniteLine, arcs, ParametricRegion[{x[t], y[t]}, ...]) by an antiderivative or direct parametrised integration; every result is checked against numerical quadrature (parameters sampled); poles on the contour and symbolic pole locations raise |
| `CoordinateTransform` | added | 5 | CoordinateTransform["chart1" -> "chart2", pt]: the coordinates of pt in chart2 (charts Cartesian, Polar (n-dimensional hyperspherical), Spherical, Cylindrical; {"chart", n} gives the dimension); threads over a list of points |
| `DEigensystem` | added | 3 | DEigensystem[{L[u[x]], bcs}, u[x], {x, a, b}, n]: {eigenvalues, eigenfunctions} (unnormalised, e.g. Sin[k x]) for the same class of problems as DEigenvalues |
| `DEigenvalues` | added | 3 | DEigenvalues[{L[u[x]], bcs}, u[x], {x, a, b}, n]: the n smallest-magnitude eigenvalues of L u == lambda u for constant-coefficient second-order operators on an interval with Dirichlet (DirichletCondition or u[a] == 0) or Neumann (u'[a] == 0, NeumannValue[0, ...], the default) ends, in closed form; other regions, variable coefficients and Robin/periodic conditions raise |
| `DFixedPoints` | added | 3 | DFixedPoints[eqns, x[t] \| {x1, ...}, t]: the equilibria of an autonomous ODE system, as points of the first-order state (x, x', ..., x^(k-1)) for higher-order equations; sorted; raises for continua |
| `DSolveChangeVariables` | added | 7 | DSolveChangeVariables[Inactive[DSolve][ode, y \| y[x], x], u, t, trans]: changes the variables of a single ODE by the chain rule; trans is t == phi[x] or x == psi[t] (independent variable), y[x] == G[x, u[x]] (dependent variable, t = x) or a list of one of each; returns Inactive[DSolve][ode', u \| u[t], t]; PDEs, systems and named coordinate charts raise |
| `DStabilityConditions` | added | 3 | DStabilityConditions[eqns, x, t] (optionally with a list of points): {{point, condition}, ...} for asymptotic stability from the linearisation (all eigenvalues with negative real part: True/False for numeric Jacobians, Re[l] < 0 conditions otherwise); non-hyperbolic numeric cases raise |
| `DependentVariables` | added | 3 | option of NDSolve/DSolve listing the dependent variables (inert symbol) |
| `DifferenceQuotient` | added | 4 | DifferenceQuotient[f, {x, h}] = (f[x + h] - f[x])/h; {x, n, h}: the n-th forward difference over h^n; several specifications apply in turn; threads over lists |
| `DifferenceRoot` | added | 3 | DifferenceRoot[Function[{y, n}, {rec, ics}]][k]: the solution of a linear recurrence with initial values; evaluated exactly at integers (iterating the recurrence), unevaluated otherwise |
| `DifferenceRootReduce` | added | 4 | DifferenceRootReduce[expr, n]: DifferenceRoot[Function[{y, n}, {rec == 0, ics}]][n] with a linear recurrence with polynomial coefficients (guessed from exact values, then verified symbolically or numerically at non-integer points); DifferenceRootReduce[f &]; DifferenceRootReduce[lhs == rhs, n] gives True when both sides reduce to the same sequence; non-holonomic inputs raise |
| `DifferentialRoot` | added | 3 | DifferentialRoot[Function[{y, x}, {ode, ics}]][x]: the solution of an ODE with initial conditions; exact at the initial point, numerical (mpmath Taylor ODE solver) at inexact arguments, unevaluated otherwise |
| `DifferentialRootReduce` | added | 4 | DifferentialRootReduce[expr, x]: DifferentialRoot[Function[{y, x}, {ode == 0, ics}]][x], a linear ODE with polynomial coefficients (SymPy's holonomic conversion, verified by substitution) with initial conditions at a regular point (0, else 1, 2, ...); DifferentialRootReduce[f &]; non-holonomic inputs raise |
| `DiscreteAsymptotic` | added | 5 | DiscreteAsymptotic[f, n -> Infinity] / {n, Infinity, k}: asymptotic approximation of a sequence (n an integer): Fibonacci, LucasL, factorials, binomials, HarmonicNumber, CatalanNumber are rewritten (Binet, Stirling/LogGamma), (-1)^n-type factors are kept as oscillating factors; dominated terms are dropped; leading terms are verified |
| `DiscreteLimit` | added | 7 | DiscreteLimit[f, n -> Infinity]: the limit of the sequence f(n) over integers n; sequences with periodic oscillation ((-1)^n, Sin[Pi q n], ...) are split into residue classes n = p m + r (Indeterminate when the subsequence limits differ); Fibonacci/factorial/binomial forms are rewritten; threads over lists; unevaluated when undecided (e.g. Sin[n]) |
| `DiscreteLyapunovSolve` | added | 3 | DiscreteLyapunovSolve[a, c]: a.x.a^H - x == c; [a, b, c]: a.x.b - x == c; [{a, d}, c]: a.x.a^H - d.x.d^H == c; [{a, d}, {b, e}, c]: a.x.b - d.x.e == c; exact or numerical; raises when the solution is not unique |
| `DiscreteMaxLimit` | added | 4 | DiscreteMaxLimit[f, n -> Infinity]: lim sup of the sequence f(n) (residue-class splitting for periodic oscillation; e.g. (-1)^n -> 1, Sin[n Pi/3] -> Sqrt[3]/2); unevaluated when undecided |
| `DiscreteMinLimit` | added | 4 | DiscreteMinLimit[f, n -> Infinity]: lim inf of the sequence f(n) (residue-class splitting for periodic oscillation); unevaluated when undecided |
| `DiscreteRiccatiSolve` | added | 3 | DiscreteRiccatiSolve[{a, b}, {q, r}] (or {q, r, p}): the stabilising solution of the discrete algebraic Riccati equation; exact via the stable eigenvectors of the symplectic pencil (invertible a, verified by substitution) for exact input, SciPy's solve_discrete_are for inexact input; unevaluated otherwise |
| `DiscreteVariables` | added | 3 | option of NDSolve listing variables that change only at discrete events (inert symbol) |
| `ExponentialGeneratingFunction` | added | 4 | ExponentialGeneratingFunction[expr, n, x] = Sum[expr x^n/n!, {n, 0, Infinity}] in closed form (SymPy summation, checked against the first Taylor coefficients); raises when no closed form is found |
| `FactorialMomentGeneratingFunction` | added | 4 | FactorialMomentGeneratingFunction[dist, t] = Expectation[t^x] = MomentGeneratingFunction[dist, Log[t]], simplified; raises when the moment generating function is not known |
| `FindGeneratingFunction` | added | 3 | FindGeneratingFunction[{a0, a1, ...}, x]: a rational generating function from a constant-coefficient linear recurrence (at least two confirming terms), else the sum of the FindSequenceFunction term; verified against the series; unevaluated when nothing is found |
| `FindSequenceFunction` | added | 5 | FindSequenceFunction[{a1, a2, ...}, n] (or {{n1, a1}, ...}): rational functions of n, hypergeometric terms (rational successive ratios: products, factorials, powers), constant-coefficient linear recurrences (solved exactly; Fibonacci/LucasL recognised) and sums of hypergeometric differences, each fitted exactly with at least two confirming terms; FindSequenceFunction[list] gives a pure function; stays unevaluated when nothing is found |
| `FractionalD` | added | 10 | FractionalD[f, {x, alpha}]: the Riemann-Liouville differintegral with lower terminal 0; sums of powers c x^p (p > -1) in closed form for any (also symbolic) alpha, Gamma(p+1)/Gamma(p+1-alpha) x^(p-alpha); integer alpha gives D or repeated integrals; other f with numeric alpha via the defining integral (D^m I^(m-alpha) f), checked against quadrature; threads over lists |
| `FrenetSerretSystem` | added | 4 | FrenetSerretSystem[{x[t], y[t]}, t] = {{k}, {T, N}} with the signed curvature k = (x' y'' - y' x'')/\|r'\|^3 and N = T rotated by Pi/2; FrenetSerretSystem[{x, y, z}, t] = {{k, tau}, {T, N, B}} (curvature, torsion, tangent, normal, binormal); t is assumed real; curves of other dimensions raise |
| `FromPolarCoordinates` | added | 4 | FromPolarCoordinates[{r, t}] = {r Cos[t], r Sin[t]}; {r, t1, ..., t(n-1)} -> {r Cos[t1], r Sin[t1] Cos[t2], ..., r Sin[t1] ... Sin[t(n-1)]}; threads over a list of points |
| `FromSphericalCoordinates` | added | 3 | FromSphericalCoordinates[{r, t, p}] = {r Cos[p] Sin[t], r Sin[t] Sin[p], r Cos[t]}; threads over a list of points |
| `FunctionInterpolation` | added | 7 | FunctionInterpolation[expr, {x, xmin, xmax}]: samples expr on a uniform grid (31 points, doubled until the piecewise interpolant of InterpolationOrder (default 3) agrees with expr at every midpoint to 1e-7 relative, at most 8193 points) and returns an InterpolatingFunction; several variables and region specifications raise |
| `GreenFunction` | added | 4 | GreenFunction[{L[u[x]], bcs}, u[x] \| u, {x, a, b}, y]: the Green's function of a linear ODE of any order with homogeneous two-point or initial conditions (homogeneous solutions by DSolve; continuity of the first n-2 derivatives and the jump 1/c_n(y) of the (n-1)th), as HeavisideTheta pieces; u gives Function[{x, y}, ...]; PDEs and problems without a unique Green's function raise |
| `Hermitian` | added | 4 | Hermitian[{s1, s2}]: invariance under conjugate transposition of the two slots; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `HodgeDual` | promoted | 4 | HodgeDual[t]: the Hodge dual of an explicit array whose slots all have dimension n: (1/k!) LeviCivita_{i1..ik j1..j(n-k)} t_{i1..ik} (t is antisymmetrized in effect); HodgeDual[{a, b, c}] = {{0, c, -b}, {-c, 0, a}, {b, -a, 0}}; HodgeDual[x, n] for a scalar is x LeviCivitaTensor[n]; HodgeDual[t, n, slots] dualizes the given slots (the others become the last slots of the result); previously quarantined |
| `IncludeConstantBasis` | added | 3 | option of DesignMatrix, LinearModelFit and related fitting functions: whether to include a constant basis function (default True; inert symbol) |
| `IntegrateChangeVariables` | added | 9 | IntegrateChangeVariables[Inactive[Integrate][f, x \| {x, a, b}], u, u == phi[x] \| x == psi[u]]: integration by substitution, f(psi(u)) psi'(u), returned as Inactive[Integrate][...]; for definite integrals the inverse branch mapping [ua, ub] onto [a, b] is chosen and the result is checked by quadrature; multiple integrals, regions and named coordinate charts raise |
| `InterpolatingPolynomial` | added | 8 | InterpolatingPolynomial[{f1, f2, ...}, x] (nodes 1, 2, ...), [{{x1, f1}, ...}, x] and Hermite data {{x1, f1, df1, ...}, ...} or {{x1, {f1, df1, ...}}, ...}: the lowest-degree interpolating polynomial in Newton/Horner form by (confluent) divided differences; symbolic nodes and values allowed; multidimensional data and the Modulus option raise |
| `InterpolationOrder` | added | 4 | option of Interpolation, ListInterpolation and FunctionInterpolation: the polynomial order of the interpolation (default 3) |
| `InterpolationPoints` | added | 3 | option of FunctionInterpolation: the number of sample points (default 31, refined until the interpolant agrees with the function at the midpoints) |
| `LineIntegrate` | added | 11 | LineIntegrate[f, {x, y, ...} ∈ curve] (scalar, f \|r'\| dt) and LineIntegrate[{p, q, ...}, {x, y, ...} ∈ curve] (vector, F.r' dt) over Line (points in order), HalfLine, InfiniteLine, Circle (counterclockwise; arcs and ellipses) and one-parameter ParametricRegion (increasing parameter); symbolic integration checked against numerical quadrature (parameters sampled); unevaluable integrals raise |
| `LyapunovSolve` | added | 5 | LyapunovSolve[a, c]: a.x + x.a^H == c; [a, b, c]: a.x + x.b == c; [{a, d}, c]: a.x.d^H + d.x.a^H == c; [{a, d}, {b, e}, c]: a.x.e + d.x.b == c; exact (Kronecker linear system) or numerical for inexact input; raises when the solution is not unique |
| `Matrices` | added | 5 | Matrices[{m, n}] / Matrices[{m, n}, dom] / Matrices[{m, n}, dom, sym]: the domain of m x n matrices (sym a Symmetric/Antisymmetric/Hermitian/... specification); inert; malformed specifications raise |
| `MatrixSymbol` | added | 13 | MatrixSymbol["A", {m, n}] / [.., dom] / [.., dom, sym]: a symbolic m x n matrix (inert); ArrayExpand / ArraySimplify treat it as a SymPy MatrixSymbol (symmetry Symmetric[{1, 2}] makes Transpose[A] = A) |
| `NCaputoD` | added | 4 | NCaputoD[f, {x, alpha}, x0]: numerical Caputo differintegral at x0 > 0 (tanh-sinh quadrature of the defining integral of f^(m)) |
| `NContourIntegrate` | added | 3 | NContourIntegrate[f, z ∈ cont]: numerical contour integral along the contours supported by ContourIntegrate (Line, HalfLine, InfiniteLine, Circle, arcs, Disk/Rectangle/Triangle/Polygon boundaries counterclockwise, ParametricRegion) |
| `NFractionalD` | added | 6 | NFractionalD[f, {x, alpha}, x0]: numerical Riemann-Liouville differintegral at x0 > 0 (Caputo integral of f^(m) by tanh-sinh quadrature plus the initial-value terms, or numerical differentiation of the fractional integral when f^(k)(0) is singular) |
| `NLineIntegrate` | added | 3 | NLineIntegrate[f \| {p, q, ...}, {x, ...} ∈ curve]: numerical scalar or vector line integral over the curves supported by LineIntegrate (adaptive Gauss-Legendre/tanh-sinh quadrature) |
| `NSurfaceIntegrate` | added | 3 | NSurfaceIntegrate[f \| {p, q, r}, {x, y, z} ∈ surface]: numerical scalar or flux surface integral over the surfaces supported by SurfaceIntegrate |
| `PrincipalValue` | added | 4 | option of Integrate (and ContourIntegrate): PrincipalValue -> True gives Cauchy principal values (default False); handled by Integrate (batch 4); an inert symbol otherwise |
| `RFixedPoints` | added | 3 | RFixedPoints[eqns, a[n] \| {a1, ...}, n]: the fixed points of an autonomous recurrence system (all shifts equal), as points of the state (a[n], ..., a[n + k - 1]); sorted; raises for continua |
| `RStabilityConditions` | added | 3 | RStabilityConditions[eqns, a, n] (optionally with points): {{point, condition}, ...} for asymptotic stability of the fixed points from the linearisation (all eigenvalues inside the unit circle; a single real parameter gives an interval such as -1 < a < 1, otherwise Abs[l] < 1 conditions) |
| `ResidueSum` | added | 19 | ResidueSum[f, z]: the sum of the residues at all finite poles (rational f: -Res at infinity; entire f: 0; other functions with infinitely many poles raise); ResidueSum[{f, cons}, z]: the residues at the poles satisfying cons (bounded constraints Abs[z - c] < r, or any constraint for rational f), with poles found exactly (polynomial roots, periodic families enumerated, Gamma poles); non-meromorphic functions, poles on the constraint boundary and symbolic pole locations raise |
| `RiccatiSolve` | added | 3 | RiccatiSolve[{a, b}, {q, r}] (or {q, r, p}): the stabilising solution of the continuous algebraic Riccati equation; exact via the stable invariant subspace of the Hamiltonian matrix (verified by substitution) for exact input, SciPy's solve_continuous_are for inexact input; unevaluated when no exact solution is found |
| `SeriesTermGoal` | added | 12 | option of Asymptotic and the Asymptotic* functions: the number of (non-zero) terms of the asymptotic approximation to return for x -> x0 specifications (the expansion order is raised until that many terms appear, then the most dominant are kept; default Automatic: the leading term) |
| `SurfaceIntegrate` | added | 12 | SurfaceIntegrate[f, {x, y, z} ∈ surface] (scalar, f dS) and SurfaceIntegrate[{p, q, r}, ...] (flux F.n dS) over Sphere, Ball (its boundary), Cuboid and Cube (outward normals), Triangle (scalar only) and two-parameter ParametricRegion (normal r_u x r_v); in the plane, boundaries of Disk/Circle/Rectangle/Triangle/Polygon (outward normal) and parametric curves; results checked by numerical quadrature |
| `SymbolicDeltaProductArray` | added | 3 | SymbolicDeltaProductArray[dims, {{s1, t1}, ...}]: the array whose entries are the product of KroneckerDelta[i_s, i_t] over the given slot pairs (pairs disjoint, slots in range); Normal gives the dense array |
| `SymbolicIdentityArray` | added | 5 | SymbolicIdentityArray[{n1, ..., nk}]: the identity array of dimensions {n1, ..., nk, n1, ..., nk} (entries KroneckerDelta[i1, j1] ... KroneckerDelta[ik, jk]); SymbolicIdentityArray[{n}] is the n x n identity matrix; Normal gives the dense array; A.SymbolicIdentityArray[{n}] simplifies to A |
| `SymbolicOnesArray` | added | 3 | SymbolicOnesArray[{d1, ...}]: a symbolic all-ones array (inert); Normal gives the dense array for integer dimensions |
| `SymbolicZerosArray` | added | 4 | SymbolicZerosArray[{d1, ...}]: a symbolic all-zero array (inert); Normal gives the dense array for integer dimensions; ArrayExpand/ArraySimplify use A.0 = 0, A + 0 = A |
| `Symmetric` | added | 17 | Symmetric[{s1, ..., sn}]: symmetry under any permutation of the slots; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |
| `TargetStructure` | added | 5 | option of symbolic-array functions (Normal of SymbolicIdentityArray etc.): "Dense" (default) gives a dense list; other structures raise |
| `TensorExpand` | added | 3 | TensorExpand[expr]: distributes TensorProduct, TensorWedge and Dot over Plus and pulls numeric factors out of them (multilinearity), recursively; symbolic factors are treated as tensors (numeric coefficients only are scalars), so the result is valid for any ranks |
| `TensorReduce` | added | 4 | TensorReduce[expr]: canonical form of tensor polynomials: TensorExpand, then TensorWedge factors of known rank (VectorSymbol, MatrixSymbol, ArraySymbol) are sorted with the Koszul sign (-1)^(p q), a repeated odd-rank factor gives 0, and like terms are collected; explicit arrays evaluate directly |
| `TensorSymmetry` | added | 7 | TensorSymmetry[tensor]: the symmetry of an explicit array: Symmetric[{..}] / Antisymmetric[{..}] when the symmetry group is the full symmetric group on a set of slots (the others fixed), ZeroSymmetric[{1, ..., r}] for a zero array, {} for none, else a generating set {{Cycles[..], phase}, ...} (phases +-1 or roots of unity); also reads the symmetry of MatrixSymbol/ArraySymbol and SymbolicIdentityArray; TensorSymmetry[t, slots] restricts to the given slots |
| `TensorWedge` | added | 11 | TensorWedge[t1, t2, ...]: the exterior product of explicit antisymmetric arrays of equal dimension, normalized as (p + q)!/(p! q!) Alt(t1 (x) t2) (TensorWedge[{a1, a2}, {b1, b2}] = {{0, a1 b2 - a2 b1}, {a2 b1 - a1 b2, 0}}); scalars multiply; symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `ToPolarCoordinates` | added | 5 | ToPolarCoordinates[{x, y}] = {Sqrt[x^2 + y^2], ArcTan[x, y]}; {x1, ..., xn} -> hyperspherical {r, ArcCos[x1/Sqrt[x1^2 + ... + xn^2]], ..., ArcTan[x(n-1), xn]}; threads over a list of points |
| `ToSphericalCoordinates` | added | 3 | ToSphericalCoordinates[{x, y, z}] = {Sqrt[x^2 + y^2 + z^2], ArcTan[z, Sqrt[x^2 + y^2]], ArcTan[x, y]} (radius, polar angle from the z axis, azimuth); threads over a list of points |
| `TransformedField` | added | 4 | TransformedField["chart1" -> "chart2", f, {x1, ...} -> {y1, ...}]: a scalar field f (expression) or a vector field (list of components in the orthonormal frame of chart1) re-expressed in chart2 coordinates {y1, ...} (charts Cartesian, Polar, Spherical, Cylindrical); simplification assumes the standard chart ranges (r > 0, polar angles in (0, Pi)); rank >= 2 tensor fields raise |
| `TruncateSum` | added | 5 | TruncateSum[expr, m]: every Sum and Inactive[Sum] in expr is replaced by the sum of its first m terms (explicit, activated); symbolic m gives Inactive[Sum][f, {i, a, a + m - 1}]; ActivateResult -> False keeps the truncated sums inactive |
| `VectorSymbol` | added | 12 | VectorSymbol["v"] / VectorSymbol["v", n] / VectorSymbol["v", {n}] / VectorSymbol["v", n, dom]: a symbolic vector of length n (inert; used by ArrayExpand, ArraySimplify, TensorDimensions-style functions in this module); bad arguments raise |
| `Vectors` | added | 5 | Vectors[n] / Vectors[n, dom]: the domain of length-n vectors (dom Complexes by default, Reals, ...); inert domain used with Element and VectorSymbol; malformed specifications raise |
| `ZeroSymmetric` | added | 4 | ZeroSymmetric[{s1, ..., sn}]: the symmetry of a zero array; inert symmetry specification used by TensorSymmetry, ArraySymbol, MatrixSymbol, Matrices and Arrays (Symmetric/Antisymmetric/ZeroSymmetric[All]: every slot); malformed slot lists raise |

### combinatorics

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ArithmeticSequence` | fixed | 3 | first n terms a, a + d, ...; exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats and rounded to 10 digits) |
| `Binomial` | fixed | 15 | legacy truncated arguments with int() (Binomial[5/2, 1] = 2) and failed on symbols; now exact/symbolic with Wolfram's conventions for negative integers (Binomial[-1, -1] = 1) |
| `CartesianProduct` | verified | 3 | pairs {a, b} for a in the first list and b in the second (= Tuples[{l1, l2}]); now variadic |
| `Compositions` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned k-subsets of {1..n-1}; now Compositions[n, k] lists all ordered k-tuples of non-negative integers summing to n (Combinatorica convention), lexicographic |
| `Derangements` | verified | 4 | number of derangements !n (MikoshiLang; same as Subfactorial) |
| `DoubleFactorial` | fixed | 4 | negative odd integers supported ((-5)!! = 1/3); legacy truncated reals |
| `Factorial` | fixed | 6 | non-integers use Gamma[n + 1] (Factorial[1/2] = Sqrt[Pi]/2); negative integers give ComplexInfinity; symbols stay unevaluated; legacy truncated with int() |
| `Factorial2` | promoted | 3 | Wolfram name for the double factorial |
| `FibonacciSequence` | verified | 3 | first n Fibonacci numbers F0, F1, ... (MikoshiLang) |
| `GeometricSequence` | fixed | 3 | first n terms a, a r, ...; exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats and rounded) |
| `HookLengthFormula` | fixed | 5 | legacy subtracted the row index from each hook (wrong counts / ZeroDivisionError); now the number of standard Young tableaux of the shape |
| `IntegerPartitions` | fixed | 7 | legacy returned the number of partitions; Wolfram IntegerPartitions lists them in reverse lexicographic order; supports [n], [n, k], [n, {k}], [n, {kmin, kmax}], [n, kspec, {s1, ...} or All] and [n, kspec, sspec, m] (first m partitions, batch 6) |
| `Multinomial` | fixed | 4 | legacy Multinomial[n, {k...}] was not Wolfram's form; now Multinomial[n1, n2, ...] = (n1 + n2 + ...)!/(n1! n2! ...), exact for any exact arguments |
| `PartitionCount` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of PartitionsP |
| `PartitionsList` | fixed | 3 | legacy reused one mutable dict (every entry was the last partition); now the partitions of n in Wolfram IntegerPartitions order |
| `PartitionsP` | promoted | 3 | number of unrestricted partitions |
| `PartitionsQ` | promoted | 3 | number of partitions into distinct parts |
| `Permutations` | fixed | 4 | legacy Permutations[n, k] returned the count n!/(n-k)!; Wolfram lists permutations of a list (duplicates removed); Permutations[list, n] and [list, {n}] supported; integer arguments raise |
| `PowerSet` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of Subsets[list] |
| `Subfactorial` | promoted | 3 | !n |
| `Subsets` | fixed | 12 | Subsets[list], [list, n], [list, {n}], [list, {min, max}], [list, {min, max, step}] in Wolfram order (by size, then position); batch 6 adds Subsets[list, nspec, s] (first s), [list, nspec, {s}] (the s-th, computed by unranking) and [list, nspec, {m, n(, step)}], and any head (Subsets[f[a, b]]) |
| `Tuples` | promoted | 9 | Tuples[list, n] and Tuples[{l1, l2, ...}]; batch 6 adds Tuples[list, {n1, n2, ...}] (all n1 x n2 x ... arrays) and any head (Tuples[f[a, b], 2] = {f[a, a], f[a, b], f[b, a], f[b, b]}) |
| `YoungTableaux` | promoted | 22 | YoungTableaux[shape]: all standard Young tableaux of the partition shape (rows strictly increase left to right and down columns), sorted by row word, as Combinatorica Tableaux[shape]; their number equals HookLengthFormula[shape]. YoungTableaux[n]: all standard tableaux with n cells (over all partitions in reverse-lexicographic order), whose number is the number of involutions of n. Refuses more than 100000 tableaux. Legacy built one tableau and returned its size |

### combinatorics (batch 6)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AbelianGroup` | added | 4 | AbelianGroup[{n1, n2, ...}] inert object (direct product of cyclic groups); GroupOrder supported |
| `AlternatingFactorial` | added | 3 | Sum[(-1)^(n - k) k!, {k, n}] for integers n >= 0 (OEIS A005165); other arguments stay unevaluated |
| `AlternatingGroup` | added | 12 | AlternatingGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
| `Cycles` | added | 72 | canonical form: 1-cycles dropped, each cycle starts with its smallest point, cycles sorted by first point; repeated or non-positive points raise |
| `CyclicGroup` | added | 8 | CyclicGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
| `DihedralGroup` | added | 8 | DihedralGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
| `Fibonorial` | added | 3 | Product[Fibonacci[k], {k, n}] for integers n >= 0 (OEIS A003266); other arguments stay unevaluated |
| `FindPermutation` | added | 6 | FindPermutation[e1, e2]: Cycles p with Permute[e1, p] == e2; FindPermutation[e] relative to Sort[e]; repeated elements (ambiguous) raise |
| `FrobeniusNumber` | added | 7 | largest integer not a non-negative integer combination of the a_i; -1 if some a_i = 1; Infinity when GCD > 1 (round-robin shortest paths over residues of the smallest a_i) |
| `FrobeniusSolve` | added | 6 | all non-negative integer solutions of a.x == b, sorted lexicographically; the 3-argument form (first m solutions) raises because Wolfram's selection order is not reproduced |
| `GroupElementPosition` | added | 3 | position of a permutation (or list of them) in GroupElements[group]; non-members raise |
| `GroupElementQ` | added | 5 | membership test for a permutation in a group |
| `GroupElements` | added | 19 | all elements as Cycles objects sorted by image lists (Wolfram's standard order for a sorted base; the identity is first); GroupElements[g, {r1, ...}] picks positions (negative from the end) |
| `GroupGenerators` | added | 11 | generators of a PermutationGroup (as given, identities removed), SymmetricGroup[n] ({Cycles[{{1, 2}}], Cycles[{{1, ..., n}}]}) and CyclicGroup[n]; other named groups raise because Wolfram's chosen generating sets are not reproduced |
| `GroupMultiplicationTable` | added | 5 | m[[i, j]] = position of PermutationProduct[g_i, g_j] in GroupElements (array indexing) |
| `GroupOrbits` | added | 3 | GroupOrbits[group] orbits of the support points; GroupOrbits[group, {p1, ...}] orbits of the given points; each orbit sorted, orbits ordered by smallest point |
| `GroupOrder` | added | 58 | order of SymmetricGroup (n!), AlternatingGroup (n!/2, 1 for n <= 2), CyclicGroup (n), DihedralGroup (2n), AbelianGroup (product) or a PermutationGroup (Schreier-Sims via SymPy) |
| `Hyperfactorial` | added | 3 | Product[k^k, {k, n}] for integers n >= 0 (Hyperfactorial[0] = 1); approximate real arguments via mpmath hyperfac; exact non-integers stay unevaluated |
| `InversePermutation` | added | 4 | inverse of a Cycles object or permutation list (same representation as the input) |
| `PermutationCycles` | added | 4 | disjoint-cycle form of a permutation list (i -> p_i) or canonical form of a Cycles object |
| `PermutationCyclesQ` | added | 5 | True for a valid Cycles object (disjoint positive integer cycles), else False |
| `PermutationGroup` | added | 24 | PermutationGroup[{g1, ...}] inert group generated by Cycles objects or permutation lists |
| `PermutationLength` | added | 3 | number of points moved |
| `PermutationList` | added | 6 | PermutationList[perm] (length PermutationMax) and PermutationList[perm, n] padded to length n; n smaller than the largest moved point raises |
| `PermutationListQ` | added | 5 | True for a rearrangement of Range[n] (n >= 0) |
| `PermutationMax` | added | 6 | largest moved point; identity -> -Infinity (Max of the empty support) |
| `PermutationMin` | added | 3 | smallest moved point; identity -> Infinity |
| `PermutationOrder` | added | 3 | LCM of the cycle lengths; identity -> 1 |
| `PermutationPower` | added | 5 | n-th power (n any integer, negative = inverse powers) |
| `PermutationProduct` | added | 12 | left-to-right product (apply the first permutation first); PermutationProduct[] = Cycles[{}], PermutationProduct[x] = x; all permutation lists give a list, otherwise Cycles |
| `PermutationReplace` | added | 5 | replaces each positive integer in expr (at any depth in lists) by its image; Cycles objects in expr are relabelled (conjugated); a group gives the list of images under GroupElements |
| `PermutationSupport` | added | 3 | sorted list of the points moved |
| `Permute` | added | 7 | Permute[expr, perm] moves part i to position perm(i) (Permute[{a, b, c}, {2, 3, 1}] = {c, a, b}); a group gives the list over GroupElements |
| `PolygonalNumber` | added | 6 | PolygonalNumber[n] = n (n + 1)/2 (triangular); PolygonalNumber[r, n] = n ((r - 2) n - (r - 4))/2; exact numeric arguments of any kind; symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `RandomPermutation` | added | 5 | RandomPermutation[n] (uniform on Range[n], a Cycles object), [n, k] k of them, [group], [group, k]; reproducible after SeedRandom (the stream differs from Wolfram's generator) |
| `Signature` | added | 8 | sign (+1/-1) of the permutation that sorts the list into canonical order; 0 if any element is repeated |
| `SymmetricGroup` | added | 35 | SymmetricGroup[n] inert group object (permutation representation on 1..n) for GroupOrder, GroupElements, GroupMultiplicationTable, GroupOrbits, Permute, ... |
