### graphics: colours (W14)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Black` | added | 11 | Black evaluates to GrayLevel[0], as documented by Wolfram; an assignment by the user takes precedence. Check: reference (Wolfram documentation value) in tests. |
| `Blend` | added | 13 | Blend[{c1, c2, ...}] (equal parts), Blend[{c1, ..., cn}, x] (piecewise-linear from c1 at 0 to cn at 1; x clipped to [0, 1]), Blend[{{x1, c1}, {x2, c2}, ...}, x] (ci at xi, clipped to [x1, xn]) and Blend[{c1, ...}, {u1, ...}] (fractions normalised to total 1). Additive mixing: colours all in RGBColor or all in GrayLevel mix in that space, any other mixture is converted to RGBColor; opacities mix when any colour has one. Machine-real result. Symbolic x or colours stay unevaluated; non-colours raise TypeError, negative or all-zero fractions ValueError, images NotImplementedError. Check: reference (Wolfram documentation examples, e.g. Blend[{Red, Blue}] = RGBColor[0.5, 0., 0.5]) and the identity Lighter[c, f] = Blend[{c, White}, f] in tests. |
| `Blue` | added | 43 | Blue evaluates to RGBColor[0, 0, 1], as documented by Wolfram; an assignment by the user takes precedence. Check: reference (Wolfram documentation value) in tests. |
| `Brown` | added | 5 | Brown evaluates to RGBColor[0.6, 0.4, 0.2], as documented by Wolfram; an assignment by the user takes precedence. Check: reference (Wolfram documentation value) in tests. |
| `CMYKColor` | added | 7 | CMYKColor[c, m, y] (k = 0), CMYKColor[c, m, y, k], CMYKColor[c, m, y, k, a] and the list form are inert; CMYKColor[color] converts with the device-independent formulas k = 1 - max(r, g, b), c = (1 - r - k)/(1 - k) (and back r = (1 - c)(1 - k)); no ICC profile is applied (Wolfram may use a profile in some versions). Symbolic stays unevaluated; wrong kinds TypeError, counts ValueError. Check: reference (Wolfram documentation values, sRGB/CIE formulas) and round trips through ColorConvert in tests. |
| `ColorConvert` | added | 20 | ColorConvert[color, space] for space "Grayscale", "RGB", "CMYK", "HSB", "XYZ", "LAB", "LCH", "LUV" (machine-real channels; opacity kept; a colour already in space is returned unchanged); ColorConvert[{c1, c2, ...}, space] maps; ColorConvert[color, src -> dst] reinterprets the channels of color in src. Conversions: sRGB with D65 for Grayscale/RGB/CMYK/HSB, CIE spaces with D50 (Bradford matrix), channels clipped to [0, 1] only for Grayscale/RGB/CMYK/HSB targets, as documented. WhitePoint -> Automatic \| "D50" only (other white points raise NotImplementedError), images and ColorProfileData raise NotImplementedError, raw channel lists raise NotImplementedError, an unknown space ValueError, a non-colour TypeError; symbolic input stays unevaluated. Check: reference (sRGB/CIE formulas, Wolfram documentation values) and round trips in tests. |
| `ColorDistance` | added | 27 | ColorDistance[c1, c2] Euclidean distance of the LABColor values (CIE76 on Wolfram's 1/100 scale, opacity ignored); ColorDistance[list, c], [c, list] and [list1, list2] (equal lengths) thread. DistanceFunction -> "CIE76" (default), "CIE94" (graphic arts, kL = 1), "CIE2000" (Sharma et al. 2005), {"CMC", {l, c}}, {"CMC", "Perceptibility" \| "Acceptability"} (1:1, 2:1), "DeltaL", "DeltaC", or a function f applied to the two {l, a, b} lists; "DeltaH" raises NotImplementedError (undocumented scaling); the ΔE formulas run on the 0-100 scale and are divided by 100. Images raise NotImplementedError, non-colours TypeError, unequal list lengths ValueError; symbolic colours stay unevaluated. Check: reference (Wolfram/Mathics documentation values, Sharma's CIEDE2000 test data) in tests. |

Check: exact (structural test) in tests. |

Matching against it (in ColorReplace, ImageValuePositions) belongs to the image batches. Symbolic input stays unevaluated; a non-colour raises TypeError, d <= 0 or an unknown distance ValueError. | `FindMatchingColor` | added | 16 | FindMatchingColor[{col1, col2, ...}, color] gives the coli closest to color (the element itself, first one on ties) and FindMatchingColor[{col1, ...}, {color1, color2, ...}] a list of matches. MaxColorDistance must be given explicitly: a positive number d (a candidate further than d gives Missing["NotFound"]; the Missing tag is not documented by Wolfram) or Infinity (always the nearest, as documented). DistanceFunction takes any ColorDistance setting (default Automatic, i.e. CIE76 as in ColorDistance). Not supported (NotImplementedError): MaxColorDistance -> Automatic (Wolfram's default threshold is not documented), the image forms (they need DominantColors, batch W21), and ColorCoverage or MinColorDistance other than Automatic (they apply to images). A non-colour or a non-list first argument raises TypeError, an empty colour list, a bad MaxColorDistance or an unknown option ValueError; symbolic colours stay unevaluated. Check: self-check (the returned colour is re-measured against every candidate and must be minimal and within d, else ArithmeticError) and reference cases (independent CIE76 distances) in tests. |

Contents are checked: numbers or strings as primitives raise TypeError, a Graphics inside Graphics or a 3D-only primitive ValueError; options must be known Graphics options (AspectRatio, PlotRange, Axes, Background, ImageSize, ...; unknown names raise ValueError, deviating from Wolfram's message-only behaviour) and known settings are validated. Check: reference (Wolfram documentation forms) in tests. |
| `Graphics3D` | added | 55 | Graphics3D[primitives, options] inert 3D graphics object; contents checked as for Graphics (2D-only primitives such as Raster raise ValueError); the 3D options Boxed, BoxRatios, BoxStyle, ClipPlanes, Lighting, ViewPoint, ViewVector, ViewAngle, ViewCenter, ViewVertical, ViewRange, ViewMatrix, ViewProjection, RotationAction, RenderingOptions, ... are validated; unknown options raise ValueError. Rendering of 3D graphics is not available yet (Rasterize raises NotImplementedError). Check: reference (Wolfram documentation forms) in tests. |
| `GraphicsColumn` | added | 4 | GraphicsColumn[{g1, g2, ...}], GraphicsColumn[list, alignment], GraphicsColumn[list, alignment, spacing] and options inert layout of graphics in a column (alignment Left, Center, Right or Automatic; Wolfram expands it into Graphics with Insets; here it stays a GraphicsColumn object). Bad alignments or spacings raise ValueError. Check: reference in tests. |
| `GraphicsComplex` | added | 14 | GraphicsComplex[{pt1, pt2, ...}, data, opts] inert: integers i used as coordinates in primitives of data stand for pti. The points must form a numeric n x 2 or n x 3 array (strings or ragged lists raise TypeError/ValueError), indices in point-list primitives must lie in 1..n (else ValueError), and VertexColors / VertexNormals / VertexTextureCoordinates must have one entry per point. Normal[GraphicsComplex[...]] substitutes the coordinates (and per-vertex options) into the primitives. Check: exact (Normal substitution) and reference values in tests. |

(unknown options raise ValueError). Deviation: Wolfram expands the layout into a Graphics of Insets; here it stays a GraphicsGrid object. A non-matrix first argument raises TypeError/ValueError. Check: reference in tests. |

### graphics: rendering (W14)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Rasterize` | added | 17 | Rasterize[g, "Data"] the pixel array (integers 0-255, top row first, {r, g, b} or {r, g, b, a} with Background -> None) of a 2D Graphics drawn with matplotlib; Rasterize[g, "RasterSize"] its {width, height}; Rasterize[g, {elem1, ...}] a list. Options ImageSize (points, default 360 wide; Tiny/Small/Medium/Large), RasterSize (pixels), ImageResolution (dpi, default 72) and Background. Drawn: Point, Line, Polygon, Rectangle, Disk, Circle (and sectors), Text, Arrow, Raster, GraphicsComplex, GraphicsGroup, Style, Annotation, Translate/Rotate/Scale, colours, Opacity, Thickness, PointSize, Dashing, EdgeForm, FaceForm, CapForm, JoinForm, Arrowheads[s], Directive; PlotRange, PlotRangePadding, AspectRatio. Anything else (Graphics3D, axes, frames, gradient fillings, insets, ...) raises NotImplementedError rather than being left out; Rasterize[g] / "Image" needs the Image object (image batch W21) and raises NotImplementedError until then. Pixels approximate, not reproduce, Wolfram's renderer. Requires matplotlib and numpy (NotImplementedError otherwise); symbolic input stays unevaluated. Check: self-check of the raster shape against RasterSize, and pixel read-back of filled regions in tests. |

### graphs

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BetweennessCentrality` | fixed | 9 | Graph or edge-rule input: list of unnormalised betweenness centralities in VertexList order (machine reals; directed graphs count ordered pairs; EdgeWeight respected); legacy: {v -> c} unnormalised betweenness (Wolfram BetweennessCentrality); legacy returned NetworkX's normalised values; exact rationals (Brandes, unweighted) |
| `BipartiteQ` | verified | 3 | 2-colourability test (BFS) |
| `ChromaticNumber` | fixed | 4 | exact chromatic number by backtracking (legacy used a greedy colouring, an upper bound only); graphs with more than 40 vertices raise |
| `ClusteringCoefficient` | fixed | 3 | ClusteringCoefficient[g] = mean local clustering (vertices of degree < 2 count 0, as Wolfram MeanClusteringCoefficient), [g, v] local coefficient; exact rationals (legacy floats) |
| `ConnectedComponents` | fixed | 9 | Graph or edge-rule input: connected components (strongly connected for directed graphs), largest first, vertices in VertexList order; legacy: components largest first (Wolfram), vertices in order of appearance; legacy returned NetworkX set order |
| `ConnectedQ` | verified | 3 | True when the graph is connected |
| `Dijkstra` | fixed | 3 | {v -> distance} for every reachable vertex, in vertex order |
| `GraphCenter` | fixed | 7 | vertices of minimum eccentricity; disconnected graphs raise; legacy: vertices of minimum eccentricity; disconnected graphs raise (legacy returned {} silently) |
| `GraphDiameter` | fixed | 7 | maximum eccentricity (Infinity when not (strongly) connected); weighted with EdgeWeight; legacy: maximum eccentricity; Infinity for disconnected graphs (legacy returned None) |
| `GraphRadius` | fixed | 7 | minimum eccentricity (Infinity when not (strongly) connected); legacy: minimum eccentricity; Infinity for disconnected graphs (legacy returned None) |
| `MaximalMatching` | verified | 3 | a maximum-cardinality matching (NetworkX max_weight_matching with unit weights), edges sorted; requires NetworkX |
| `MinimumSpanningTree` | fixed | 3 | edges of a minimum spanning forest (Kruskal, weights {u, v, w} supported); legacy needed NetworkX |
| `PageRank` | fixed | 3 | {v -> rank} PageRank with damping 0.85 treating each edge as two directed links (power iteration to 1e-12, matches NetworkX); legacy required NetworkX |
| `PathLength` | fixed | 3 | (weighted) shortest-path distance, Infinity when unreachable; unknown vertices raise |
| `ShortestPath` | fixed | 4 | vertex list of a shortest (weighted) path, {} when unreachable (Wolfram FindShortestPath); unknown vertices raise |
| `SpanningTree` | fixed | 3 | edges of a spanning forest (Kruskal) |
| `VertexDegree` | fixed | 12 | VertexDegree[g] = degrees in VertexList order, VertexDegree[g, v]; self-loops count 2, multi-edges counted; directed: in + out; legacy: number of incident edges (self-loops count twice, multi-edges counted); unknown vertices raise |

### graphs (batch 5)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AcyclicGraphQ` | added | 5 | undirected: a forest (self-loops and multi-edges are cycles); directed: no directed cycle |
| `AdjacencyGraph` | added | 6 | graph from an adjacency matrix (undirected when symmetric, else directed; entries > 1 give multi-edges, diagonal entries self-loops); AdjacencyGraph[{v...}, m] names the vertices |
| `AdjacencyList` | added | 4 | AdjacencyList[g, v] = vertices adjacent to v (either direction), in VertexList order; AdjacencyList[g] for every vertex |
| `AdjacencyMatrix` | added | 10 | dense form (Normal) of Wolfram's SparseArray: a_ij = number of edges i -> j, an undirected edge counts both ways, a self-loop adds 1 on the diagonal; the empty graph raises |
| `BipartiteGraphQ` | added | 6 | 2-colourable (edge directions ignored; a self-loop makes it non-bipartite) |
| `BreadthFirstScan` | added | 3 | BreadthFirstScan[g, s, {"event" -> f, ...}] calls f on events ("DiscoverVertex" f[u, v, d], "PrevisitVertex", "PostvisitVertex", "FrontierEdge", "CycleEdge", "UnvisitedVertex") in breadth-first order (neighbours in edge order) and returns g; use with Reap/Sow |
| `ChromaticPolynomial` | added | 10 | chromatic polynomial in k (deletion-contraction with memoisation, expanded); multi-edges are merged, self-loops give 0; directions ignored; graphs with more than 30 edges raise |
| `CirculantGraph` | added | 3 | CirculantGraph[n, {j1, ...}]: i <-> i + j (mod n) on 1..n, sorted edges |
| `ClosenessCentrality` | added | 6 | 1/(mean distance to the reachable vertices), 0 for vertices that reach nothing; machine reals |
| `CompleteGraph` | added | 31 | K_n on 1..n (edges i <-> j, i < j, lexicographic); CompleteGraph[{n1, n2, ...}] complete multipartite graph |
| `CompleteGraphQ` | added | 3 | simple undirected graph with every pair of distinct vertices adjacent (directed: every ordered pair) |
| `CompleteKaryTree` | added | 3 | CompleteKaryTree[n, k] (default k = 2): complete k-ary tree with n levels, vertices numbered breadth first |
| `ConnectedGraphComponents` | added | 3 | Subgraph for each ConnectedComponents element |
| `ConnectedGraphQ` | added | 5 | True for a connected graph (strongly connected when directed); the empty graph is not connected |
| `CycleGraph` | added | 43 | C_n on 1..n: 1 <-> 2, ..., (n-1) <-> n, 1 <-> n; n >= 3 |
| `DegreeCentrality` | added | 4 | DegreeCentrality[g] (= VertexDegree), [g, "In"], [g, "Out"] |
| `DepthFirstScan` | added | 3 | DepthFirstScan[g, s, {"event" -> f, ...}]: as BreadthFirstScan, in depth-first order; returns g |
| `DirectedEdge` | added | 3 | inert edge object (u <-> v is TwoWayRule, converted to UndirectedEdge inside Graph) |
| `DirectedGraph` | added | 3 | each undirected edge u <-> v becomes the pair u -> v, v -> u (Wolfram's default conversion) |
| `DirectedGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when every edge is directed (an edgeless graph is undirected) |
| `EdgeAdd` | added | 3 | adds edges (and any new vertices); weights are not carried over |
| `EdgeCapacity` | added | 5 | Graph option for FindMaximumFlow capacities |
| `EdgeConnectivity` | added | 4 | minimum number (total weight) of edges whose removal disconnects an undirected graph; s-t form EdgeConnectivity[g, s, t] via maximum flow |
| `EdgeCount` | added | 20 | number of edges (multi-edges and self-loops counted) |
| `EdgeDelete` | added | 3 | removes every edge matching the given edges (undirected edges match either orientation) |
| `EdgeIndex` | added | 3 | position of e in EdgeList[g]; unknown edges raise |
| `EdgeList` | added | 5 | edges in Graph order (Rule given as DirectedEdge) |
| `EdgeQ` | added | 4 | True when e is an edge of g (an undirected edge matches either orientation) |
| `EdgeWeight` | added | 20 | Graph option; EdgeWeight[g] is not supported (use AnnotationValue) |
| `EdgeWeightedGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when the graph carries EdgeWeight |
| `EigenvectorCentrality` | added | 7 | principal eigenvector of the adjacency matrix normalised to total 1 ("In": left eigenvector, the default for directed graphs; "Out": right eigenvector); graphs whose principal eigenvalue is not simple (e.g. disconnected) raise; machine reals |
| `EmptyGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when the graph has no edges |
| `EulerianGraphQ` | added | 4 | True when there is a closed walk using every edge exactly once (even degrees / balanced in- and out-degrees, edges connected); edgeless graphs are not Eulerian |
| `FindClique` | added | 7 | FindClique[g] = {a largest clique}; [g, n] a maximal clique of at most n vertices... (largest found), [g, {n}] a clique of exactly n vertices, [g, nspec, All] every such clique; vertices in VertexList order; directed graphs need edges both ways |
| `FindCycle` | added | 11 | FindCycle[g] = {cycle} as a list of edges in traversal order ({} for acyclic graphs); [g, k] length at most k, [g, {k}] exactly k, [g, kspec, n] at most n cycles (All for all); shortest cycles first |
| `FindEulerianCycle` | added | 7 | {cycle} as edges in traversal order (Hierholzer, multigraphs and self-loops allowed), {} when the graph is not Eulerian |
| `FindGraphIsomorphism` | added | 3 | {<\|v1 -> w1, ...\|>} for one isomorphism (VF2 via NetworkX), {} when none; [g1, g2, n] / All for several; keys in VertexList order of g1 |
| `FindHamiltonianCycle` | added | 7 | {cycle} as edges in traversal order starting at the first vertex, {} when the graph is not Hamiltonian (exact backtracking; graphs above 30 vertices raise) |
| `FindHamiltonianPath` | added | 3 | a Hamiltonian path as a vertex list, {} when none (exact backtracking; graphs above 30 vertices raise) |
| `FindIndependentVertexSet` | added | 5 | {a largest independent vertex set} (maximum clique of the complement; edge directions ignored); [g, {n}] an independent set of exactly n vertices; vertices in VertexList order |
| `FindMaximumFlow` | added | 7 | value of a maximum s-t flow (Edmonds-Karp; capacities from EdgeCapacity, default 1; undirected edges carry flow either way); exact for exact capacities |
| `FindMinimumCut` | added | 7 | {c, {part1, part2}}: a global minimum cut of a connected undirected graph (Stoer-Wagner, EdgeWeight respected; parts in VertexList order, the part with the first vertex first); disconnected graphs give 0; directed graphs raise |
| `FindPath` | added | 6 | FindPath[g, s, t] = {path} with one simple path ({} when none); FindPath[g, s, t, k] paths of length at most k, [g, s, t, {k}] exactly k, [g, s, t, kspec, n] at most n paths (All for every path); paths are enumerated shortest first; unweighted lengths |
| `FindShortestPath` | added | 7 | vertex list of a shortest path (BFS, or Dijkstra with EdgeWeight), {} when t is unreachable; unknown vertices raise |
| `FindSpanningTree` | added | 6 | a minimum spanning tree (forest when disconnected) as a Graph on all the vertices (Kruskal with EdgeWeight, edges kept in the input orientation); directed graphs use the underlying undirected graph |
| `FindVertexCover` | added | 4 | a minimum vertex cover (complement of a maximum independent set), as a flat vertex list in VertexList order |
| `GlobalClusteringCoefficient` | added | 4 | 3 x triangles / connected triples (transitivity), exact; 0 when there are no triples; undirected graphs |
| `Graph` | added | 284 | Graph[{e...}] and Graph[{v...}, {e...}] normalise to Graph[{v...}, {e...}, opts] (Rule -> DirectedEdge, TwoWayRule -> UndirectedEdge, vertices in order of first appearance); EdgeWeight/EdgeCapacity/VertexWeight options are used by the algorithms, presentation options are kept; unknown options, tagged edges and edges to unlisted vertices raise |
| `GraphComplement` | added | 4 | same vertices; undirected: u <-> v (VertexList order) for every non-adjacent pair; directed: every missing u -> v; requires a simple (loop-free, no multi-edge) graph |
| `GraphDensity` | added | 5 | m / (n (n - 1)/2) for undirected, m / (n (n - 1)) for directed graphs (exact) |
| `GraphDifference` | added | 3 | vertices of g1 with the edges of g1 that are not in g2; multigraphs raise |
| `GraphDisjointUnion` | added | 3 | disjoint union with vertices relabelled 1, 2, ... in order (Wolfram's relabelling) |
| `GraphDistance` | added | 5 | GraphDistance[g, s, t] shortest distance (Infinity when unreachable); GraphDistance[g, s] to every vertex |
| `GraphDistanceMatrix` | added | 7 | matrix of shortest distances in VertexList order (Infinity when unreachable) |
| `GraphIntersection` | added | 3 | common vertices and common edges (order of the first graph); multigraphs raise |
| `GraphPeriphery` | added | 4 | vertices of maximum eccentricity; disconnected graphs raise |
| `GraphQ` | added | 4 | True for a valid Graph expression |
| `GraphUnion` | added | 3 | vertices and edges of all the graphs (first occurrence order); multigraphs and weights raise |
| `GridGraph` | added | 14 | GridGraph[{n1, ..., nk}]: vertices 1..n1*...*nk with the first coordinate varying fastest; edges between vertices one step apart in one coordinate, sorted |
| `HITSCentrality` | added | 4 | {authorities, hubs}: authority = principal eigenvector of A^T.A normalised to total 1, hub = A.authority (Wolfram's definition); degenerate principal eigenvalues raise; machine reals |
| `HamiltonianGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when a Hamiltonian cycle exists |
| `HypercubeGraph` | added | 6 | Q_n on 1..2^n: i <-> j when the binary digits of i - 1 and j - 1 differ in one place |
| `IncidenceMatrix` | added | 4 | vertices x edges: undirected 1 at each end (2 for a self-loop); directed -1 at the tail and 1 at the head; directed self-loops raise |
| `IndependentVertexSetQ` | added | 3 | True when no two listed vertices are adjacent |
| `IndexGraph` | added | 3 | vertices renamed 1, 2, ... in VertexList order (IndexGraph[g, r] starts at r) |
| `IsomorphicGraphQ` | added | 6 | graph isomorphism (VF2 via NetworkX), direction-aware; multigraphs raise |
| `KaryTree` | added | 7 | KaryTree[n, k] (default k = 2): n vertices 1..n, the children of i are k(i-1)+2, ..., k(i-1)+k+1 |
| `KirchhoffMatrix` | added | 4 | Laplacian D - A of an undirected loop-free graph (multi-edges counted); directed graphs and self-loops raise |
| `LineGraph` | added | 3 | undirected simple graphs: vertices 1..m for the edges, i <-> j when edges i and j share an endpoint |
| `LocalClusteringCoefficient` | added | 4 | fraction of pairs of neighbours that are adjacent (0 for degree < 2), exact; undirected simple graphs |
| `LoopFreeGraphQ` | added | 3 | no self-loops |
| `MeanClusteringCoefficient` | added | 4 | mean of LocalClusteringCoefficient over all vertices, exact; undirected graphs |
| `MixedGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when the graph has both directed and undirected edges |
| `MultigraphQ` | added | 3 | has multiple edges between some pair of vertices |
| `NeighborhoodGraph` | added | 4 | subgraph induced by the vertices within distance k (default 1) of v (undirected graphs; edges followed both ways for directed graphs) |
| `PageRankCentrality` | added | 6 | PageRankCentrality[g, alpha] (default 0.85): solution of c = alpha A^T D^-1 c + (1 - alpha)/n normalised to total 1, undirected edges counted both ways; graphs with a vertex of out-degree 0 raise (Wolfram's dangling-vertex convention is not reproduced); machine reals |
| `PathGraph` | added | 12 | PathGraph[{v1, ..., vn}]: v1 <-> v2 <-> ... <-> vn |
| `PetersenGraph` | added | 34 | generalised Petersen graph GP(n, k) (default GP(5, 2)): outer cycle 1..n, spokes i <-> n + i, inner edges n + i <-> n + (i + k mod n) |
| `PlanarGraphQ` | added | 4 | planarity test (edge directions ignored) via NetworkX's left-right planarity algorithm |
| `RandomGraph` | added | 6 | RandomGraph[{n, m}] uniformly random simple undirected graph with n vertices and m edges; RandomGraph[{n, m}, k] a list of k; reproducible with SeedRandom (the random stream differs from Wolfram's) |
| `ReverseGraph` | added | 3 | reverses every directed edge; undirected edges unchanged |
| `SimpleGraphQ` | added | 4 | undirected, without self-loops or multiple edges |
| `StarGraph` | added | 13 | S_n on 1..n with centre 1: 1 <-> 2, ..., 1 <-> n |
| `Subgraph` | added | 4 | Subgraph[g, {v...}] = subgraph induced by the vertices (VertexList order kept); edges in the list are included with their endpoints |
| `TopologicalSort` | added | 5 | a topological order of a directed acyclic graph (Kahn's algorithm, ties broken by VertexList order); graphs with a cycle or undirected edges raise |
| `TreeGraphQ` | added | 7 | connected acyclic undirected graph (n - 1 edges); directed graphs: True for an out-tree with every vertex reachable from one root along the underlying tree |
| `TuranGraph` | added | 3 | TuranGraph[n, k]: complete k-partite graph on 1..n with parts as equal as possible, consecutive |
| `TwoWayRule` | added | 3 | inert edge object (u <-> v is TwoWayRule, converted to UndirectedEdge inside Graph) |
| `UndirectedEdge` | added | 5 | inert edge object (u <-> v is TwoWayRule, converted to UndirectedEdge inside Graph) |
| `UndirectedGraph` | added | 3 | every edge made undirected; for directed input, u -> v and v -> u become one edge (duplicates merged) |
| `UndirectedGraphQ` | added | 4 | True when every edge is undirected |
| `VertexAdd` | added | 3 | adds new vertices (existing ones are ignored) |
| `VertexCount` | added | 10 | number of vertices |
| `VertexCoverQ` | added | 3 | True when every edge has an endpoint in the list |
| `VertexDelete` | added | 3 | removes the vertices and their incident edges |
| `VertexEccentricity` | added | 5 | maximum distance from v to the other vertices; Infinity when some vertex is unreachable |
| `VertexInComponent` | added | 3 | vertices that can reach v (v first, then breadth-first order); VertexInComponent[g, v, k] within k steps |
| `VertexInDegree` | added | 3 | in-degrees in VertexList order or of one vertex; for undirected edges in- and out-degree equal the degree |
| `VertexIndex` | added | 3 | position of v in VertexList[g]; unknown vertices raise |
| `VertexList` | added | 4 | vertices in Graph order |
| `VertexOutComponent` | added | 3 | vertices reachable from v (v first, then breadth-first order); VertexOutComponent[g, v, k] within k steps |
| `VertexOutDegree` | added | 3 | out-degrees in VertexList order or of one vertex; for undirected edges in- and out-degree equal the degree |
| `VertexQ` | added | 3 | True when v is a vertex of g |
| `VertexWeight` | added | 4 | Graph option for vertex weights |
| `VertexWeightedGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when the graph carries VertexWeight |
| `WeaklyConnectedComponents` | added | 3 | components ignoring edge direction, largest first, vertices in VertexList order |
| `WeaklyConnectedGraphQ` | added | 3 | connected when edge directions are ignored |
| `WeightedAdjacencyMatrix` | added | 4 | a_ij = weight of the edge i -> j (0 when absent); multigraphs raise |
| `WeightedGraphQ` | added | 3 | True when the graph carries edge or vertex weights |
| `WheelGraph` | added | 3 | W_n on 1..n: hub 1 joined to the cycle 2..n; n >= 4 |

### group theory (W09)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BabyMonsterGroupB` | added | 3 | BabyMonsterGroupB[] represents the Baby Monster group B, of order 4154781481226426191177580544000000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `ConwayGroupCo1` | added | 3 | ConwayGroupCo1[] represents the Conway group Co1, of order 4157776806543360000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `ConwayGroupCo2` | added | 3 | ConwayGroupCo2[] represents the Conway group Co2, of order 42305421312000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `ConwayGroupCo3` | added | 3 | ConwayGroupCo3[] represents the Conway group Co3, of order 495766656000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `CycleIndexPolynomial` | added | 5 | CycleIndexPolynomial[perm, {x1, ..., xn}] = Product[x_k^(number of k-cycles)] (fixed points up to PermutationMax, or up to the degree given as a third argument, count as 1-cycles); for a group the average over its elements |
| `FischerGroupFi22` | added | 3 | FischerGroupFi22[] represents the Fischer group Fi22, of order 64561751654400; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `FischerGroupFi23` | added | 3 | FischerGroupFi23[] represents the Fischer group Fi23, of order 4089470473293004800; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `FischerGroupFi24Prime` | added | 3 | FischerGroupFi24Prime[] represents the Fischer group Fi24', of order 1255205709190661721292800; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `GroupActionBase` | added | 5 | option for GroupElements and GroupStabilizerChain: GroupActionBase -> {b1, b2, ...} orders the elements by their images of the base points (then the remaining points), and starts the stabilizer chain with the given base |
| `GroupCentralizer` | added | 3 | GroupCentralizer[group, g]: the subgroup of elements commuting with the permutation g, as a PermutationGroup (generators may differ from the Wolfram Language's choice) |
| `GroupElementFromWord` | added | 6 | GroupElementFromWord[group, {i1, i2, ...}] = PermutationProduct of the generators GroupGenerators[group][[\|ik\|]] (inverted for negative ik), applied left to right |
| `GroupElementToWord` | added | 3 | GroupElementToWord[group, g]: a word {i1, i2, ...} in the generators (negative for inverses) whose product is g -- a shortest word (breadth-first search) for groups of at most 10^5 elements, otherwise a word from a stabilizer chain; words are not unique and may differ from the Wolfram Language's |
| `GroupSetwiseStabilizer` | added | 3 | GroupSetwiseStabilizer[group, {p1, ...}]: the subgroup mapping the set of points to itself, as a PermutationGroup (generators may differ from the Wolfram Language's choice); the Permute action form is not supported |
| `GroupStabilizer` | added | 4 | GroupStabilizer[group, {p1, ...}]: the pointwise stabilizer subgroup as a PermutationGroup (generators may differ from the Wolfram Language's choice); the Permute action form is not supported |
| `GroupStabilizerChain` | added | 5 | GroupStabilizerChain[group]: {{} -> G, {b1} -> G_b1, {b1, b2} -> ..., base -> PermutationGroup[{}]} with the base chosen greedily (smallest point moved by the current stabilizer) or given by GroupActionBase; the stabilizers are PermutationGroup objects whose generators may differ from the Wolfram Language's choice |
| `HaradaNortonGroupHN` | added | 3 | HaradaNortonGroupHN[] represents the Harada-Norton group HN, of order 273030912000000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `HeldGroupHe` | added | 3 | HeldGroupHe[] represents the Held group He, of order 4030387200; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `HigmanSimsGroupHS` | added | 3 | HigmanSimsGroupHS[] represents the Higman-Sims group HS, of order 44352000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `JankoGroupJ1` | added | 3 | JankoGroupJ1[] represents the Janko group J1, of order 175560; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `JankoGroupJ2` | added | 3 | JankoGroupJ2[] represents the Janko group J2, of order 604800; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `JankoGroupJ3` | added | 3 | JankoGroupJ3[] represents the Janko group J3, of order 50232960; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `JankoGroupJ4` | added | 3 | JankoGroupJ4[] represents the Janko group J4, of order 86775571046077562880; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `LyonsGroupLy` | added | 3 | LyonsGroupLy[] represents the Lyons group Ly, of order 51765179004000000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `MathieuGroupM11` | added | 7 | MathieuGroupM11[] represents the Mathieu group M11, of order 7920; permutation representation on 1..11 (GroupGenerators, GroupElements, GroupOrbits, GroupStabilizer, ... work) |
| `MathieuGroupM12` | added | 4 | MathieuGroupM12[] represents the Mathieu group M12, of order 95040; permutation representation on 1..12 (GroupGenerators, GroupElements, GroupOrbits, GroupStabilizer, ... work) |
| `MathieuGroupM22` | added | 4 | MathieuGroupM22[] represents the Mathieu group M22, of order 443520; permutation representation on 1..22 (GroupGenerators, GroupElements, GroupOrbits, GroupStabilizer, ... work) |
| `MathieuGroupM23` | added | 4 | MathieuGroupM23[] represents the Mathieu group M23, of order 10200960; permutation representation on 1..23 (GroupGenerators, GroupElements, GroupOrbits, GroupStabilizer, ... work) |
| `MathieuGroupM24` | added | 4 | MathieuGroupM24[] represents the Mathieu group M24, of order 244823040; permutation representation on 1..24 (GroupGenerators, GroupElements, GroupOrbits, GroupStabilizer, ... work) |
| `McLaughlinGroupMcL` | added | 3 | McLaughlinGroupMcL[] represents the McLaughlin group McL, of order 898128000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `MonsterGroupM` | added | 4 | MonsterGroupM[] represents the Monster group M, of order 808017424794512875886459904961710757005754368000000000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `ONanGroupON` | added | 3 | ONanGroupON[] represents the O'Nan group O'N, of order 460815505920; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `RightCosetRepresentative` | added | 3 | RightCosetRepresentative[group, g]: the smallest permutation (in image-list order, as Sort[..., Less]) of the right coset {PermutationProduct[h, g] : h in group} |
| `RudvalisGroupRu` | added | 3 | RudvalisGroupRu[] represents the Rudvalis group Ru, of order 145926144000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `SuzukiGroupSuz` | added | 3 | SuzukiGroupSuz[] represents the Suzuki group Suz, of order 448345497600; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `ThompsonGroupTh` | added | 3 | ThompsonGroupTh[] represents the Thompson group Th, of order 90745943887872000; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |
| `TitsGroupT` | added | 3 | TitsGroupT[] represents the Tits group 2F4(2)', of order 17971200; GroupOrder is supported; operations that need its permutation representation raise |

### import/export & serialisation (W13)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `$CacheBaseDirectory` | added | 3 | directory where LocalCache stores cached copies (~/.mikoshilang/Caches or $MIKOSHILANG_CACHE_BASE). Check: exact (environment/home lookup). |
| `$DefaultLocalBase` | added | 3 | default base directory for local object storage (~/.mikoshilang/Objects or $MIKOSHILANG_LOCAL_BASE). Check: exact (environment/home lookup). |
| `$ExportFormats` | added | 3 | list of the formats Export supports. Check: exact (registry contents). |
| `$ImportFormats` | added | 4 | list of the formats Import supports. Check: exact (registry contents). |
| `$LocalBase` | added | 3 | base directory for local objects; initially $DefaultLocalBase. Check: exact. |
| `$LocalSymbolBase` | added | 3 | directory where LocalSymbol values are stored (~/.mikoshilang/LocalSymbols or $MIKOSHILANG_LOCALSYMBOL_BASE). Check: exact (shared with LocalSymbol). |
| `BinaryDeserialize` | added | 8 | BinaryDeserialize[ba] reads WXF data (compressed or not, including packed and numeric arrays) and evaluates the result; BinaryDeserialize[ba, h] wraps it in h before evaluation; data that is not WXF raises an error. Check: reference (documented WXF bytes) and serialise/deserialise round trips in tests. |
| `BinarySerialize` | added | 13 | BinarySerialize[expr] gives a ByteArray with the WXF serialisation of expr (header 8:, documented tokens and varint lengths; System` symbols unqualified, others with their context); PerformanceGoal -> "Size" zlib-compresses (header 8C:); "Speed"/Automatic do not. Check: reference (documented WXF bytes) and the round trip BinaryDeserialize[BinarySerialize[e]] === e in tests. |
| `Boxes` | added | 3 | element name for the box (typesetting) form of an expression in Import/Export; inert symbol. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `CachePersistence` | added | 3 | option for URL fetching functions: how long downloaded content may be cached (Automatic, Infinity, a Quantity or None); inert symbol. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `Compress` | added | 8 | Compress[expr] gives Wolfram's compressed string: "1:" <> Base64 of the zlib deflation of the !boR serialisation of expr (i/I integers, r/R reals, S strings with \ooo and \:xxxx escapes, s symbols, f expressions); Uncompress inverts it. Check: exact; the round trip Uncompress[Compress[e]] === e in tests. |
| `ConversionRules` | added | 3 | option for Import/Export of some formats giving rules applied while converting; inert symbol (no MikoshiLang format uses it, so Import/Export reject it). Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `CreateArchive` | added | 6 | CreateArchive[src] compresses a file or directory (or a list of them) into src.zip; CreateArchive[src, dest] writes dest (.zip, .tar, .tar.gz/.tgz, .tar.bz2, or a directory to put src.zip in); returns the full archive path; OverwriteTarget -> False (default) refuses to replace. Check: exact (zipfile/tarfile); CreateArchive/ExtractArchive round trips in tests. |
| `CreateUUID` | added | 4 | CreateUUID[] gives a new random (version 4) UUID string xxxxxxxx-xxxx-4xxx-yxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx; CreateUUID["base"] prefixes it with base. Check: exact (uuid4); format and uniqueness in tests. |
| `DefaultElement` | added | 3 | option for importers naming the element imported by default; see FileFormatProperties[fmt, "DefaultImportElement"]; inert symbol. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `DumpSave` | added | 4 | DumpSave["file.mx", s] writes the full definitions of s (a symbol, name, context "ctx`" or list) to a binary MikoshiLang MX file and returns the list of symbols saved; Get["file.mx"] restores them (like Wolfram MX files, the dump is specific to the system that wrote it); HoldRest. Check: exact; definitions restored with Get after Clear in tests. |
| `Export` | added | 13 | Export["file", expr], Export["file", expr, fmt], Export["file", expr, {fmt, elem}] writes expr in the format given or implied by the extension (file.csv.gz: GZIP of CSV) and returns the file name; an unknown extension raises Export::infer. Check: reference (documented text per format) and Import round trips in tests. |
| `ExportByteArray` | added | 7 | ExportByteArray[expr, fmt] gives a ByteArray with the bytes of expr exported in the format, e.g. ExportByteArray[-1, "Integer8"] == ByteArray[{255}], ExportByteArray[4., "Real64"]. Check: reference (explicit byte layouts) and ImportByteArray round trips in tests. |
| `ExportString` | added | 14 | ExportString[expr, fmt] gives the text of expr in the format (binary formats as a string of characters with codes 0..255, as Wolfram does); CSV/TSV quote fields only when needed, JSON is tab-indented unless "Compact" -> True. Check: reference (documented ExportString text) and ImportString round trips in tests. |
| `ExtractArchive` | added | 5 | ExtractArchive["file.zip"] extracts a zip/tar/tar.gz/tar.bz2/gz archive into the current directory; ExtractArchive[src, dir] into dir; ExtractArchive[src, dir, patt] only members matching a string or "*" wildcard pattern; returns the list of extracted files (full paths); members with absolute or .. paths are refused; OverwriteTarget -> False (default) refuses to replace existing files. Check: exact (zipfile/tarfile); round trips with CreateArchive in tests. |
| `FileConvert` | added | 4 | FileConvert["src" -> "fmt"] converts a file to the format, writing src with the format's extension; FileConvert["src" -> "dest"] infers the format from dest; returns the new file name. Check: self-check by construction (Import then Export); tests re-import the converted file. |
| `FileFormat` | added | 4 | FileFormat["file"] gives the Import format of a file: from its extension when known, otherwise from its content (GZIP, BZIP2, ZIP, TAR, WXF, MX, PEM, XML, JSON, Text); a missing file without a known extension raises FileFormat::nffil. Check: exact (extension, then content sniffing); reference files in tests. |
| `FileFormatProperties` | added | 7 | FileFormatProperties[fmt] gives <\|"FileNamePatterns", "MIMETypes", "ImportElements", "DefaultImportElement"\|>; FileFormatProperties[fmt, prop \| {props}]; lists of formats and All give associations keyed by format; FileFormatProperties[] gives every format. Check: exact (registry lookup); reference values in tests. |
| `FileFormatQ` | added | 4 | FileFormatQ[file, fmt] is True when the file has format fmt (its FileFormat, or content that parses as fmt for JSON/XML/WXF/archives), False otherwise; an unknown format name raises. Check: exact; reference files in tests. |
| `FileNameToFormatList` | added | 4 | FileNameToFormatList["name.ext"] gives the formats whose file name patterns match (e.g. {"CSV"} for *.csv); {} when none does. Check: exact (registry lookup); reference values in tests. |
| `Import` | added | 51 | Import[src], Import[src, fmt], Import[src, elem], Import[src, {fmt, elem, sub...}] for files, File[], URL[], RemoteFile[] and http(s)/file URLs; format from the extension or the content; elements "Elements", "Rules" and each format's elements with row/column subelements; formats as in $ImportFormats (Package files are evaluated, as Get does). Check: reference (documented results per format) and Export/Import round trips in tests. |
| `ImportByteArray` | added | 5 | ImportByteArray[ba, fmt or {fmt, elem, ...}] imports the bytes of a ByteArray in the given format (format sniffed from the content when omitted). Check: reference and ExportByteArray round trips in tests. |
| `ImportOptions` | added | 3 | element/option name used in format registrations to describe import options; inert symbol. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `ImportString` | added | 19 | ImportString["data", fmt or {fmt, elem, ...}] imports from a string (binary formats read the characters as bytes 0..255); without a format the content is sniffed (WXF, JSON, XML, compressed data, else CSV/TSV when every line has commas/tabs, else Text). Check: reference (documented ImportString results) and ExportString round trips in tests. |
| `ImportedObject` | added | 3 | ImportedObject[assoc] holds imported data with no Wolfram Language representation (e.g. unsupported PEM entries); obj["prop"] gives its properties, including "OriginalFormat" and "RawContent". Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `IncludeMetaInformation` | added | 3 | option for Import/Export of some formats controlling whether meta information is included; inert symbol (the supported formats carry none, so Import/Export reject it). Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `LocalCache` | added | 5 | LocalCache[URL[url]] downloads url (file:// or http(s)) into $CacheBaseDirectory and returns LocalObject["file:///..."], which Import and Get read; LocalCache[obj, LocalObject["name" or "file:///path"]] chooses the cache; re-evaluation refreshes the copy; CloudObject caches are not available (no Wolfram Cloud). Check: exact; the cached copy is re-imported in tests. |
| `MIMETypeToFormatList` | added | 4 | MIMETypeToFormatList["type/subtype"] gives the formats with that MIME type (case-insensitive, parameters such as ;charset= ignored); {} when none. Check: exact (registry lookup); reference values in tests. |
| `MetaInformation` | added | 4 | option for Export of formats that carry metadata; Export raises a clear error for every supported format since none can store it. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `PersistenceLocation` | added | 4 | PersistenceLocation["type"] for the documented types (KernelSession, FrontEndSession, Notebook, Local, LocalShared, Cloud, Installation); "Local" gets its default base LocalObject[file URL of $DefaultLocalBase/Persistence]; PersistenceLocation["type" -> base] is PersistenceLocation["type", base]; unknown types raise. Check: exact (inert wrapper with validation). |
| `PersistenceTime` | added | 3 | option for persistent values: how long a value is kept (Infinity, a time in seconds or a DateObject); inert symbol. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `Put` | added | 5 | Put[e1, e2, ..., "file"] (expr >> file) writes each expression in InputForm on its own line, replacing the file (arbitrary-precision numbers keep their precision); Put["file"] empties it; Get reads the file back. Check: exact; Put/Get round trips in tests. |
| `PutAppend` | added | 3 | PutAppend[e1, ..., "file"] (expr >>> file) appends the expressions in InputForm, one per line. Check: exact; PutAppend/Get round trips in tests. |
| `RemoteFile` | added | 4 | RemoteFile[URL["url"]] (or RemoteFile["url"], normalised to URL form) is an inert reference to a remote file that Import reads. Check: exact (inert wrapper); Import through it in tests. |
| `Save` | added | 4 | Save["file", s] appends the full definitions of s and the symbols it depends on (FullDefinition: values, attributes, options, messages) in InputForm; s may be a name, a "pattern*", a context "ctx`" or a list; Get restores them; HoldRest. Check: exact; definitions restored with Get after Clear in tests. |
| `SetFileFormatProperties` | added | 4 | SetFileFormatProperties[fmt, prop -> val] or {rules} (or an association) changes "FileNamePatterns", "MIMETypes" or "DefaultImportElement" (which must be an import element) for the session; Import then uses the new default element. Check: exact; the change is read back with FileFormatProperties in tests. |
| `ToMemory` | added | 4 | ToMemory[obj] gives the in-memory version of an out-of-core object; MikoshiLang keeps every object in memory, so it returns obj unchanged. Check: exact (identity). |
| `URL` | added | 9 | URL["url"] is an inert symbolic URL accepted by Import, FileFormat and LocalCache (file:// URLs read local files); URL[URL[u]] is URL[u]; a non-string argument raises. Check: exact (inert wrapper with validation). |
| `Uncompress` | added | 7 | Uncompress["1:..."] recovers and evaluates the expression from a Compress string (also reads packed real arrays 'e'); Uncompress[c, h] wraps it in h before evaluation; invalid data raises. Check: exact; Compress round trips in tests. |
| `VerifySecurityCertificates` | added | 3 | option for URL functions: whether to verify the server's TLS certificate (True by default); inert symbol. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `XMLElement` | added | 5 | XMLElement[tag, {attr -> val, ...}, {data, ...}] represents a symbolic XML element; produced by Import of XML and accepted by Export. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |
| `XMLObject` | added | 3 | XMLObject["type"][...] represents a symbolic XML object (Document, Declaration, Comment, ProcessingInstruction); produced by Import of XML and accepted by Export. Check: exact (inert symbol; tests check it stays unevaluated and is used as documented). |

### interop (I2)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ExternalEvaluate` | error | 3 | ExternalEvaluate is reserved and always raises NotImplementedError: MikoshiLang never evaluates code strings in-process. Python is reachable only through PythonFunction / PythonImport (gated by Session(allow_python=...)) and @session.function. Check: reference (Raises cases in tests). |
| `ExternalFunction` | error | 1 | ExternalFunction is reserved and always raises NotImplementedError: MikoshiLang never evaluates code strings in-process. Python is reachable only through PythonFunction / PythonImport (gated by Session(allow_python=...)) and @session.function. Check: reference (Raises cases in tests). |
| `FromPackedArray` | added | 5 | FromPackedArray[list] returns the same list with its elements stored one by one (an unpacked List); any other expression is returned unchanged. Also available as Developer`FromPackedArray. Check: exact; equality with the packed list in tests. |
| `PackedArrayQ` | added | 11 | PackedArrayQ[expr] is True for a packed List; PackedArrayQ[expr, type] also tests the element type (Integer, Real or Complex) and PackedArrayQ[expr, type, rank] the array rank. Unknown types raise ValueError, a non-integer rank TypeError. Also available as Developer`PackedArrayQ. Check: exact; tested on packed and unpacked lists. |
| `StartExternalSession` | error | 1 | StartExternalSession is reserved and always raises NotImplementedError: MikoshiLang never evaluates code strings in-process. Python is reachable only through PythonFunction / PythonImport (gated by Session(allow_python=...)) and @session.function. Check: reference (Raises cases in tests). |
| `ToPackedArray` | added | 25 | ToPackedArray[list] stores a rectangular list of machine integers or reals as one NumPy array (a packed List: same elements, same Head, equal to the unpacked list); a list that cannot be packed (symbolic, exact rational, ragged, bignum, empty) is returned unchanged, as in Wolfram. ToPackedArray[list, type] with type Integer or Real converts machine numbers to that type; an unknown type raises ValueError, a non-symbol type TypeError, Integer for non-integers ValueError, Complex NotImplementedError (complex arrays are packed only when they come from NumPy). Also available as Developer`ToPackedArray. Check: exact (lossless for machine numbers); round trip through FromPackedArray in tests. |

### linear algebra

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CharacteristicPolynomial` | fixed | 4 | legacy returned Det[x I - m]; Wolfram CharacteristicPolynomial[m, x] = Det[m - x I] (differs by (-1)^n) |
| `CholeskyDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned the lower factor; Wolfram returns the upper triangular u with ConjugateTranspose[u].u = m; non-Hermitian or non-positive-definite matrices raise |
| `ColumnSpace` | verified | 3 | basis of the column space (pivot columns; MikoshiLang) |
| `ConditionNumber` | fixed | 3 | 2-norm condition number sigma_max/sigma_min (MikoshiLang); exact for exact matrices, Infinity for singular matrices (legacy returned a huge float or an Error expression without NumPy) |
| `Cross` | fixed | 3 | 3D cross product, exact; Cross[{x, y}] = {-y, x}; non-3D vectors raise |
| `Cross3D` | fixed | 3 | alias of Cross; legacy converted to floats and returned an Error expression for bad input |
| `CrossProduct` | fixed | 3 | alias of Cross (exact) |
| `DiagonalMatrix` | fixed | 4 | DiagonalMatrix[list] and DiagonalMatrix[list, k] (k-th diagonal) |
| `DiagonalMatrixQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `Eigenvalues` | fixed | 13 | eigenvalues with multiplicity in Wolfram order (decreasing absolute value; legacy returned SymPy's dict order). Eigenvalues[m, k] gives the k largest in absolute value (k < 0: the \|k\| smallest, i.e. Take[Eigenvalues[m], k]); Eigenvalues[m, UpTo[k]]; generalized {m, a} raises |
| `Eigenvectors` | fixed | 6 | ordered like Eigenvalues, exact vectors scaled so the last non-zero entry is 1, zero vectors pad defective matrices (Wolfram); machine matrices give unit vectors; Eigenvectors[m, k] and UpTo[k] as for Eigenvalues |
| `FrobeniusNorm` | fixed | 3 | exact Frobenius norm (legacy returned floats) |
| `HermitianQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `HessenbergDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram {p, h} with m = p.h.ConjugateTranspose[p] (legacy returned {H -> h, Q -> q} rules); numeric |
| `IdentityMatrix` | fixed | 15 | IdentityMatrix[n] and IdentityMatrix[{m, n}]; non-integer sizes raise (legacy truncated) |
| `JordanForm` | verified | 3 | {s, j} with m = s.j.Inverse[s] (Wolfram JordanDecomposition convention) |
| `KroneckerProduct` | fixed | 4 | variadic Kronecker product of matrices and/or vectors (vectors give vectors, as Wolfram) |
| `LUDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | legacy ignored the row permutation (L.U != m whenever pivoting occurs); now Wolfram's {lu, p, c}: lu holds L (unit lower, below the diagonal) and U, m[[p]] == L.U, c = 0 for exact matrices and the infinity-norm condition number for machine matrices |
| `LinearSolve` | fixed | 11 | solves m.x == b for a vector or matrix b; underdetermined systems give one particular solution (free parameters 0, as Wolfram); inconsistent systems raise |
| `LowerTriangularMatrixQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `MatrixCos` | fixed | 3 | exact (legacy summed a truncated Taylor series) |
| `MatrixExp` | fixed | 5 | exact via the Jordan form (SymPy); machine matrices via SciPy expm |
| `MatrixLog` | fixed | 3 | principal logarithm; legacy discarded the imaginary part (MatrixLog[{{-1, 0}, {0, 1}}] was wrong) |
| `MatrixNullity` | verified | 3 | columns - rank (MikoshiLang) |
| `MatrixPower` | fixed | 3 | integer powers (negative powers of singular matrices raise); symbolic n via diagonalisation |
| `MatrixRank` | verified | 4 | exact rank; machine matrices use an SVD tolerance; W05: option ZeroTest -> f (f decides which pivot candidates are zero) |
| `MatrixSin` | fixed | 3 | exact (legacy summed a truncated Taylor series) |
| `MatrixSqrt` | fixed | 3 | principal square root; legacy discarded the imaginary part |
| `MatrixTan` | fixed | 3 | exact (legacy summed a truncated Taylor series) |
| `Norm` | fixed | 13 | vectors: Norm[v] and Norm[v, p] (p >= 1 or Infinity); matrices: Norm[m] is the spectral 2-norm as in Wolfram (legacy flattened the matrix, giving the Frobenius norm), Norm[m, 1], Norm[m, Infinity], Norm[m, "Frobenius"] |
| `Normalize` | fixed | 3 | v / Norm[v]; the zero vector is returned unchanged as in Wolfram (legacy divided by zero); Normalize[z] for a number gives z/Abs[z] |
| `NullSpace` | fixed | 4 | basis vectors in Wolfram order (legacy reversed it), exact; empty list for full column rank; W05: option ZeroTest -> f (f decides which pivot candidates are zero) |
| `PseudoInverse` | verified | 3 | Moore-Penrose pseudo-inverse (exact; NumPy for machine matrices) |
| `QRDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | legacy returned {Q, R} with m = Q.R; Wolfram returns {q, r} with m = ConjugateTranspose[q].r (q has orthonormal rows) |
| `Rank` | verified | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of MatrixRank |
| `RowReduce` | verified | 6 | reduced row echelon form; W05: option ZeroTest -> f (f decides which pivot candidates are zero) |
| `RowSpace` | verified | 3 | basis of the row space (non-zero rows of the RREF; MikoshiLang) |
| `SVD` | fixed | 3 | MikoshiLang alias of SingularValueDecomposition; Wolfram {u, s, v} with m = u.s.ConjugateTranspose[v] and singular values in decreasing order (legacy did not order them); machine matrices give the full SVD |
| `ScalarTripleProduct` | fixed | 3 | a.(b x c), exact (legacy converted to floats) |
| `SchurDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram {q, t} with m = q.t.ConjugateTranspose[q] (legacy returned {T -> t, Z -> z} rules); numeric, real Schur form for real matrices |
| `SingularValueDecomposition` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram {u, s, v} with m = u.s.ConjugateTranspose[v] and singular values in decreasing order (legacy did not order them); machine matrices give the full SVD |
| `SpectralNorm` | fixed | 3 | largest singular value, exact for exact matrices (legacy always returned a float via NumPy) |
| `SymmetricMatrixQ` | fixed | 4 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `Tr` | promoted | 5 | trace of a matrix (sum of the diagonal); Tr[list] sums a vector |
| `Trace` | fixed | 36 | Wolfram evaluation trace: Trace[expr] evaluates expr and returns the chain of forms as HoldForm[...] with subevaluations as nested lists (Trace[1 + 2 3] -> {{2 3, 6}, 1 + 6, 7}); Trace[expr, form] keeps only forms matching the pattern. Internal evaluations made by library functions can add extra steps that Wolfram would not show. For the matrix trace use Tr (legacy Trace[m] meant Tr[m]); options TraceDepth, TraceForward, TraceBackward, TraceAbove, TraceOn, TraceOff (W01) |
| `TransposeList` | fixed | 3 | transpose of a rectangular list of lists (MikoshiLang); ragged input now raises (legacy zip-truncated) |
| `UpperTriangularMatrixQ` | fixed | 3 | False for non-matrices (legacy raised); exact test on the entries |
| `VectorAngle` | fixed | 4 | ArcCos[u.Conjugate[v]/(\|u\| \|v\|)]; zero vectors and different lengths raise |
| `ZeroMatrix` | fixed | 3 | m x n zero matrix (MikoshiLang); ZeroMatrix[n] is n x n; non-integer sizes raise |

### linear algebra & optimisation (W08)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ArrayDot` | added | 6 | ArrayDot[a, b, k] contracts the last k indices of a with the first k of b; ArrayDot[a, b, {{s1, t1}, ...}] contracts index si of a with index ti of b (remaining indices of a, then of b); exact, symbolic and numeric arrays |
| `Band` | added | 17 | Band[start], Band[start, end], Band[start, end \| Automatic, step] in SparseArray rules: Band[...] -> v repeats v along the diagonal to the edge (or end); -> {v1, ...} fills successive positions (cycling when an end is given); -> array inserts a block; -> {a1, ...} places blocks along the diagonal (cyclically up to an explicit end, clipped at the edge); negative positions count from the end; Band itself is inert |
| `BayesianMaximization` | added | 8 | BayesianMaximization[f, spec] gives a BayesianMaximizationObject: Gaussian-process Bayesian optimisation (RBF kernel with the length scale chosen by marginal likelihood, expected-improvement or improvement-probability acquisition over random candidates) over an Interval, Rectangle/Cuboid, a list of numeric configurations or a sampler function; options MaxIterations (default 100), AssumeDeterministic, InitialEvaluationHistory, Method, RandomSeeding (default 1234, deterministic); results are stochastic like Wolfram's and the GP model differs, so configurations differ in detail; the "PredictorFunction" property raises |
| `BayesianMaximizationObject` | added | 3 | result of BayesianMaximization; obj["MaximumConfiguration"], ["MaximumValue"], ["EvaluationHistory"], ["ObjectiveFunction"], ["Method"], ["NextConfiguration"], ["Properties"] and lists of properties |
| `BayesianMinimization` | added | 11 | BayesianMinimization[f, spec] gives a BayesianMinimizationObject: Gaussian-process Bayesian optimisation (RBF kernel with the length scale chosen by marginal likelihood, expected-improvement or improvement-probability acquisition over random candidates) over an Interval, Rectangle/Cuboid, a list of numeric configurations or a sampler function; options MaxIterations (default 100), AssumeDeterministic, InitialEvaluationHistory, Method, RandomSeeding (default 1234, deterministic); results are stochastic like Wolfram's and the GP model differs, so configurations differ in detail; the "PredictorFunction" property raises |
| `BayesianMinimizationObject` | added | 3 | result of BayesianMinimization; obj["MinimumConfiguration"], ["MinimumValue"], ["EvaluationHistory"], ["ObjectiveFunction"], ["Method"], ["NextConfiguration"], ["Properties"] and lists of properties |
| `BinaryDistance` | added | 6 | BinaryDistance[u, v] = Boole[!(u == v)]: 0 for equal expressions (1 == 1. counts as equal), 1 for different ones; Boole[u != v] when equality cannot be decided |
| `BlockDiagonalMatrix` | added | 7 | BlockDiagonalMatrix[{d1, d2, ...}] the block diagonal matrix with square blocks di ({} blocks are 0 x 0); BlockDiagonalMatrix[mat] accepts a (block diagonal) square matrix; MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `BlockLowerTriangularMatrix` | added | 3 | BlockLowerTriangularMatrix[lmat] for a square matrix (any square matrix is block lower triangular with a single block, as in Wolfram); MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `BlockUpperTriangularMatrix` | added | 3 | BlockUpperTriangularMatrix[umat] for a square matrix (any square matrix is block upper triangular with a single block, as in Wolfram); MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `BrayCurtisDistance` | added | 3 | BrayCurtisDistance[u, v] = Total[Abs[u - v]]/Total[Abs[u + v]] |
| `BunchKaufmanDecomposition` | added | 6 | {l, b, p} with p.m.Transpose[p] == l.b.ConjugateTranspose[l] (Hermitian m) or l.b.Transpose[l] (complex symmetric m): Bunch-Kaufman diagonal pivoting (alpha = (1 + Sqrt[17])/8), l unit lower triangular, b block diagonal with 1x1 and 2x2 blocks, p a permutation matrix; exact or machine numeric matrices (symbolic matrices raise); pivot choices can differ from Wolfram's on ties; TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `CanberraDistance` | added | 3 | CanberraDistance[u, v] = Total[Abs[u - v]/(Abs[u] + Abs[v])] (terms with u_i = v_i = 0 are 0) |
| `ChessboardDistance` | added | 4 | ChessboardDistance[u, v] = Max[Abs[u - v]] (Chebyshev distance) |
| `CompanionMatrix` | added | 12 | CompanionMatrix[{c0, ..., c(n-1)}] (implicit monic x^n term) and CompanionMatrix[poly, x] (normalised by the leading coefficient): ones on the subdiagonal and -c_i down the last column; CompanionMatrix[..., dir] with dir = Right (default), Bottom (transpose), Top (-c(n-1), ..., -c0 along the first row) or Left (its transpose); TargetStructure -> "Dense" \| "Sparse" accepted (dense result) |
| `ConicOptimization` | added | 3 | ConicOptimization[f, cons, vars] for a linear objective with conic constraints (NormCone, SemidefiniteCone, ExponentialCone, PowerCone, quadratic inequalities): linear problems are solved exactly (results as machine numbers), convex quadratic ones by active-set enumeration, the rest with SciPy SLSQP after a sampled convexity check (a visibly non-convex objective or constraint raises); matrix forms are not supported; constraints as in NMinimize (equations, inequalities, vector inequalities, x \[Element] Integers); properties "PrimalMinimizer", "PrimalMinimizerRules", "PrimalMinimizerVector" and "PrimalMinimumValue" (dual properties raise); infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `ConvexOptimization` | added | 4 | ConvexOptimization[f, cons, vars] for a convex objective and constraints: linear problems are solved exactly (results as machine numbers), convex quadratic ones by active-set enumeration, the rest with SciPy SLSQP after a sampled convexity check (a visibly non-convex objective or constraint raises); matrix forms are not supported; constraints as in NMinimize (equations, inequalities, vector inequalities, x \[Element] Integers); properties "PrimalMinimizer", "PrimalMinimizerRules", "PrimalMinimizerVector" and "PrimalMinimumValue" (dual properties raise); infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `CoreNilpotentDecomposition` | added | 7 | {t, c, n} with m == t.BlockDiagonalMatrix[{c, n}].Inverse[t], c invertible and n nilpotent; exact matrices use t = {pivot columns of m^k, integer null-space basis of m^k} (k the index of m), as Wolfram; invertible or nilpotent m give t = IdentityMatrix and {} for the empty part; machine matrices use an SVD basis (t is not unique); TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `CorrelationDistance` | added | 3 | CorrelationDistance[u, v] = 1 - (u - Mean[u]).Conjugate[v - Mean[v]]/(Norm[u - Mean[u]] Norm[v - Mean[v]]) |
| `CosineDistance` | added | 4 | CosineDistance[u, v] = 1 - u.Conjugate[v]/(Norm[u] Norm[v]); a zero vector raises |
| `DiceDissimilarity` | added | 5 | DiceDissimilarity[u, v] = (n10 + n01)/(2 n11 + n10 + n01) for Boolean vectors (True/False or 0/1), where nij counts positions with u = i, v = j; 0/0 gives 0 |
| `DistanceFunction` | added | 27 | option name DistanceFunction -> f (Automatic or a two-argument distance) for Nearest, DistanceMatrix, WarpingDistance, FeatureDistance, ...; an inert symbol |
| `DistanceMatrix` | added | 6 | DistanceMatrix[{u1, ...}] and DistanceMatrix[{u1, ...}, {v1, ...}] matrix of distances (Euclidean for numeric data, EditDistance for strings, JaccardDissimilarity for Boolean vectors) or DistanceFunction -> f; associations use their values; PerformanceGoal is accepted (results are always computed exactly or at input precision) |
| `DrazinInverse` | added | 7 | the Drazin inverse t.BlockDiagonalMatrix[{Inverse[c], 0}].Inverse[t] from the core-nilpotent decomposition (the inverse for invertible m, zero for nilpotent m); exact, symbolic (generic) or machine square matrices; TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `EigenvalueDecomposition` | added | 5 | {s, d} with m == s.d.Inverse[s]: s has the eigenvectors (Eigenvectors[m]) as columns and d the eigenvalues on the diagonal, ordered as Eigensystem; rational eigenvectors are scaled to coprime integers as Wolfram does; a non-diagonalizable matrix raises; TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `EuclideanDistance` | added | 6 | EuclideanDistance[u, v] = Sqrt[Total[Abs[u - v]^2]] for numeric or symbolic vectors (Abs[u - v] for scalars); results keep the precision of the input |
| `FeatureDistance` | added | 4 | FeatureDistance[e1, e2, extractor] distance between extractor[e1] and extractor[e2] (EuclideanDistance for numeric features, or DistanceFunction -> f); extractor is any function (e.g. a pure Function or Identity); FeatureDistance[extractor][e1, e2] operator form; FeatureExtractorFunction objects (machine-learned extractors from FeatureExtraction) are not available and raise |
| `FindArgMax` | added | 3 | the local maximiser of f (optionally {f, cons}) as a list of values for vars ({x, x0} starting points and {x, x0, xmin, xmax} accepted); linear problems are solved exactly (rational simplex, branch and bound for integer variables), convex quadratic ones by exact active-set enumeration, others with SciPy SLSQP (a local search from the starting point (default 1)); constraints may be equations, inequalities, vector inequalities (VectorGreaterEqual, ...), x \[Element] Integers and {x, ...} \[Element] Disk/Ball/Rectangle/Cuboid; infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `FindArgMin` | added | 3 | the local minimiser of f (optionally {f, cons}) as a list of values for vars ({x, x0} starting points and {x, x0, xmin, xmax} accepted); linear problems are solved exactly (rational simplex, branch and bound for integer variables), convex quadratic ones by exact active-set enumeration, others with SciPy SLSQP (a local search from the starting point (default 1)); constraints may be equations, inequalities, vector inequalities (VectorGreaterEqual, ...), x \[Element] Integers and {x, ...} \[Element] Disk/Ball/Rectangle/Cuboid; infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `FindMatrixGameStrategies` | added | 4 | Nash equilibria of a matrix game as a list of strategy profiles: pure equilibria first, then mixed ones, each sorted; two-player games use exact support enumeration (all equilibria of a non-degenerate game; for degenerate games the extreme equilibria with equal-size supports), n-player games give the pure equilibria only with "Pure" (mixed n-player equilibria raise); FindMatrixGameStrategies[game, "Pure" \| "Mixed"] restricts the kind; the Method option is not supported |
| `FindMaxValue` | added | 3 | the local maximum value of f (optionally {f, cons}) as a machine number; linear problems are solved exactly (rational simplex, branch and bound for integer variables), convex quadratic ones by exact active-set enumeration, others with SciPy SLSQP (a local search from the starting point (default 1)); constraints may be equations, inequalities, vector inequalities (VectorGreaterEqual, ...), x \[Element] Integers and {x, ...} \[Element] Disk/Ball/Rectangle/Cuboid; infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `FindMinValue` | added | 3 | the local minimum value of f (optionally {f, cons}) as a machine number; linear problems are solved exactly (rational simplex, branch and bound for integer variables), convex quadratic ones by exact active-set enumeration, others with SciPy SLSQP (a local search from the starting point (default 1)); constraints may be equations, inequalities, vector inequalities (VectorGreaterEqual, ...), x \[Element] Integers and {x, ...} \[Element] Disk/Ball/Rectangle/Cuboid; infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `FindShortestTour` | added | 7 | FindShortestTour[{p1, ...}] gives {length, tour} for the shortest closed tour (starting and ending at 1); FindShortestTour[pts, j, k] the shortest path from pj to pk through all points; points are vectors (EuclideanDistance, exact lengths for exact points) or strings (EditDistance), or use DistanceFunction -> f; exact (Held-Karp dynamic programming) for up to 12 points, a 2-opt/Or-opt local search beyond that (not guaranteed optimal, like Wolfram's heuristics); the direction of an optimal tour and the choice among equally short tours can differ from Wolfram's; graphs and GeoPositions raise |
| `FindTreeGameStrategies` | added | 4 | pure-strategy solutions of a TreeGame by backward induction: at each action node every action that is optimal for the moving player under some tie-breaking below it is kept, and all combinations are listed (this reproduces Wolfram's results, including its handling of ties), each as <\|player -> <\|node -> probabilities\|>\|> with nodes as child-index paths ({} the root), players in order of appearance (chance players get <\|\|>), sorted; payoff vectors are matched to the non-chance players in order of appearance; as in Wolfram, information sets are ignored (each node solved separately) |
| `FitRegularization` | added | 3 | option name (Wolfram: {"Tikhonov", l}, {"LASSO", l}, ... regularisation for Fit/FindFit); MikoshiLang's Fit and FindFit do not accept it and raise an unknown-option error |
| `FrobeniusDecomposition` | added | 3 | {s, c} with m == s.c.Inverse[s] and c the Frobenius (rational canonical) form: block diagonal with companion matrices (ones below the diagonal, coefficients in the last column) of the invariant factors, each dividing the next; exact numeric and symbolic matrices (generic case) via cyclic Krylov decomposition; s is not unique and can differ from Wolfram's; Modulus and inexact input raise; TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `FrobeniusReduce` | added | 6 | the Frobenius (rational canonical) form of a square matrix (unique; FrobeniusDecomposition[m][[2]]); TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `GameActionLabels` | added | 3 | option of MatrixGame and MatrixGamePlot: one list of action labels per player |
| `GamePlayerLabels` | added | 3 | option of MatrixGame and MatrixGamePlot: a list of player labels |
| `GeometricOptimization` | added | 4 | GeometricOptimization[f, cons, vars] for a posynomial objective with posynomial <= monomial and monomial == monomial constraints in positive variables: solved as the convex problem in u = Log[x] (SciPy SLSQP); matrix forms are not supported; constraints as in NMinimize (equations, inequalities, vector inequalities, x \[Element] Integers); properties "PrimalMinimizer", "PrimalMinimizerRules", "PrimalMinimizerVector" and "PrimalMinimumValue" (dual properties raise); infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `HadamardMatrix` | added | 8 | HadamardMatrix[n] (n a power of 2): the normalised (orthogonal, symmetric) Walsh-Hadamard matrix with entries +-1/Sqrt[n], rows in sequency order (Method -> "Sequency", default), Sylvester order ("BitComplement") or Paley/dyadic order ("GrayCode"); WorkingPrecision -> p gives numeric entries; MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `HermitianMatrix` | added | 4 | HermitianMatrix[hmat] validates a Hermitian matrix; HermitianMatrix[{{h11, h12, ...}, {h22, ...}, ...}] or the lower rows {{h11}, {h21, h22}, ...} fill in the conjugate entries; MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `JaccardDissimilarity` | added | 4 | JaccardDissimilarity[u, v] = (n10 + n01)/(n11 + n10 + n01) for Boolean vectors (True/False or 0/1), where nij counts positions with u = i, v = j; 0/0 gives 0 |
| `JordanMatrix` | added | 6 | JordanMatrix[l, n] n x n Jordan block (l on the diagonal, 1 on the superdiagonal); JordanMatrix[l, n, Right] puts the ones below the diagonal (the transpose); l must be a scalar; MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `JordanReduce` | added | 4 | the Jordan normal form j of a square matrix (exact via SymPy, blocks ordered by eigenvalue real part then imaginary part); machine matrices must be diagonalizable and give the diagonal matrix of Eigenvalues[m] (decreasing absolute value); Wolfram's block order can differ for exact matrices; TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `KarhunenLoeveDecomposition` | added | 9 | KarhunenLoeveDecomposition[{a1, ..., an}] gives {{b1, ..., bn}, m}: the rows of m are the unit eigenvectors of the covariance matrix of the (flattened) arrays ai in order of decreasing eigenvalue (sign: largest component positive) and {b1, ...} == m.{a1, ...}; Standardized -> True first shifts each ai to zero mean; KarhunenLoeveDecomposition[{b1, ...}, m] inverts the transform (missing b's are 0); machine-precision results |
| `KnapsackSolve` | added | 8 | KnapsackSolve[{cost1, ...}, capacity] maximises the number of items, KnapsackSolve[{{payoff, cost}, ...}, capacity] and {payoff, cost, maxcount} items (or "Payoff"/"Cost"/"MaxCount" rules or associations) maximise the total payoff with total cost <= capacity, giving the item counts (an association of items gives an association); exact integer programming (branch and bound on the exact simplex), ties broken by the smaller total cost; the constraints can be maxcost, {maxcost}, {maxpayoff, maxcost}, {maxpayoff, maxcost, maxcount} or an association of "MaxTotalPayoff", "MaxTotalCost", "MaxTotalCount"; Quantity payoffs/costs raise |
| `LDLDecomposition` | added | 8 | {l, d} with m == l.d.ConjugateTranspose[l], l unit lower triangular and d diagonal, for a Hermitian (or real symmetric) matrix, exact, symbolic or machine; a zero pivot (no LDL factorisation without pivoting) raises (use BunchKaufmanDecomposition); TargetStructure is accepted, but MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the factors are always ordinary (dense) matrices |
| `LinearFractionalOptimization` | added | 3 | minimise (a.x + b)/(c.x + d) subject to linear constraints (the denominator must be positive on the feasible set): exact Charnes-Cooper transformation to a linear program, branch and bound for integer variables; matrix form [{a, b, c, d}, {a1, b1}, ...]; constraints as in NMinimize (equations, inequalities, vector inequalities, x \[Element] Integers); properties "PrimalMinimizer", "PrimalMinimizerRules", "PrimalMinimizerVector" and "PrimalMinimumValue" (dual properties raise); infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `LinearOptimization` | added | 8 | LinearOptimization[f, cons, vars] gives the minimiser rules; LinearOptimization[c, {a, b}] (a.x + b >= 0) and [c, {a, b}, {aeq, beq}] (aeq.x + beq == 0) give the vector; domains {Integers, Reals, ...}; exact rational simplex (Bland's rule) so exact input gives exact results, branch and bound for integer variables (integer values exact, the others machine numbers as Wolfram); vertex choice among multiple optima can differ from Wolfram's; constraints as in NMinimize (equations, inequalities, vector inequalities, x \[Element] Integers); properties "PrimalMinimizer", "PrimalMinimizerRules", "PrimalMinimizerVector" and "PrimalMinimumValue" (dual properties raise); infeasible or unbounded problems raise |
| `LinearSolveFunction` | added | 3 | LinearSolve[m] gives LinearSolveFunction[{r, c}, m] (the stored data is the matrix itself, not Wolfram's factorisation data); f[b] solves m.x == b for a vector or matrix b like LinearSolve[m, b] |
| `LossFunction` | added | 3 | option name (Wolfram: the loss for NetTrain/Predict/Classify); MikoshiLang's machine-learning functions do not accept it |
| `LowerTriangularMatrix` | added | 3 | LowerTriangularMatrix[m] validates a square lower triangular matrix (entries above the diagonal must be zero, else a clear error); MikoshiLang has no structured arrays, so the result is the ordinary matrix (Wolfram's Normal[...] or TargetStructure -> "Dense" form) and structured-array properties are not available |
| `ManhattanDistance` | added | 5 | ManhattanDistance[u, v] = Total[Abs[u - v]] |
| `MatchingDissimilarity` | added | 3 | MatchingDissimilarity[u, v] = (n10 + n01)/Length[u] for Boolean vectors (True/False or 0/1), where nij counts positions with u = i, v = j; 0/0 gives 0 |
| `MatrixGame` | added | 21 | MatrixGame[{{a11, ...}, ...}] (two-player zero-sum) or MatrixGame[p] with p of dimensions {m1, ..., mn, n} gives MatrixGame[payoff array, options..., "Validity" -> {True}]; options GameActionLabels and GamePlayerLabels; properties "PayoffArray", "ComponentPayoffArray", "GameActionLabels", "GamePlayerLabels", "Dataset" (Dataset of payoff vectors by action labels) and "Plot" (MatrixGamePlot); "TreeGame" and SparseArray/rule specifications raise |
| `MatrixGamePayoff` | added | 3 | MatrixGamePayoff[game, {s1, ..., sn}] gives the expected payoff of each player for mixed strategies si (probability vectors; also {1 -> s1, ...} rules or <\|label -> s\|>, and lists of profiles); MatrixGamePayoff[game, profile, "Expectation" \| "Variance"]; exact for exact strategies; joint group strategies and the distribution/simulation properties raise |
| `MatrixGamePlot` | added | 3 | MatrixGamePlot[game] gives Graphics[...]: a grid of payoff cells (rows: the first Ceiling[n/2] players' action combinations, columns: the rest), each split into one coloured strip per player with the payoff as Text, and action/player labels (GameActionLabels, GamePlayerLabels options); Graphics options are passed through; PlotLayout, ColorFunction and strategy thickness are not supported (raise) |
