| `PrimePowerQ` | fixed | 4 | True for p^k with k >= 1; False for 0, 1 and non-integers; negative integers raise (Gaussian/negative conventions not supported); legacy truncated reals |
| `PrimitiveRoot` | fixed | 6 | smallest primitive root; legacy returned None (displayed as nothing) when none exists, now unevaluated like Wolfram |
| `PrimitiveRootQ` | fixed | 3 | PrimitiveRootQ[a, n]: True when a generates the units mod n; False when gcd(a, n) != 1 (legacy raised a SymPy ValueError) |
| `QuadraticResidues` | verified | 3 | sorted residues x^2 mod n including 0 (MikoshiLang function); n must be positive |
| `Radical` | fixed | 3 | product of the distinct primes dividing n; Radical[0] raises; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |
| `RationalReconstruct` | fixed | 3 | legacy used nsimplify, so RationalReconstruct[1.4142135623730951] returned Sqrt[2] (not a rational); now the exact rational with the same decimal digits, simplest within machine precision |
| `Stirling` | fixed | 3 | Stirling[n, k] = StirlingS2[n, k]; Stirling[n, k, 1] now gives the signed numbers like StirlingS1 |
| `StirlingS1` | fixed | 4 | returned unsigned Stirling numbers (StirlingS1[3, 2] = 3); Wolfram uses signed numbers (-3) |
| `StirlingS2` | fixed | 3 | Stirling numbers of the second kind; legacy truncated non-integer arguments with int(); now exact integers only |

### number theory (W09)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `$MaxPrecision` | added | 3 | maximum precision of arbitrary-precision numbers: Infinity (no cap); read-only in MikoshiLang (assigning another value raises, because the fixed-precision model has no precision clamping) |
| `$MinPrecision` | added | 3 | minimum precision of arbitrary-precision numbers: 0 (no floor); read-only in MikoshiLang (assigning another value raises) |
| `Algebraics` | added | 6 | the domain of algebraics, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `Booleans` | added | 4 | the domain of booleans, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `Complexes` | added | 9 | the domain of complexes, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `ContinuedFractionK` | added | 4 | ContinuedFractionK[g, {i, imin, imax}] = 1/(g_imin + 1/(g_imin+1 + ...)) and ContinuedFractionK[f, g, {i, imin, imax}] = f_imin/(g_imin + f_imin+1/(g_imin+1 + ...)); finite numeric limits are evaluated exactly; infinite fractions with constant terms give the closed form 2 f/(g + Sqrt[g^2 + 4 f]) (for numeric terms the attracting fixed point, unevaluated when the fraction oscillates); other infinite or symbolic-length fractions stay unevaluated |
| `Cyclotomic` | added | 5 | Cyclotomic[n, x] is the n-th cyclotomic polynomial (product of x - zeta over the primitive n-th roots of unity); Cyclotomic[0, x] = 1; any x, including numbers and matrices of symbols, is substituted |
| `DeBruijnSequence` | added | 4 | DeBruijnSequence[list, n]: the lexicographically first de Bruijn sequence (FKM algorithm) in the order of the elements of list (duplicates removed, first occurrence kept); DeBruijnSequence[k, n] uses Range[0, k - 1]; a string gives a string. The positions-based form DeBruijnSequence[list, n, positions] is not supported |
| `DirichletCharacter` | added | 4 | DirichletCharacter[k, j, n]: the j-th Dirichlet character modulo k (j = 1 is the principal character); for a prime modulus chi_j(g^a) = Exp[2 Pi I (j - 1) a/(k - 1)] with g the least primitive root (the Wolfram ordering); moduli 1, 2, 4 and primes are supported, other moduli raise; zero when n is not coprime to k; listable in n |
| `DirichletConvolve` | added | 4 | DirichletConvolve[f, g, n, m] = Sum[f(d) g(m/d), {d \| m}]: explicit for a positive integer m; for symbolic m, sums of powers of n give DivisorSigma closed forms (n^a * n^b -> m^Min[a,b] DivisorSigma[\|a - b\|, m]), KroneckerDelta[n - 1] is the identity, and other inputs stay unevaluated |
| `DirichletL` | added | 3 | DirichletL[k, j, s]: the Dirichlet L-function of DirichletCharacter[k, j, n]; DirichletL[1, 1, s] = Zeta[s]; the principal character gives Zeta[s] Product[1 - p^-s, {p \| k}]; non-positive integers s give -B[1 - s, chi]/(1 - s) (generalized Bernoulli numbers); inexact s is evaluated with mpmath; other exact arguments stay unevaluated (N evaluates them) |
| `DirichletTransform` | added | 3 | DirichletTransform[f, n, s] = Sum[f(n)/n^s, {n, 1, Infinity}] in closed form for linear combinations of powers of n, a^n (PolyLog), Cos/Sin/Exp of multiples of n, Mod[n, k] (Hurwitz zeta), MoebiusMu, EulerPhi, LiouvilleLambda, DivisorSigma, DirichletCharacter (DirichletL), KroneckerDelta[n - 1] and DivisorSum[n, f] (Zeta[s] times the transform of f); other functions stay unevaluated |
| `Extension` | added | 4 | option for Factor, PolynomialGCD, ... : Extension -> a (or {a1, ...}) works over the algebraic extension Q(a); Extension -> Automatic uses the algebraic numbers in the input |
| `FindIntegerNullVector` | added | 3 | FindIntegerNullVector[{x1, ..., xn}] finds integers ai, not all zero, with a1 x1 + ... + an xn == 0 (PSLQ at the input precision, or at 3 n + 30 digits for exact input); FindIntegerNullVector[x, d] restricts to vectors of norm <= d and raises when none exists; for exact input the relation is verified to 60 more digits |
| `FiniteAbelianGroupCount` | added | 3 | number of abelian groups of order n: the product of PartitionsP[e] over the prime-power exponents e of n |
| `FiniteGroupCount` | added | 3 | number of non-isomorphic groups of order n: tabulated for n <= 2047 (SmallGroups library, OEIS A000001); beyond the table exact formulas for cyclic numbers (gcd(n, EulerPhi[n]) = 1 gives 1), square-free n (Hoelder), p, p^2, p^3, p^4 and p^5 (p >= 5); other orders raise |
| `GaussianIntegers` | added | 7 | option for Factor, FactorInteger, PrimeQ, Divisors, ... : GaussianIntegers -> True works over the Gaussian integers Z[I] |
| `Groupings` | added | 6 | Groupings[list, k] (or Groupings[n, k] for Range[n]): all trees of k-ary List nodes with the given leaves in order (compositions of the leaves lexicographically descending); Groupings[list, h -> k] uses head h; Groupings[{list1, list2, ...}, spec] gives the groupings of every list, grouping structure outermost; a third argument wraps each result (e.g. HoldForm). Several heads or arities, arity 1 and Orderless/Flat specifications are not supported (raise) |
| `Integers` | added | 18 | the domain of integers, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `LatticeReduce` | added | 5 | LatticeReduce[{v1, v2, ...}] gives an LLL-reduced basis (delta = 3/4) of the lattice spanned by the integer (or rational) vectors vi; linearly dependent inputs drop to the rank; approximate vectors raise |
| `MixedFractionParts` | added | 3 | {IntegerPart[x], FractionalPart[x]} (both parts carry the sign of x; complex numbers are split componentwise); symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `MixedRadix` | added | 5 | MixedRadix[{b1, ..., bn}] mixed-radix base for IntegerDigits, FromDigits, IntegerLength and IntegerReverse; the leading digit is unbounded |
| `Modulus` | added | 14 | option for Factor, PolynomialGCD, PolynomialQuotient/Remainder, Expand, Together, Det, Inverse, ... : Modulus -> p works modulo p (Det accepts any modulus, Inverse a prime or a modulus coprime to the determinant) |
| `NegativeIntegers` | added | 3 | the domain of negative integers: Element[x, NegativeIntegers] is Element[x, Integers] && x < 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NegativeRationals` | added | 3 | the domain of negative rationals: Element[x, NegativeRationals] is Element[x, Rationals] && x < 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NegativeReals` | added | 3 | the domain of negative reals: Element[x, NegativeReals] is Element[x, Reals] && x < 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NonNegativeIntegers` | added | 3 | the domain of nonnegative integers: Element[x, NonNegativeIntegers] is Element[x, Integers] && x >= 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NonNegativeRationals` | added | 3 | the domain of nonnegative rationals: Element[x, NonNegativeRationals] is Element[x, Rationals] && x >= 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NonNegativeReals` | added | 3 | the domain of nonnegative reals: Element[x, NonNegativeReals] is Element[x, Reals] && x >= 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NonPositiveIntegers` | added | 3 | the domain of nonpositive integers: Element[x, NonPositiveIntegers] is Element[x, Integers] && x <= 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NonPositiveRationals` | added | 3 | the domain of nonpositive rationals: Element[x, NonPositiveRationals] is Element[x, Rationals] && x <= 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NonPositiveReals` | added | 3 | the domain of nonpositive reals: Element[x, NonPositiveReals] is Element[x, Reals] && x <= 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `NumberCompose` | added | 4 | NumberCompose[{c1, ..., ck}, {u1, ..., un}] = Sum[ci u(n-k+i)]: the coefficients are aligned with the last k units (k <= n) |
| `NumberDecompose` | added | 4 | NumberDecompose[x, {u1, ..., un}]: coefficients ci with x == Sum[ci ui]; c1..c(n-1) are integers taken greedily with IntegerPart (so the signs follow x) and cn = remainder/un may be fractional; exact inputs give exact results |
| `NumeratorDenominator` | added | 3 | {Numerator[expr], Denominator[expr]}; lists give the pair of lists. The Modulus and Trig options are not supported (raise) |
| `Overflow` | added | 5 | Overflow[] represents a number too large to represent; Overflow[] + x = Overflow[], c Overflow[] = Sign[c] Overflow[] for real c != 0, 1/Overflow[] = Underflow[]; NumberQ-like tests treat it as a real number. MikoshiLang's arbitrary-precision numbers never overflow, so it only arises when entered |
| `PositiveIntegers` | added | 3 | the domain of positive integers: Element[x, PositiveIntegers] is Element[x, Integers] && x > 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `PositiveRationals` | added | 3 | the domain of positive rationals: Element[x, PositiveRationals] is Element[x, Rationals] && x > 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `PositiveReals` | added | 3 | the domain of positive reals: Element[x, PositiveReals] is Element[x, Reals] && x > 0; usable in Element, $Assumptions and Assuming |
| `Primes` | added | 4 | the domain of primes, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `PrimitivePolynomialQ` | added | 4 | PrimitivePolynomialQ[poly, p]: True if the univariate poly, reduced modulo the prime p and made monic, is primitive over GF(p) (irreducible with x of order p^n - 1) |
| `RandomComplex` | added | 4 | RandomComplex[] in the unit square, RandomComplex[zmax] in the rectangle 0..zmax, RandomComplex[{zmin, zmax}], with a count or dimension list; uniform real and imaginary parts from the session generator (SeedRandom reproducible); WorkingPrecision -> p gives p-digit numbers |
| `Rationals` | added | 4 | the domain of rationals, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `RealExponent` | added | 6 | RealExponent[x] = Log10[Abs[x]], RealExponent[x, b] = Log[b, Abs[x]], as machine reals; RealExponent[0] = -Infinity; RealExponent[0.] = -307.653 (minus the accuracy of a machine zero); symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `RealSign` | added | 5 | sign of a real number: -1, 0 or 1 (an exact integer, also for approximate input); RealSign[Infinity] = 1, RealSign[-Infinity] = -1; complex and symbolic arguments stay unevaluated |
| `RealValuedNumberQ` | added | 6 | True for integers, rationals, approximate reals and Overflow[]/Underflow[]; False for complex numbers (even with zero imaginary part), exact numeric expressions such as Sqrt[2] and symbols |
| `RealValuedNumericQ` | added | 4 | True when expr is numeric (NumericQ) and real-valued: exact quantities such as Sin[Sqrt[2]], E or Exp[I] - I Sin[1] (decided exactly when possible, otherwise numerically at 40 and 120 digits); False for complex numbers with a zero inexact imaginary part, Infinity and non-numeric input |
| `Reals` | added | 28 | the domain of reals, used in Element, assumptions (Refine, Simplify, Integrate, ...) and as a domain argument (Solve, Reduce, FindInstance) |
| `ShiftRegisterSequence` | added | 3 | linear-feedback shift register sequences: ShiftRegisterSequence[n] (maximal length over GF(2), feedback polynomial x^n + x^k + 1 with least k or else the lexicographically first primitive pentanomial), [{n, {t1, ...}}] (Fibonacci register: the new bit is the sum mod 2 of the bits at the tap positions), [{n, taps, f}] (feedback function f, result taken mod 2), [poly] (Galois register), with optional initial state (list, or polynomial for the Galois form) and length (an integer or All, default one full period p^n - 1, or 2^n terms with a feedback function); options Method -> "Fibonacci"\|"Galois" and Modulus -> p (Galois form) |
| `Underflow` | added | 4 | Underflow[] represents a number too small to represent; x + Underflow[] = x for numbers x != 0 (as a machine real), c Underflow[] = Sign[c] Underflow[], 1/Underflow[] = Overflow[] |

### number theory (batch 2)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DivisorSum` | added | 5 | DivisorSum[n, form] = Sum of form[d] over the positive divisors d of n; DivisorSum[n, form, cond] keeps the divisors for which cond[d] is True |
| `LiouvilleLambda` | added | 5 | Liouville function (-1)^Omega(n), Omega counting prime factors with multiplicity; LiouvilleLambda[1] = 1 |
| `MangoldtLambda` | added | 5 | von Mangoldt function: Log[p] if n is a power of the prime p, else 0; MangoldtLambda[1] = 0 |
| `RandomPrime` | added | 6 | RandomPrime[n] is a uniformly random prime in [2, n]; RandomPrime[{a, b}]; RandomPrime[range, k] a list of k primes (independent draws); raises when the range contains no prime |

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Bisection` | fixed | 5 | Bisection[f, x, a, b, tol=1e-6] root of f in [a, b]; raises unless f(a), f(b) bracket a root (legacy returned an Error[...] expression) |
| `EulerMethod` | verified | 4 | EulerMethod[f, {x, y}, x0, y0, xEnd, n=100] for y' = f(x, y): y(xEnd) with n fixed steps |

| `$MachinePrecision` | added | 3 | 53 Log10[2] = 15.9546 |
| `$MaxMachineNumber` | added | 3 | 1.79769*10^308 |
| `$MinMachineNumber` | added | 3 | 2.22507*10^-308 |
| `AbsArg` | added | 3 | {Abs[z], Arg[z]} |
| `Accuracy` | added | 6 | Accuracy[x] = Precision[x] - Log10[Abs[x]] (digits after the decimal point); Infinity for exact numbers; machine zero has Accuracy -Log10[$MinMachineNumber] = 307.653; the minimum over an expression |
| `Arg` | added | 6 | argument in (-Pi, Pi]; Arg[0] = 0 (Wolfram); Arg[-1] = Pi; exact for exact input |
| `Chop` | added | 13 | Chop[expr, delta] replaces approximate real numbers (and real or imaginary parts) with absolute value below delta (default 10^-10) by exact 0; exact numbers are untouched |
| `Clip` | added | 9 | Clip[x] clips to [-1, 1]; Clip[x, {min, max}]; Clip[x, {min, max}, {vmin, vmax}] gives vmin below and vmax above; threads over lists; symbolic x stays unevaluated |
| `Denominator` | added | 6 | denominator of a rational number or of the factors with negative exponents (Denominator[x/y^2] = y^2); 1 for sums and approximate numbers |
| `ExactNumberQ` | added | 5 | True for integers, rationals and exact complex numbers; False for approximate numbers and for numeric expressions such as Pi or Sqrt[2] (which are not numbers) |
| `FindFit` | added | 3 | FindFit[data, expr, pars, vars] nonlinear least squares -> {a -> a*, ...}; pars may be {{a, a0}, ...} with starting values (default 1); constraints FindFit[data, {expr, cons}, ...] are not supported |
| `FindMaximum` | added | 3 | FindMaximum[f, {x, x0}] / {{x, x0}, {y, y0}} -> {fmax, {x -> xmax, ...}}: FindMinimum of -f (local, BFGS); non-convergence raises |
| `FindRoot` | added | 6 | FindRoot[f, {x, x0}] / [lhs == rhs, {x, x0}] / {x, x0, x1} (secant) / {x, x0, xmin, xmax} (bounded) and systems FindRoot[{eqs}, {{x, x0}, {y, y0}}] -> {x -> root, ...}; complex starting values search in the complex plane; WorkingPrecision -> p; raises when no root is found |
| `Fit` | added | 5 | Fit[data, {f1, f2, ...}, vars] linear least-squares fit -> c1 f1 + c2 f2 + ... (data {y1, y2, ...} at x = 1, 2, ... or {{x, y}, ...} or {{x1, x2, y}, ...}); machine-precision coefficients |
| `FractionalPart` | added | 9 | FractionalPart[x] = x - IntegerPart[x] (same sign as x: FractionalPart[-2.4] = -0.4), separately for real and imaginary parts; exact for exact input |
| `Im` | added | 7 | imaginary part (Wolfram name; RealPart/ImaginaryPart are the legacy MikoshiLang names); exact for exact input, 0 (exact) for the imaginary part of a real |
| `InexactNumberQ` | added | 3 | True for approximate (machine or arbitrary-precision) real and complex numbers |
| `IntegerPart` | added | 8 | integer part (truncation toward zero) as an exact integer, separately for real and imaginary parts; IntegerPart[Infinity] = Infinity |
| `InterpolatingFunction` | added | 4 | InterpolatingFunction[{{xmin, xmax}}, id] objects from Interpolation / ListInterpolation / NDSolve: ifun[x], ifun'[x], Derivative[n][ifun][x], lists of points; real arguments; outside the domain the end polynomials extrapolate |
| `Interpolation` | added | 15 | Interpolation[{f1, f2, ...}] (x = 1, 2, ...) and Interpolation[{{x1, f1}, ...}] -> InterpolatingFunction: piecewise polynomials of InterpolationOrder (default 3) through the nearest order+1 points (Wolfram's default method; exact through the data); Interpolation[data, x] evaluates at x; one-dimensional data only (derivative data and grids raise) |
| `ListInterpolation` | added | 5 | ListInterpolation[{f1, ..., fn}] on x = 1..n and ListInterpolation[list, {{xmin, xmax}}] on an equally spaced grid -> InterpolatingFunction (same piecewise polynomials as Interpolation); 1-D only |
| `MachineNumberQ` | added | 7 | True for machine-precision real and complex numbers only |
| `MachinePrecision` | added | 7 | symbol for machine precision; N[MachinePrecision] = 15.9546 (53 Log10[2]) |
| `NDSolve` | added | 7 | NDSolve[{odes, ics}, y, {x, xmin, xmax}] (also y[x], and systems {y, z}) for initial-value problems of any order that can be solved for the highest derivatives -> {{y -> InterpolatingFunction[...]}} (or {{y[x] -> InterpolatingFunction[...][x]}}); SciPy DOP853 (Radau fallback for stiff problems), rtol 1e-10; the initial point may be inside the range; machine precision only; boundary-value problems, DAEs, PDEs and delay equations raise |
| `NDSolveValue` | added | 6 | NDSolveValue[{odes, ics}, y, {x, xmin, xmax}] -> InterpolatingFunction (y[x] -> ifun[x], {y, z} -> list); same solver and limits as NDSolve |
| `NIntegrate` | added | 13 | numerical integration NIntegrate[f, {x, a, b}, {y, c, d}, ...] (later limits may depend on earlier variables, Wolfram order), infinite ranges, intermediate points {x, a, x1, ..., b} for interior singularities, complex-valued integrands and complex contours; WorkingPrecision -> p for arbitrary precision (mpmath tanh-sinh / Gauss-Legendre: Method -> "DoubleExponential" \| "GaussKronrod") |
| `NMaximize` | added | 3 | NMaximize[f, vars] / [{f, cons}, vars] -> {fmax, {x -> x*, ...}} (NMinimize of -f) |
| `NMinimize` | added | 5 | NMinimize[f, vars] / NMinimize[{f, cons}, vars] -> {fmin, {x -> x*, ...}}: global numeric search (differential evolution when every variable is bounded, plus 40 seeded multi-start SLSQP/BFGS local searches; constraints: <, <=, >, >=, ==, Inequality, And); like Wolfram's it is a heuristic global method; integer domains raise |
| `NProduct` | added | 4 | NProduct[f, {n, nmin, nmax}] (also infinite products, mpmath nprod); WorkingPrecision -> p |
| `NRoots` | added | 4 | NRoots[poly == 0, x] gives x == r1 \|\| x == r2 \|\| ... with roots repeated by multiplicity (machine precision, or the precision of approximate coefficients); non-polynomials raise |
| `NSolve` | added | 9 | NSolve[eqns, vars] numerical solutions of polynomial (and rational) equations and systems as {{x -> x1}, ...} with every complex root repeated by multiplicity; NSolve[eqns, vars, Reals] keeps real solutions; WorkingPrecision -> p; transcendental equations raise (use FindRoot) |
| `NSum` | added | 7 | NSum[f, {n, nmin, nmax}] and {n, nmin, nmax, dn}, multiple iterators, nmax = Infinity (mpmath nsum: Richardson/Shanks/Euler-Maclaurin acceleration, alternating series); WorkingPrecision -> p; finite short ranges are summed directly; divergent sums raise |
| `Numerator` | added | 5 | numerator of a rational number or of a product with negative exponents (Numerator[x^2 y^-3] = x^2); sums are not combined over a common denominator (Numerator[a/b + c/d] is the whole sum) |
| `Precision` | added | 18 | Precision[x]: Infinity for exact numbers and symbolic expressions without approximate numbers, MachinePrecision for machine numbers, the decimal precision of a bignum; the minimum over an expression (a machine number makes the whole expression MachinePrecision). An arbitrary-precision zero has Precision 0. |
| `Rationalize` | added | 16 | Rationalize[x] gives the first continued-fraction convergent p/q with \|p/q - x\| < 10^-4/q^2, or x unchanged when there is none (Rationalize[N[Pi]] = 3.14159); Rationalize[x, dx] gives the first convergent within dx, refined to the smallest semiconvergent within dx (Rationalize[Pi, 1/1000] = 201/64); Rationalize[x, 0] gives the simplest rational within the precision of x; applies to every approximate number of an expression |
| `Re` | added | 5 | real part (Wolfram name; RealPart/ImaginaryPart are the legacy MikoshiLang names); exact for exact input, 0 (exact) for the imaginary part of a real |
| `ReIm` | added | 3 | {Re[z], Im[z]} |
| `RealAbs` | added | 5 | absolute value for real numbers; stays unevaluated for symbolic and non-real arguments |
| `RealDigits` | added | 23 | RealDigits[x, b, len, n]: {digits, exponent}. Machine numbers give their full precision (16 decimal digits, 53 binary), digits beyond the precision of an approximate number are Indeterminate; exact rationals give the exact digits with the recurring block as a nested list (RealDigits[19/7] = {{2, {7, 1, 4, 2, 8, 5}}, 1}); exact irrationals need len. Digits are of Abs[x]; an approximate number's digits are those of its exact binary value, rounded at its precision |
| `Rescale` | added | 6 | Rescale[x, {min, max}] = (x - min)/(max - min); Rescale[x, {min, max}, {ymin, ymax}]; Rescale[list] uses Min[list] and Max[list]; works symbolically |
| `SetAccuracy` | added | 3 | SetAccuracy[expr, a]: each number gets precision a + Log10[Abs[x]] (an arbitrary-precision zero keeps accuracy a) |
| `SetPrecision` | added | 10 | SetPrecision[expr, p] sets every number in expr (and the numeric constants Pi, E, ...) to precision p: machine numbers keep their exact binary value padded with binary zeros (SetPrecision[0.1, 20] = 0.10000000000000000555), p = Infinity converts approximate numbers to the exact rationals they represent, p = MachinePrecision gives machine numbers; the result is re-evaluated |
| `Unitize` | added | 4 | Unitize[x] is 0 for x == 0 and 1 otherwise (exact integers, also for 0.); Unitize[x, dx] is 0 when Abs[x] < dx; threads over lists |

### output forms & formatting (W04)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `$CharacterEncoding` | added | 3 | system variable: default raw character encoding, "UTF-8" |
| `$NumberMarks` | added | 3 | system variable: default NumberMarks setting, Automatic |
| `$OutputSizeLimit` | added | 3 | system variable: maximum output size in bytes before eliding, 2^20 |
| `$SystemCharacterEncoding` | added | 3 | system variable: the operating system's encoding, "UTF-8" |
| `Above` | added | 5 | inert symbol: position symbol: Labeled/Placed position, same as Top |
| `AccountingForm` | added | 5 | AccountingForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts]: no exponents, negative numbers in parentheses |
| `After` | added | 4 | inert symbol: position symbol: Labeled/Placed position, same as Right |
| `Alignment` | added | 13 | inert symbol: option of Column, Grid, Item, Multicolumn: Left, Center, Right or {h, v} (honoured in text) |
| `AlignmentPoint` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Inset and related: alignment point of an object (inert) |
| `AssociationFormat` | added | 4 | option of TextString: Automatic ({"<\|", ", ", "\|>", " -> "}), a separator string or {"left", "sep", "right"[, "arrow"]} for associations; other values raise ValueError. Check: exact; reference strings in tests. |
| `Background` | added | 9 | inert symbol: option of Style, Grid, Framed, Item, ...: background colour; no effect on plain text |
| `BaseForm` | added | 15 | BaseForm[expr, b] inert wrapper (2 <= b <= 36): integers and approximate reals in expr show as digits in base b with subscript _b (e.g. 100001_2); reals keep as many base-b digits as their decimal precision (6 digits for machine numbers) and drop trailing zeros |
| `Baseline` | added | 3 | inert symbol: alignment symbol: vertical alignment on the text baseline (top line in plain text) |
| `BaselinePosition` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Grid, Row, Column, Framed, ...: baseline for alignment with text (accepted) |
| `Before` | added | 4 | inert symbol: position symbol: Labeled/Placed position, same as Left |
| `Below` | added | 4 | inert symbol: position symbol: Labeled/Placed position, same as Bottom |
| `Bold` | added | 6 | inert symbol: style directive for Style (FontWeight -> "Bold"); no effect on plain text |
| `Bottom` | added | 8 | inert symbol: position/alignment symbol: Labeled position (default, label below), vertical alignment |
| `CForm` | added | 11 | CForm[expr] inert: C source for expr (Power(x,2), Sin(x), rationals as decimals, 1.5e10) |
| `Center` | added | 14 | inert symbol: position/alignment symbol: centred alignment in Column, Grid, Item, TableForm |
| `CharacterEncoding` | added | 12 | option of ToString and other output functions: the raw character encoding ("ASCII" writes other characters as \[Name] or \:xxxx escapes) |
| `Column` | added | 23 | Column[{e1, e2, ...}, alignment, spacing, opts] inert layout: elements one per line (spacing blank lines between), aligned Left, Center or Right |
| `ColumnAlignments` | added | 7 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): per-column horizontal alignment (honoured in text) |
| `ColumnLines` | added | 8 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): draw \| between columns (honoured in text) |
| `ColumnSpacings` | added | 8 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): spaces between columns (honoured in text) |
| `ColumnWidths` | added | 6 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): minimum column widths in characters (honoured in text) |
| `DecimalForm` | added | 5 | DecimalForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts]: approximate reals always in decimal notation (no exponent) |
| `DefaultPrintPrecision` | added | 6 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default Automatic) |
| `Delimiters` | added | 6 | inert symbol: option of various functions: the delimiters to use or look for (inert) |
| `DigitBlock` | added | 9 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default Infinity) |
| `DisableFormatting` | added | 3 | DisableFormatting[expr] gives expr as soon as it is evaluated (it only matters inside held expressions) |
| `EngineeringForm` | added | 5 | EngineeringForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts]: exponents that are multiples of 3 |
| `ExponentFunction` | added | 12 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default Automatic) |
| `ExponentStep` | added | 8 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default 1) |
| `FontFamily` | added | 5 | inert symbol: option of Style: font family name (a string); validated, no effect on plain text |
| `FontSize` | added | 7 | inert symbol: option of Style: font size (positive number, Scaled[s], Tiny/Small/Medium/Large); validated, no effect on plain text |
| `FormatType` | added | 10 | option of ToString and Text: the format type (InputForm, OutputForm, TeXForm, ...) |
| `FortranForm` | added | 6 | FortranForm[expr] inert: Fortran source for expr (x**2, Sin(x), rationals as decimals) |
| `Framed` | added | 4 | Framed[expr, opts] inert display wrapper; text draws an ASCII box (+---+ / \| expr \|) around expr |
| `Grid` | added | 57 | Grid[{{a, b}, ...}, opts] inert layout object; text: columns 3 spaces apart, rows separated by a blank line, left aligned (Wolfram's OutputForm), with Alignment, Spacings, Frame, Dividers and the GridBox options ColumnAlignments, ColumnSpacings, ColumnWidths, ColumnLines, RowAlignments, RowSpacings, RowLines, RowMinHeight honoured |
| `GridDefaultElement` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of GridBox: element inserted for a new grid entry (accepted) |
| `HeaderAlignment` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: alignment of headers (inert) |
| `HeaderBackground` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: header background colour (inert) |
| `HeaderDisplayFunction` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: function applied to headers before display (inert) |
| `HeaderLines` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of SemanticImport and related import functions: number of header rows (inert) |
| `HeaderSize` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: widths and heights of headers (inert) |
| `HeaderStyle` | added | 5 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: style of headers (inert) |
| `HiddenItems` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: positions of items to hide (inert) |
| `InsertLinebreaks` | added | 8 | InsertLinebreaks[s, n] (default 78) replaces whitespace by newlines so no line exceeds n characters; words longer than n are broken every n characters |
| `Italic` | added | 5 | inert symbol: style directive for Style (FontSlant -> "Italic"); no effect on plain text |
| `Item` | added | 8 | Item[expr, opts] inert: an entry of Grid, Column, ... with its own options (Alignment honoured in text) |
| `ItemAspectRatio` | added | 5 | inert symbol: option of GraphicsGrid: aspect ratio of each item's region (inert) |
| `ItemDisplayFunction` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Dataset: function applied to items before display (inert) |
| `Labeled` | added | 20 | Labeled[expr, lbl, pos] inert display wrapper (pos Bottom by default; Top, Left, Right, Before, After, Above, Below, a list of positions, or All for {bottom, left, top, right}); text places the label blocks around expr, centred above/below |
| `Left` | added | 13 | inert symbol: position/alignment symbol: Column/Grid/Item alignment, Labeled position (label to the left) |
| `ListFormat` | added | 7 | option of TextString: {"left", "sep", "right"} for lists (Automatic: {"{", ", ", "}"}) |
| `MathMLForm` | added | 6 | MathMLForm[expr] inert: presentation MathML for expr (<math> with mi, mn, mo, msup, mfrac, msqrt, mrow) |
| `MissingString` | added | 7 | option of TextString: the string for Missing[...] (default ""; Automatic: the reason; None: unchanged) |
| `MissingStyle` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of GeoRegionValuePlot and related: style for missing data (inert) |
| `Multicolumn` | added | 11 | Multicolumn[list, cols \| {rows, Automatic} \| {rows, cols}] evaluates to the corresponding Grid, filling the first column first (Appearance -> "Horizontal" fills by rows); Multicolumn[list] uses about Sqrt[n] columns; {rows, cols} drops elements beyond rows*cols |
| `NumberForm` | added | 104 | NumberForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts] inert display wrapper; ToString/REPL show approximate reals to n significant digits (f decimals), with DigitBlock, ExponentFunction, ExponentStep, NumberFormat, NumberMultiplier, NumberPadding, NumberPoint, NumberSeparator, NumberSigns, ScientificNotationThreshold, SignPadding, DefaultPrintPrecision |
| `NumberFormat` | added | 6 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default Automatic) |
| `NumberMarks` | added | 12 | option of ToString[..., InputForm]: whether approximate numbers carry ` marks (True: all; Automatic: arbitrary-precision numbers; False: none) |
| `NumberMultiplier` | added | 3 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default "×") |
| `NumberPadding` | added | 16 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default {"", "0"}) |
| `NumberPoint` | added | 7 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default ".") |
| `NumberSeparator` | added | 3 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default {",", " "}) |
| `NumberSigns` | added | 8 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default {"-", ""}) |
| `OutputForm` | added | 7 | OutputForm[expr] inert: shows expr in linear OutputForm text (strings without quotes) |
| `Overlay` | added | 6 | Overlay[{e1, e2, ...}, {i, j, ...}] inert: the expressions drawn on top of each other (top-left aligned); text superimposes the blocks, later ones overwriting earlier ones where they are not blank |
| `PaddedForm` | added | 5 | PaddedForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts]: numbers left-padded with spaces to n digits (room for a sign) |
| `PageWidth` | added | 7 | option of ToString: lines longer than n characters are broken at spaces |
| `Pagination` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of Multicolumn/GalleryView: items per page (accepted; plain text shows every item) |
| `PercentForm` | added | 7 | PercentForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts]: numbers shown as percentages (x 100, then %) |
| `Placed` | added | 6 | Placed[expr, pos] / Placed[{e1, ...}, pos, f] inert: an annotation at relative position pos (Top, Bottom, Left, Right, Center, Above, Below, Before, After, {x, y}, ...); text shows expr (f applied to each ei when given) |
| `Right` | added | 19 | inert symbol: position/alignment symbol: Column/Grid/Item alignment, Labeled position (label to the right) |
| `Row` | added | 13 | Row[{e1, e2, ...}] / Row[list, sep] inert layout: the elements' text concatenated (with sep between); multi-line elements are placed side by side |
| `RowAlignments` | added | 8 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): vertical alignment of multi-line rows (honoured in text) |
| `RowLines` | added | 6 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): draw a line between rows (honoured in text) |
| `RowMinHeight` | added | 6 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): minimum row height in lines (honoured in text) |
| `RowSpacings` | added | 13 | inert symbol: option of Grid (GridBox): blank lines between rows (honoured in text) |
| `ScientificForm` | added | 7 | ScientificForm[expr, n \| {n, f}, opts]: one digit before the point and an explicit exponent |
| `ScientificNotationThreshold` | added | 7 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default {-5, 6}) |
| `ScriptLevel` | added | 5 | inert symbol: option of Style: script nesting level (non-negative integer); validated, no effect on text |
| `Short` | added | 8 | Short[expr, n] inert wrapper: shows expr in about n lines (n * 78 characters), omitted runs of k elements written <<k>> (Skeleton[k]) and long strings elided with an ellipsis |
| `SignPadding` | added | 9 | option of NumberForm and related number forms (inert symbol; default False) |
| `Skeleton` | added | 3 | Skeleton[k] inert: k omitted elements, shown as <<k>> (what Short inserts) |
| `StandardForm` | added | 3 | StandardForm[expr] inert: shows expr in standard (linear) text |
| `StringForm` | added | 12 | StringForm["... `` ... `n` ...", e1, e2, ...] inert: shows the template with `` replaced by successive ei and `n` by en (\` is a literal backquote); out-of-range or unmatched placeholders raise |
| `StringSkeleton` | added | 3 | StringSkeleton[k] inert: k omitted characters of a string, shown as an ellipsis |
| `Style` | added | 22 | Style[expr, styles...] inert display wrapper (Bold, Italic, colours, font size n, "stylename", FontSize -> n, FontFamily -> "name", Background -> c, ...); plain text shows expr unchanged |
| `SurdForm` | added | 4 | inert symbol: option of RadicalBox/SqrtBox: whether the radical is a Surd (inert) |
| `TableAlignments` | added | 7 | inert symbol: option of TableForm: Left, Center, Right (honoured in text) |
| `TableDepth` | added | 7 | inert symbol: option of TableForm: levels laid out as a table; deeper levels shown as lists |
| `TableDirections` | added | 7 | inert symbol: option of TableForm: Column/Row for successive dimensions (Column: first dimension down) |
| `TableForm` | added | 31 | TableForm[list, opts] inert display wrapper; text: rows separated by a blank line and columns by 3 spaces (Wolfram's OutputForm), with TableDepth, TableDirections, TableSpacing, TableAlignments and TableHeadings |
| `TableHeadings` | added | 7 | inert symbol: option of TableForm: {row headings, column headings} (None, Automatic or lists) |
| `TableSpacing` | added | 10 | inert symbol: option of TableForm: {blank lines between rows, spaces between columns} (default {1, 3}) |
| `TeXForm` | added | 27 | TeXForm[expr] inert: shows the LaTeX of expr (TraditionalForm conventions: \frac, \sqrt, \sin (x), x^2+1); ToString[TeXForm[expr]] gives the TeX string |
| `Text` | added | 7 | Text[expr] shows expr as plain text; Text[expr, coords, offset, dir] is an inert graphics primitive (coordinates a list of 2 or 3 numbers, Scaled[...] or Offset[...]) |
| `TextElement` | added | 4 | TextElement[text, props] / TextElement[{e1, e2, ...}, props] inert: an element of text with a property association; text shows the elements' strings concatenated |
| `TextString` | added | 30 | TextString[expr, opts] human-readable string: strings unchanged, numbers in decimal notation (6 digits for machine reals, rationals as decimals), symbols by name, Missing per MissingString (default ""), lists per ListFormat, Row/Column/TableForm/Grid as text, otherwise InputForm; BooleanStrings |
| `Top` | added | 11 | inert symbol: position/alignment symbol: Labeled position (label above), vertical alignment in Grid rows |
| `TotalWidth` | added | 7 | option of ToString: output longer than n characters is shortened with <<k>> skeletons |
| `TraditionalForm` | added | 8 | TraditionalForm[expr] inert: shows expr in traditional mathematical notation (f(x), sin(x), e^x, π, i, polynomials in decreasing degree) |
| `Visible` | added | 5 | inert symbol: option of Style and notebooks: whether shown (True/False); validated |

### physics

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Acceleration` | fixed | 3 | velocity change/time, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `BeatFrequency` | fixed | 3 | \|f1 - f2\|, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `BraggLaw` | fixed | 3 | BraggLaw[d, theta] and [d, theta, n] give the wavelength 2 d Sin[theta]/n (theta in degrees); BraggLaw[d, theta, n, lambda] gives the Bragg angle in degrees for lambda (theta ignored); impossible angles raise; exact for exact input |
| `Capacitance` | fixed | 3 | Q/V, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `CapacitorEnergy` | fixed | 3 | C V^2/2, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `CarnotEfficiency` | fixed | 4 | CarnotEfficiency[Th, Tc] = 1 - Tc/Th (kelvin), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); Th <= 0, Tc < 0 or Tc > Th raise (legacy returned negative efficiencies) |
| `CentripetalForce` | fixed | 3 | m v^2/r, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `ComptonWavelength` | fixed | 3 | scattered wavelength lambda + h/(m_e c) (1 - Cos[theta]), theta in degrees; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `CoulombForce` | fixed | 3 | k q1 q2/r^2; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `DeBroglieWavelength` | fixed | 3 | h/p; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `DopplerShift` | fixed | 3 | f0 (c + v_observer)/(c - v_source), source approaching for v_source > 0; default c = 343 m/s (sound in air) |
| `ElectricField` | fixed | 3 | k Q/r^2; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `ElectricPotential` | fixed | 3 | k Q/r; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `EntropyChange` | fixed | 3 | Q/T, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); T <= 0 raises |
| `EscapeVelocity` | fixed | 3 | Sqrt[2 G M/r]; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `FaradayLaw` | fixed | 3 | -dPhi/dt, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `Force` | fixed | 3 | m a, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `FreeFallDistance` | fixed | 3 | g t^2/2; default g = 9.80665 (standard gravity; legacy used 9.81) |
| `HeatCapacity` | fixed | 3 | Q/dT, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `Impulse` | fixed | 3 | F t, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `InductanceEnergy` | fixed | 3 | L I^2/2, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `KineticEnergy` | fixed | 3 | m v^2/2, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `LCFrequency` | fixed | 3 | 1/(2 Pi Sqrt[L C]), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats); L, C <= 0 raise |
| `LengthContraction` | fixed | 3 | L0/gamma; default c = 299792458 (legacy 3e8) |
| `MagneticFieldWire` | fixed | 3 | mu0 I/(2 Pi r); default mu0 is CODATA 1.25663706212e-6 |
| `MagneticForce` | fixed | 3 | q v B (perpendicular), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `Magnification` | fixed | 3 | -di/do, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `MassEnergyEquivalence` | fixed | 3 | m c^2; default c = 299792458 (legacy 3e8) |
| `MechanicalPower` | fixed | 3 | work/time, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `Momentum` | fixed | 3 | m v, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `OrbitalVelocity` | fixed | 3 | Sqrt[G M/r]; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `PhotonEnergy` | fixed | 3 | h c/lambda; uses CODATA values by default (legacy used rounded defaults such as G = 6.674e-11, c = 3e8, k = 8.99e9, h = 6.626e-34) |
| `PotentialEnergy` | fixed | 3 | m g h; default g = 9.80665 (standard gravity; legacy used 9.81) |
| `PowerElectric` | fixed | 3 | V I, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `ProjectileMaxHeight` | fixed | 3 | v^2 Sin[theta]^2/(2 g), theta in degrees; default g = 9.80665 (standard gravity; legacy used 9.81) |
| `ProjectileRange` | fixed | 3 | v^2 Sin[2 theta]/g, theta in degrees; default g = 9.80665 (standard gravity; legacy used 9.81) |
| `RelativisticMass` | fixed | 3 | gamma m0; default c = 299792458 (legacy 3e8) |
| `ResonanceFrequencyPendulum` | fixed | 3 | Sqrt[g/L]/(2 Pi); default g = 9.80665 (standard gravity; legacy used 9.81) |
| `ResonanceFrequencySpring` | fixed | 3 | Sqrt[k/m]/(2 Pi), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `RydbergFormula` | fixed | 3 | 1/lambda = R (1/n1^2 - 1/n2^2); default R is CODATA 10973731.568160 (legacy 1.097e7) |
| `SnellsLaw` | fixed | 3 | refraction angle in degrees ArcSin[n1 Sin[theta1]/n2]; total internal reflection raises (legacy returned an Error expression); exact for exact input |
| `StefanBoltzmann` | fixed | 5 | StefanBoltzmann[T, emissivity = 1]: emitted power per area eps sigma T^4; sigma from the SI definition (legacy 5.67e-8); emissivity outside [0, 1] or T < 0 raises |
| `ThermalExpansion` | fixed | 3 | L0 (1 + alpha dT), exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `TimeDilation` | fixed | 3 | gamma t0; default c = 299792458 (legacy 3e8); \|v\| >= c raises |
| `UncertaintyProduct` | fixed | 3 | dx dp/hbar (dimensionless, >= 1/2 by Heisenberg); default hbar is CODATA (legacy 1.055e-34) |
| `Velocity` | fixed | 3 | displacement/time, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `WaveSpeed` | fixed | 3 | f lambda, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |
| `Work` | fixed | 3 | F d, exact for exact input (legacy converted to floats) |

### special functions (batch 2)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AiryAiPrime` | added | 3 | derivative of AiryAi; AiryAiPrime[0] = -1/(3^(1/3) Gamma[1/3]) |
| `AiryBiPrime` | added | 3 | derivative of AiryBi; AiryBiPrime[0] = 3^(1/6)/Gamma[1/3] |
| `BarnesG` | added | 6 | Barnes G-function, G(z + 1) = Gamma(z) G(z); BarnesG[n] = Prod[k!, {k, 0, n - 2}] for positive integers, 0 at non-positive integers; mpmath barnesg |
| `BetaRegularized` | added | 6 | BetaRegularized[z, a, b] = Beta[z, a, b]/Beta[a, b]; exact for positive integer a, b |
| `CoshIntegral` | added | 4 | Chi(z) = EulerGamma + Log[z] + Integral[(Cosh[t] - 1)/t, {t, 0, z}], branch cut along the negative real axis (CoshIntegral[-1.] = 0.837867 + 3.14159 I); CoshIntegral[0] = -Infinity |
| `ExpIntegralE` | fixed | 8 | ExpIntegralE[n, 0] = 1/(n - 1) for n > 1 and ComplexInfinity for n <= 1 (legacy left the integer cases unevaluated); numeric values at any precision via mpmath |
| `FactorialPower` | added | 5 | falling factorial x (x - 1) ... (x - n + 1) = Gamma[x + 1]/Gamma[x - n + 1]; FactorialPower[x, n, h] = x (x - h) ... (x - (n - 1) h); stays unevaluated for symbolic x |
| `FresnelC` | added | 4 | FresnelC[z] = Integral[Cos[Pi t^2/2], {t, 0, z}]; FresnelC[Infinity] = 1/2 |
| `FresnelS` | added | 4 | FresnelS[z] = Integral[Sin[Pi t^2/2], {t, 0, z}]; FresnelS[Infinity] = 1/2 |
| `GammaRegularized` | added | 8 | GammaRegularized[a, z] = Gamma[a, z]/Gamma[a] (regularized upper incomplete gamma Q); exact for positive integer a; mpmath gammainc(regularized=True) |
| `HurwitzLerchPhi` | added | 3 | HurwitzLerchPhi[z, s, a] = Sum[z^k/(k + a)^s, {k, 0, Infinity}] (principal powers) |
| `Hypergeometric0F1Regularized` | added | 3 | regularized 0F1: Hypergeometric0F1/Gamma[b] (finite for non-positive integer b, e.g. via the limit formula z^(m+1) (a)_(m+1)/(m+1)! 0F1(a + m + 1; m + 2; z) for b = -m) |
| `Hypergeometric1F1Regularized` | added | 3 | regularized 1F1: Hypergeometric1F1/Gamma[b] (finite for non-positive integer b, e.g. via the limit formula z^(m+1) (a)_(m+1)/(m+1)! 1F1(a + m + 1; m + 2; z) for b = -m) |
| `Hypergeometric2F1Regularized` | added | 3 | regularized 2F1: Hypergeometric2F1/Gamma[b] (finite for non-positive integer b, e.g. via the limit formula z^(m+1) (a)_(m+1)/(m+1)! 2F1(a + m + 1; m + 2; z) for b = -m) |
| `HypergeometricPFQ` | added | 5 | generalized hypergeometric function pFq(a1, ..., ap; b1, ..., bq; z); exact closed forms via SymPy hyperexpand; p = q + 1 has the branch cut [1, Infinity) |
| `HypergeometricPFQRegularized` | added | 3 | regularized generalized hypergeometric function pFq(a1, ..., ap; b1, ..., bq; z)/Prod[Gamma[bj]] (finite for non-positive integer bj) |
| `HypergeometricU` | added | 4 | Tricomi confluent hypergeometric function U(a, b, z) (branch cut along the negative real axis); HypergeometricU[0, b, z] = 1 |
| `InverseErf` | added | 7 | inverse of Erf on [-1, 1]: InverseErf[0] = 0, InverseErf[1] = Infinity; complex and \|x\| > 1 arguments raise (not supported) |
| `InverseErfc` | added | 6 | inverse of Erfc on [0, 2] (Newton refinement keeps full relative precision for tiny arguments); complex arguments raise (not supported) |

| `MeijerG` | added | 3 | MeijerG[{{a1, ..., an}, {an+1, ..., ap}}, {{b1, ..., bm}, {bm+1, ..., bq}}, z] (and the form with a final argument r) evaluated with mpmath meijerg for approximate arguments; exact arguments stay unevaluated (FunctionExpand is not implemented) |
| `Pochhammer` | added | 6 | rising factorial (a)_n = Gamma[a + n]/Gamma[a]; exact for integer n (including negative n: Pochhammer[a, -n] = 1/((a - 1)...(a - n))); stays unevaluated for symbolic a (Wolfram) |
| `PolyLog` | added | 11 | PolyLog[n, z] = Sum[z^k/k^n, {k, 1, Infinity}] continued analytically, branch cut [1, Infinity) (PolyLog[2, 2.] = 2.4674 - 2.17759 I); PolyLog[1, z] = -Log[1 - z], PolyLog[n, z] for integer n <= 0 is rational, PolyLog[s, 1] = Zeta[s], PolyLog[2, 1/2] = Pi^2/12 - Log[2]^2/2. The Nielsen form PolyLog[n, p, z] is not supported |
