| `Snippet` | added | 4 | Snippet[doc] / Snippet[doc, n]: the first n lines (default 1) of a ContentObject's text or of a File, long lines wrapped at 80 characters; a string is its own snippet |
| `SpecificityGoal` | added | 3 | inert symbol: ImageIdentify option; only the default is accepted by the search functions |
| `SquareRepeatingElement` | added | 3 | inert symbol: form/interpreter specification of a square array (forms are not available) |
| `StringApply` | added | 8 | applies f to Characters[s] and joins the result (nested lists of strings keep their structure, joined at the innermost level); StringApply[f] operator form; threads over lists |
| `StringFormat` | added | 6 | heuristic import format of a string: magic numbers for binary formats (PNG, GIF, JPEG, PDF, ZIP, GZIP, TIFF, WAV, WXF, ...), else JSON, XML, HTML, SVG, TSV, CSV, Table (numeric columns) or Text; "Binary" for unrecognised binary data |
| `StringFormatQ` | added | 5 | whether the string is (heuristically) in the format or any of a list of formats; "Text" and "Binary" are the generic text/binary classes |
| `StringTemplate` | added | 17 | TemplateObject[{pieces}, InsertionFunction -> TextString, CombinerFunction -> StringJoin] from a string or File: `` sequential slots, `n` positional, `name` named, <* expr *> template expressions with #name/#n slots; \` escapes; an optional association of default values |
| `StringToByteArray` | added | 5 | bytes of a string in UTF-8 (default) or a named $CharacterEncodings encoding; a character the encoding cannot represent raises |
| `StringToStream` | added | 19 | InputStream[String, n] reading from the string; Find and FindList read records from it (the stream keeps its position); Read/ReadList/Close belong to the stream I/O batch |
| `SyntaxLength` | added | 3 | length of the longest prefix that is syntactically valid so far (the position of the first syntax error); StringLength + 1 when the whole string is valid but incomplete |
| `SyntaxQ` | added | 4 | True if the string parses as one Wolfram Language expression (InputForm); other forms raise |
| `TemplateApply` | added | 64 | applies a template (TemplateObject, string, File or any expression containing TemplateSlot, TemplateExpression, TemplateIf, TemplateSequence, TemplateWith or nested TemplateObject) to positional arguments (a list, or a single non-list value) and named values from associations; the template's own bound association is searched first; :> values are inserted unevaluated; a missing slot without "DefaultValue" inserts Missing["TemplateSlotAbsent", key] ("" in string templates) |
| `TemplateExpression` | added | 4 | template construct: HoldFirst; the expression is filled in and evaluated on application |
| `TemplateIf` | added | 7 | template construct: HoldAll; TemplateIf[cond, t, f] chooses a branch at application time |
| `TemplateObject` | added | 26 | TemplateObject[body, (assoc), InsertionFunction -> f, CombinerFunction -> g] is applied with TemplateApply or t[args]; defaults Identity |
| `TemplateSequence` | added | 6 | template construct: TemplateSequence[body, values] splices one copy of body per value, with Delimiters, InsertionFunction and "DefaultValue" |
| `TemplateSlot` | added | 28 | template construct: slot n or "name" with InsertionFunction and "DefaultValue" options |
| `TemplateWith` | added | 3 | template construct: HoldAll; TemplateWith["name" -> value or list, body] binds named slots |
| `TextSearch` | added | 35 | searches a SearchIndexObject, a list of them, a file, a directory, or ContentObjects; queries: "phrase", {all}, any \| alternatives, Except/Not, "field" -> q, FixedOrder, SearchAdjustment (boost, MaxWordGap), And/Or/ContainsAll/ContainsAny/ContainsNone, All, SearchQueryString; words are lower-cased with plural stemming; results ranked by BM25 (Lucene's formula, k1 = 1.2, b = 0.75); ContentFieldOptions weights, DocumentWeightingRules, MaxItems; third argument a property, "Count" or "ContentObject"; numeric range queries raise |
| `TokenWords` | added | 3 | inert symbol: Read option (stream reading belongs to the stream I/O batch) |
| `UpdateSearchIndex` | added | 3 | re-reads the files and directories an index was built from (adding new files, dropping deleted ones); returns the index |
| `ValuePreprocessingFunction` | added | 3 | inert symbol: PersistentSymbol option; accepted by CreateSearchIndex/TextSearch only with its default |
| `WordCount` | added | 4 | number of words, identified as by TextWords (hyphenated and apostrophe words count once) |
| `WordCounts` | added | 5 | association of word counts (words as in TextWords); WordCounts[s, n] counts n-grams of consecutive words separated only by whitespace (keys are lists of words); Wolfram's *Counts ordering; IgnoreCase |
| `WordOrientation` | added | 3 | inert symbol: WordCloud option (WordCloud graphics are not available) |
| `WordSearch` | added | 6 | Find/FindList option: the text must appear as a whole word |
| `WordSelectionFunction` | added | 3 | inert symbol: WordCloud option (WordCloud graphics are not available) |
| `WordSeparators` | added | 3 | Find/FindList option: strings separating words (default {" ", "\t"}) |
| `WordSpacings` | added | 3 | inert symbol: WordCloud option (WordCloud graphics are not available) |
| `WordStem` | added | 5 | Porter stemming algorithm (reference implementation: bli->ble, logi->log), preserving the word's case and surrounding punctuation; listable |
| `XMLTemplate` | added | 26 | TemplateObject from XML/HTML text or File with <wolfram:slot id= inserter=>default</wolfram:slot>, expr, if test=, which/else, sequence (values, slot, index, delimiters, default, inserter), with, template (inserter, combiner), get path=, comment and verbatim, plus `slot` and <* expr *>; InsertionFunction "HTMLFragment" (escapes & < >) by default, "JSON" supported; block tags alone on a line drop that line |

### strings (batch 1)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Alphabet` | added | 3 | Alphabet[] and Alphabet["English"] give the 26 lower-case letters; other languages are not supported (curated data) |
| `Capitalize` | added | 4 | upper-cases the first character and leaves the rest; Capitalize[s, "AllWords"] does that for every word, "LongWords" for words longer than three characters, "FirstWord" = default ("TitleCase" with its small-word rules is not supported) |
| `CharacterRange` | added | 3 | characters from c1 to c2 by code point (strings or integer codes); empty when c2 < c1 |
| `Decapitalize` | added | 3 | lower-cases the first character only |
| `DigitCharacter` | added | 14 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `DigitQ` | added | 3 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `EditDistance` | added | 32 | Levenshtein distance (insertions, deletions, substitutions) between strings or lists; IgnoreCase |
| `EndOfLine` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `EndOfString` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `FromLetterNumber` | added | 4 | the n-th lower-case English letter for 1 <= n <= 26 (negative n counts from z, 0 gives " "); other values raise; threads over lists |
| `FromRomanNumeral` | added | 13 | integer value of a Roman numeral string ("N" -> 0) |
| `HammingDistance` | added | 4 | number of differing positions between equal-length strings or lists (unequal lengths raise); IgnoreCase |
| `HexadecimalCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `IgnoreCase` | added | 23 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `IntegerString` | added | 10 | digits of \|n\| (the sign is dropped, as Wolfram) in base 2-36 with lower-case letters, padded with zeros or truncated to the last len digits; IntegerString[n, "Roman"]; threads over lists |
| `LetterCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `LetterNumber` | added | 3 | position of a letter in the English alphabet (either case), 0 for other characters; a string of several characters gives a list; threads over lists |
| `LetterQ` | added | 4 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `Longest` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `LongestCommonSequence` | added | 3 | longest common (not necessarily contiguous) subsequence of two strings or lists by dynamic programming; among equal-length answers the one found by the standard back-trace is returned |
| `LongestCommonSubsequence` | added | 3 | longest contiguous common run of two strings or lists (Wolfram's meaning; the non-contiguous one is LongestCommonSequence); ties go to the earliest position in the first argument |
| `LowerCaseQ` | added | 4 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `NumberString` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `Overlaps` | added | 5 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `PalindromeQ` | added | 5 | True for a string, list or non-negative integer (decimal digits) equal to its reverse; other expressions False |
| `PrintableASCIIQ` | added | 3 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `PunctuationCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `RegularExpression` | added | 4 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `RemoveDiacritics` | added | 4 | drops combining marks after Unicode NFKD decomposition (ligatures such as æ are kept) |
| `RomanNumeral` | added | 14 | Roman numeral string of n for 1-4999 (sign dropped); RomanNumeral[0] gives "N"; threads over lists; the overbar forms for n >= 5000 are not supported |
| `Shortest` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StartOfLine` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StartOfString` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StringCases` | added | 35 | substrings matching a string pattern, or rule right-hand sides with named parts substituted; StringCases[s, p, n]; Overlaps -> False by default; IgnoreCase |
| `StringContainsQ` | fixed | 8 | string patterns (legacy: literal substrings only); IgnoreCase; threads over lists |
| `StringCount` | fixed | 10 | non-overlapping matches of a string pattern (legacy: literal substrings only); Overlaps -> True counts one match per start position; IgnoreCase; the empty literal raises |
| `StringDelete` | fixed | 7 | deletes every match of a string pattern (or list of patterns); IgnoreCase; the legacy slice form StringDelete[s, i, j] raises (use StringDrop[s, {i, j}]) |
| `StringEndsQ` | fixed | 6 | string patterns (legacy: literal suffix only); IgnoreCase |
| `StringExpression` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `StringExtract` | added | 5 | StringExtract[s, n] gives the n-th whitespace-separated piece (spans, lists and negative n allowed); StringExtract[s, sep -> n] splits at sep |
| `StringFreeQ` | added | 3 | not StringContainsQ; string patterns; IgnoreCase |
| `StringMatchQ` | added | 26 | whole-string match against a string pattern; literal patterns use the metacharacters * (any characters) and @ (characters other than upper-case letters); IgnoreCase; threads over lists |
| `StringPartition` | added | 3 | substrings of length n (incomplete final piece dropped), offset d, or UpTo[n] keeping the remainder |
| `StringPosition` | added | 4 | {{start, end}, ...} 1-based; Overlaps -> True by default (one match per start position), StringPosition[s, p, n]; IgnoreCase |
| `StringReplace` | fixed | 24 | StringReplace[s, p -> r], [s, {rules}], [s, rules, n] with string patterns; named pattern parts substitute into the right-hand side; RegularExpression rules accept "$1" group references; non-string replacements give a StringExpression; the legacy form StringReplace[s, old, new] raises |
| `StringReplaceList` | added | 3 | every string obtained by replacing a single match of the rules (one result per match position and rule, left to right); StringReplaceList[s, rules, n] keeps at most n |
| `StringReplacePart` | added | 3 | StringReplacePart[s, new, {m, n}], a list of ranges with one new string, or a list of new strings matched to the ranges; negative positions count from the end |
| `StringRiffle` | added | 9 | StringRiffle[list] joins with spaces (nested lists: spaces at the lowest level, one more newline per level above), StringRiffle[list, sep], StringRiffle[list, {left, sep, right}], StringRiffle[list, sep1, sep2, ...] per level; non-strings are converted with ToString |
| `StringRotateLeft` | added | 3 | cyclic rotation by n characters (default 1) |
| `StringRotateRight` | added | 3 | cyclic rotation by n characters (default 1) |
| `StringSplit` | added | 15 | splits at whitespace or at matches of a string pattern; empty pieces at the ends are dropped (kept with StringSplit[s, p, All]), interior ones kept; p -> r inserts r at each split; StringSplit[s, p, n] gives at most n pieces; IgnoreCase; threads over lists |
| `StringStartsQ` | fixed | 8 | string patterns (legacy: literal prefix only); IgnoreCase |
| `StringTakeDrop` | added | 3 | {StringTake[s, spec], StringDrop[s, spec]} |
| `StringTrim` | fixed | 7 | StringTrim[s] removes leading and trailing whitespace; StringTrim[s, p] removes a leading and a trailing match of the string pattern p |
| `Symbol` | added | 10 | Symbol["name"] gives the symbol (evaluated); invalid names raise |
| `SymbolName` | added | 4 | name of a symbol as a string (no context prefix) |
| `TextWords` | added | 3 | words of the text: runs of letters/digits, keeping internal apostrophes and hyphens (rule-based; Wolfram's tokenizer may differ on unusual punctuation) |
| `ToExpression` | added | 14 | parses and evaluates InputForm text; ToExpression[s, InputForm, h] wraps the unevaluated parse in h (e.g. Hold); invalid syntax gives $Failed; non-strings are returned evaluated |
| `ToLowerCase` | added | 4 | lower-cases every letter; threads over lists |
| `ToUpperCase` | added | 14 | upper-cases every letter; threads over lists |
| `UpperCaseQ` | added | 4 | True when every character of the string satisfies the test (so True for ""); non-strings False |
| `Whitespace` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `WhitespaceCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `WordBoundary` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |
| `WordCharacter` | added | 3 | string pattern object / option interpreted by the batch-1 string functions |

### strings and dates

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AlphabetPosition` | fixed | 3 | like Wolfram LetterNumber: position in the English alphabet, 0 for non-letters; multi-character strings give a list (legacy returned 0) |
| `Base64Decode` | fixed | 3 | invalid Base64 or non-UTF-8 data now raises |
| `Base64Encode` | verified | 3 | Base64 of the UTF-8 bytes (MikoshiLang) |
| `CaesarCipher` | verified | 3 | shift ASCII letters by k (k may be negative); integer k only |
| `Characters` | verified | 5 | list of single-character strings |
| `DateTimeString` | fixed | 3 | ISO 8601 "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS" for now or a date list (MikoshiLang); legacy ignored its argument |
| `DayOfWeek` | fixed | 3 | day of the week of a Gregorian date as a symbol (Friday), DayOfWeek[y, m, d] or DayOfWeek[{y, m, d}] (Wolfram DayName); legacy returned a string and truncated reals |
| `DaysInMonth` | fixed | 3 | days in month m of year y; invalid months raise |
| `FromCharacterCode` | fixed | 8 | FromCharacterCode[n] and FromCharacterCode[{n1, n2, ...}] (legacy accepted only one code and truncated reals) |
| `HexDecode` | fixed | 3 | invalid hex now raises (legacy returned an Error expression) |
| `HexEncode` | verified | 3 | hex of the UTF-8 bytes (MikoshiLang) |
| `LeapYearQ` | fixed | 3 | Gregorian leap year test; LeapYearQ[{y, ...}] accepted |
| `MD5` | verified | 3 | hex digest of the UTF-8 bytes (= Hash[s, "MD5", "HexString"]) |
| `ROT13` | verified | 3 | ROT13 on ASCII letters |
| `SHA1` | verified | 3 | hex digest of the UTF-8 bytes (= Hash[s, "SHA1", "HexString"]) |
| `SHA256` | verified | 3 | hex digest of the UTF-8 bytes (= Hash[s, "SHA256", "HexString"]) |
| `SHA512` | verified | 3 | hex digest of the UTF-8 bytes (= Hash[s, "SHA512", "HexString"]) |
| `StringCapitalize` | fixed | 3 | upper-cases the first character only (Wolfram Capitalize); legacy also lower-cased the rest ("iPhone" -> "Iphone") |
| `StringCenter` | fixed | 3 | centres s in width n (extra padding on the right, MikoshiLang); single-character fill, width checked |
| `StringDrop` | fixed | 3 | StringDrop[s, n], [s, -n], [s, {n}], [s, {m, n}] |
| `StringInsert` | fixed | 3 | Wolfram argument order StringInsert[s, new, n]: new starts at position n (negative n counts from the end, -1 appends); legacy was StringInsert[s, i, new] with 0-based i |
| `StringPadLeft` | fixed | 3 | pads on the left or truncates to the rightmost n characters, as Wolfram (legacy never truncated); single-character padding |
| `StringPadRight` | fixed | 3 | pads on the right or truncates to the leftmost n characters, as Wolfram; single-character padding |
| `StringPart` | fixed | 3 | 1-based (legacy was 0-based: StringPart["abc", 1] gave "b"); negative positions count from the end; a list of positions gives a list |
| `StringRepeat` | fixed | 5 | StringRepeat[s, n] for n >= 1; legacy truncated non-integers |
| `StringReverse` | verified | 3 | reverses the characters |
| `StringTake` | fixed | 4 | StringTake[s, n], [s, -n], [s, {n}], [s, {m, n}] (legacy: only the first n, and Python slicing silently accepted negative/out-of-range n) |
| `StringToLowerCase` | verified | 3 | alias of ToLowerCase |
| `StringToTitleCase` | fixed | 3 | capitalises the first letter of each whitespace-separated word and leaves the rest unchanged; legacy str.title() mangled words ("don't" -> "Don'T", "NASA" -> "Nasa") |
| `StringToUpperCase` | verified | 3 | alias of ToUpperCase |
| `TimeString` | fixed | 3 | TimeString[] -> "HH:MM:SS" (MikoshiLang); legacy only had a 1-argument form that ignored its argument |
| `ToCharacterCode` | fixed | 5 | list of all character codes (legacy returned only the first code); lists of strings thread |
| `UnixTime` | fixed | 3 | UnixTime[] = whole seconds since 1970-01-01 00:00 UTC; UnixTime[{y, m, d, ...}] interprets the date in UTC; legacy only had a 1-argument form that ignored its argument |
| `XORCipher` | fixed | 3 | hex string of UTF-8 bytes XOR the repeated key; empty key raises |

### structural (W03)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AllSameBy` | added | 4 | True when f[e] is the same (SameQ) for every element (short-circuits; lists of length 0 or 1 give True); AllSameBy[f] operator form |
| `AlphabeticOrder` | added | 9 | 1 / -1 / 0 for alphabetical (dictionary) order of two strings: punctuation < digits < letters, letters by base letter, then diacritics, then case (CaseOrdering "LowerFirst" default or "UpperFirst"); options IgnoreCase, IgnoreDiacritics, IgnorePunctuation; English collation only (other languages raise); AlphabeticOrder[] / AlphabeticOrder["English"] / AlphabeticOrder[opts] operator forms |
| `AlphabeticSort` | added | 5 | sorts strings into alphabetical order (the AlphabeticOrder collation; stable); AlphabeticSort[list, "English"] and the CaseOrdering / IgnoreCase / IgnoreDiacritics / IgnorePunctuation options |
| `AnySubset` | added | 5 | AnySubset[{c1, ...} \| {lab -> c, ...} \| EntityClass[...]], AnySubset[choices, max \| {min, max}]: an inert interpreter/form specification (arguments are validated) |
| `ArrayFilter` | added | 6 | ArrayFilter[f, array, r \| {r1, r2, ...} \| template] applies f to the range-r blocks around every element (template 0s become Missing[]); Padding -> "Fixed" (default), None (smaller result), a constant, "Periodic", "Reflected" or "Reversed" |
| `ArrayPad` | added | 9 | ArrayPad[array, m \| {m, n} \| {{m1, n1}, ...}, padding]: constant padding (default 0) or "Fixed", "Periodic", "Reflected", "Reversed"; negative amounts trim; any depth of full array (cyclic constant lists and the differences / extrapolated paddings raise) |
| `ArrayReduce` | added | 4 | ArrayReduce[f, array, n \| n1 ;; n2 \| {n1, n2, ...} \| {{...}, {...}}]: f applied to the vectors (or arrays) along the given dimensions, which are removed; f's result takes the place of each vector |
| `ArrayResample` | added | 18 | ArrayResample[array, n \| Scaled[s] \| All \| {d1, d2, ...}] and ArrayResample[array, dspec, "Point"]: point sampling on 1..m (n samples at 1 + (k-1)(m-1)/(n-1); Scaled[s] gives Round[s (m-1)] + 1 samples), separably along each dimension, exact for exact data; Resampling -> "Linear" (default), "Nearest" (halfway averages), "NearestLeft", "NearestRight". "Bin" schemes, subranges, Automatic dimensions, DataRange, Antialiasing -> True and other kernels raise |
| `AssociationComap` | added | 3 | AssociationComap[{f1, f2, ...}, x] = <\|f1 -> f1[x], ...\|> (a repeated key keeps its last value); AssociationComap[fs] operator form |
| `CaseOrdering` | added | 3 | option of AlphabeticOrder and AlphabeticSort: "LowerFirst" (default), "UpperFirst" or Automatic |
| `CenterArray` | added | 4 | CenterArray[a, n \| {n1, n2, ...}, pad]: a centred in an array of the given dimensions (extra padding on the right; Inherited keeps a dimension; a lower-rank a is centred at the deepest levels); CenterArray[n] has a single 1; non-list a means {a}; trimming by an odd amount raises; padding as in ArrayPad |
| `ColumnsEqual` | added | 3 | option for GridBox (equal column widths); an inert option name |
| `ComponentKeys` | added | 3 | option naming the data components of a time or event series; an inert option name |
| `Cyclic` | added | 4 | Cyclic[{x1, ..., xn}] cyclic specification; Cyclic[{...}][k] = x[[Mod[k, n, 1]]] |
| `DeleteContents` | added | 3 | option for DeleteDirectory (whether to delete the directory contents); an inert option name |
| `Discard` | added | 4 | removes the elements for which crit is True (opposite of Select); Discard[data, crit, n] removes only the first n; data may be a list, any expression or an association; crit -> "Element" \| "Index" \| {props}; Discard[crit] operator form |
| `Dispatch` | added | 5 | Dispatch[rules] is an optimised rule set usable wherever the rule list is (ReplaceAll, Replace, ReplaceRepeated, ...); Normal[Dispatch[rules]] gives the rules; Dispatch[<\|...\|>] takes the association's rules |
| `Elementwise` | added | 3 | Elementwise[f][args...] threads f over all lists among the arguments to the deepest common level (makes non-listable functions behave as listable) |
| `FoldWhile` | added | 4 | folds f over the list while test of the most recent m (default 1, or All) results is True; returns the first result failing the test (or the last one); FoldWhile[f, list, test]; extra n / -n steps; FoldWhile[f, test] operator form |
| `FoldWhileList` | added | 3 | the list of FoldWhile results, starting with x |
| `IncrementalFunction` | added | 15 | IncrementalFunction[f] is an inert function that can pause; IncrementalFunction[f][args] creates a running instance DataStructure["IncrementalFunction", ...] (no FunctionCompile step is needed). ds["Next"] runs to the next IncrementalYield (giving its value) or IncrementalReceive (giving Null); ds["Send", x] resumes, making the pending IncrementalReceive return x; once the code ends the call gives Failure["IncrementalFunctionTerminated", ...]. Errors inside the function are raised to the caller |
| `IncrementalObject` | added | 20 | IncrementalObject["name"][args] for "Identity" [expr], "Range" [Range args], "Select" [src, crit], "Take" [src, n], "Map" [f, src], "FoldList" [f, x, src], "Permutations" [list], "Subsets" [list], "Tuples" [list, n \| {lists}]: a lazy stateful iterator; sources may be expressions, incremental objects or incremental function instances (consumed, not copied). Values are taken with NextValue; Table/Do iteration over an incremental object is not supported |
| `IncrementalReceive` | added | 6 | IncrementalReceive[] / IncrementalReceive[default] pauses the enclosing running IncrementalFunction; when resumed with "Send" it returns the sent value, with "Next" the default (Null if none); outside one it raises |
| `IncrementalYield` | added | 12 | IncrementalYield[e] pauses the enclosing running IncrementalFunction, which then gives e; outside one it raises |
| `KeyCollisionFunction` | added | 6 | option for JoinAcross (how to handle same-key elements not being joined); an inert option name |
| `KeyValuePattern` | added | 5 | an association or list of rules with a different element matching each pi, in any order; KeyValuePattern[p] = KeyValuePattern[{p}] |
| `LexicographicOrder` | added | 5 | Order of the first differing pair of elements (canonical Order or ordering function p); if one list is a prefix of the other the shorter comes first; strings compare by Characters; any common head; LexicographicOrder[p] operator form |
| `Nearest` | added | 18 | Nearest[data, x] all elements at the smallest distance; Nearest[data, x, n \| {n, r} \| {All, r}]; data as a list, {e1 -> v1, ...}, {e1, ...} -> {v1, ...}, {e1, ...} -> "Element" \| "Index" \| "Distance" \| {props} \| All, or an association; lists of query points thread; distances: \|x - y\| for numbers, the Euclidean norm for vectors, EditDistance for strings, Jaccard dissimilarity for Boolean vectors, or DistanceFunction -> f; Nearest[data] gives a NearestFunction |
| `NearestFunction` | added | 3 | NearestFunction[data] (made by Nearest[data]) applied as nf[x], nf[{x1, ...}], nf[x, n], nf[x, {n, r}] |
| `NearestTo` | added | 3 | operator form: NearestTo[x][elems] = Nearest[elems, x]; NearestTo[x, n \| {n, r}]; applied to a NearestFunction nf it gives nf[x, ...] |
| `NextValue` | added | 20 | NextValue[obj] gives the next value of an incremental object (Failure["IncrementalObjectTerminated", ...] when exhausted) or of a running incremental function instance (as ds["Next"]) |
| `NumericArray` | added | 14 | NumericArray[array, type, method] typed numeric array (types Integer8 ... UnsignedInteger64, Real32, Real64, ComplexReal32, ComplexReal64; methods ClipAndRound (default), Round, Check, ClipAndCheck; Coerce methods raise); stored as NumericArray[values, type]; Normal, Length and Dimensions read it; NumericArray[array] without a type raises |
| `NumericArrayQ` | added | 5 | True for a valid NumericArray; NumericArrayQ[expr, tpatt] also tests the type against a pattern, NumericArrayQ[expr, tpatt, dpatt] the array depth |
| `NumericArrayType` | added | 3 | the element type string of a NumericArray |
| `NumericalOrder` | added | 5 | 1 if e1 < e2, -1 if e1 > e2, 0 if numerically equal (no tolerance); complex numbers by real part then \|imaginary part\|; numeric expressions precede non-numeric ones; otherwise canonical Order (dates and quantities raise) |
| `OrderlessPatternSequence` | added | 3 | matches a sequence of arguments matching p1, p2, ... in any order (the order of the matched arguments is preserved; at most 8 patterns) |
| `Padding` | added | 3 | option of ArrayFilter (and CenterArray-style padding values): a constant, "Fixed", "Periodic", "Reflected", "Reversed" or None |
| `PaddingSize` | added | 3 | option of AudioDelay and other functions giving the amount of padding; an inert option name |
| `PartBehavior` | added | 5 | option of Query and related functions for nonexistent parts; an inert option name |
| `PatternSequence` | added | 6 | PatternSequence[p1, p2, ...] matches a sequence of arguments (also inside Alternatives and Repeated, and named x : PatternSequence[...]); PatternSequence[] matches the empty sequence |
| `RangeSpace` | added | 4 | basis of the range (column space) of a matrix: for exact and symbolic matrices the nonzero rows of RowReduce[Transpose[a]] (generic values for symbols); for approximate matrices the left singular vectors of the nonzero singular values (Tolerance option) |
| `RepeatingElement` | added | 5 | RepeatingElement[spec], RepeatingElement[spec, max \| {min, max} \| {n, {min, max}} \| {{i, n}, {min, max}}]: an inert interpreter/form specification (count specifications are validated; Interpreter and forms are not available) |
| `ReplaceAt` | added | 6 | ReplaceAt[expr, rules, n \| {i, j, ...} \| {{...}, ...}]: Replace (level 0, first matching rule) at the given parts; negative indices, All, Span, keys; repeated positions apply the rules repeatedly; ReplaceAt[rules, pos] operator form |
| `RowsEqual` | added | 3 | option for GridBox (equal row heights); an inert option name |
| `SequenceFold` | added | 3 | SequenceFold[f, {x1, ..., xn}, list]: f of the last n results and the next element (a recurrence); {x} gives Fold, {} applies f to each element; k = n (f of the last n results only, len + 1 steps); k > n + 1 raises |
| `SequenceFoldList` | added | 3 | the seeds followed by the successive results of SequenceFold |
| `SortedBy` | added | 3 | option specifying a function by which to sort the results of a computation; an inert option name (functions accepting it validate it) |
| `SubsetCases` | added | 4 | sublists (in any order, not necessarily contiguous) matching a list pattern, given in the pattern's order; longest matches first, non-overlapping by default (Overlaps -> True gives all); patt -> rhs; n matches; Alternatives tried in order |
| `SubsetCount` | added | 3 | number of sublists matching patt in any order (non-overlapping by default; Overlaps -> True counts all) |
| `SubsetMap` | added | 5 | SubsetMap[f, list, {i, j, ...}] replaces the elements at the given positions by the elements of f[{e_i, e_j, ...}] (f must return a list of the same length); positions {pos1, ...}, i ;; j or part specs with All / Span; SubsetMap[f, pos] operator form |
| `SubsetPosition` | added | 3 | positions {i, j, ...} (in the pattern's order) of sublists matching patt in any order; overlapping by default (Overlaps -> False excludes); first n |
| `SubsetReplace` | added | 3 | replaces sublists matching a rule's pattern (in any order) by its right-hand side, inserted at the position of the element matched first; rules applied in order to untransformed elements; first n replacements; SubsetReplace[rules] operator form |
| `SuchThat` | added | 3 | x \[SuchThat] y: an operator with no built-in meaning (stays unevaluated) |
| `SymmetrizedArray` | added | 7 | SymmetrizedArray[{pos -> val, ...}, dims, sym] with sym Symmetric[slots], Antisymmetric[slots], {} or {{perm, phase}, ...}: stored as SymmetrizedArray[StructuredData[dims, {independent rules, sym}]] (the lexicographically smallest member of each orbit is independent; dependent positions are converted with their phase; pattern positions evaluated per independent component; unspecified entries 0); SymmetrizedArray[list] detects the slot-permutation symmetry; Normal gives the full array |
| `SymmetrizedArrayRules` | added | 3 | independent nonzero rules of a SymmetrizedArray followed by {_, _, ...} -> 0; for an ordinary array, the rules of the array symmetrized with sym (Symmetrize: the phase-weighted group average); without sym the array's own nonzero rules |
| `SymmetrizedDependentComponents` | added | 3 | sorted list of the components related to comp by the symmetry sym (its orbit, including comp) |
| `SymmetrizedIndependentComponents` | added | 4 | independent components (lexicographically smallest orbit members, vanishing orbits excluded) of an array with dimensions dims and symmetry sym, in lexicographic order |
| `SymmetrizedReplacePart` | added | 3 | SymmetrizedReplacePart[sa, {pos -> val, ...}] replaces independent values (dependent positions are mapped to their independent component); SymmetrizedReplacePart[array, rules, sym] symmetrizes first |
| `UniqueElements` | added | 3 | for each list, the elements (without repeats, in order) that are not in any other list; UniqueElements[lists, test] uses test for equivalence |
| `UnitVector` | added | 4 | UnitVector[n, k]: length-n vector with 1 at position k; UnitVector[k] = UnitVector[2, k] |
| `Verbatim` | added | 4 | Verbatim[expr] matches expr literally (blanks inside are not patterns) |
| `Wedge` | added | 6 | x \[Wedge] y: an operator with no built-in meaning (stays unevaluated) |
| `WrapAround` | added | 3 | option for NotebookFind (continue past the end of a document); an inert option name |

| `FourierDFT` | fixed | 3 | X_k = Σ x_j e^(-2πi jk/n) (NumPy fft convention; Wolfram Fourier[] differs by 1/√n and sign); exact for exact input (n ≤ 64), complex floats otherwise; legacy FFT rounded results to 6 digits |

| `FourierTransform` | fixed | 15 | Wolfram convention (1/√(2π)) ∫ f e^(iωt) dt, numerically verified at frequencies of both signs (raises if SymPy's closed form cannot be verified); batch 4: FourierParameters -> {a, b} (numeric) gives Sqrt[\|b\|/(2π)^(1-a)] ∫ f e^(i b ω t) dt, threads over equations and lists |
| `HilbertTransform` | fixed | 3 | discrete Hilbert transform H[x] (imaginary part of SciPy's analytic signal); floats; legacy returned the complex analytic signal x + iH[x] under this name |
| `InverseFFT` | fixed | 4 | x_j = (1/n) Σ X_k e^(2πi jk/n) (NumPy ifft); legacy discarded imaginary parts and rounded |
| `InverseFourierCosTransform` | added | 3 | InverseFourierCosTransform[f, t, ω] = Sqrt[2/π] ∫_0^∞ f cos(ωt) dt (Wolfram default); FourierParameters -> {a, b} (numeric) gives 2 Sqrt[\|b\|/(2π)^(1+a)] times the integral with frequency b ω; the closed form is checked numerically at three frequencies and returned in the form valid for ω > 0 (as Wolfram); threads over equations; unevaluated if SymPy has no closed form |
| `InverseFourierSinTransform` | added | 3 | InverseFourierSinTransform[f, t, ω] = Sqrt[2/π] ∫_0^∞ f sin(ωt) dt (Wolfram default); FourierParameters -> {a, b} (numeric) gives 2 Sqrt[\|b\|/(2π)^(1+a)] times the integral with frequency b ω; the closed form is checked numerically at three frequencies and returned in the form valid for ω > 0 (as Wolfram); threads over equations; unevaluated if SymPy has no closed form |
| `InverseFourierTransform` | fixed | 4 | Wolfram convention (1/√(2π)) ∫ F e^(-iωt) dω, numerically verified; batch 4: FourierParameters -> {a, b} gives Sqrt[\|b\|/(2π)^(1+a)] ∫ F e^(-i b ω t) dω, threads over equations and lists |
| `InverseLaplaceTransform` | fixed | 3 | inverse Laplace transform for t > 0 (Wolfram form); legacy kept SymPy's HeavisideTheta[t] factor |
| `InverseMellinTransform` | added | 3 | InverseMellinTransform[F, s, x] via SymPy (fundamental strip inferred); result checked by transforming back with MellinTransform; unevaluated otherwise |
| `InverseZTransform` | fixed | 17 | InverseZTransform[F, z, n] for rational F (causal, n >= 0, Wolfram convention F(z) = Σ f(n) z^-n): partial fractions in w = 1/z give c/(1 - p w)^k -> c Binomial[n + k - 1, k - 1] p^n and c w^m -> c DiscreteDelta[n - m]; F must stay finite as z -> Infinity (causal sequence). Every result is checked against the first 12 coefficients of the series of F at z = Infinity. Non-rational F stays unevaluated; legacy returned the input |
| `LaplaceTransform` | fixed | 9 | ∫_0^∞ f e^(-st) dt (same convention as Wolfram/SymPy), convergence conditions dropped; batch 4: derivatives of undefined functions (LaplaceTransform[f'[t], t, s] -> s LaplaceTransform[f[t], t, s] - f[0]), HeavisideTheta and DiracDelta shifts, threads over equations and lists; unevaluated when SymPy cannot transform |
| `MellinTransform` | added | 6 | MellinTransform[f, x, s] = ∫_0^∞ x^(s-1) f dx (Wolfram); convergence strip dropped (generic value), checked numerically at two real s inside the strip; unevaluated if SymPy has no closed form |
| `WaveletTransform` | fixed | 4 | WaveletTransform[list] or [list, "Haar"\|"db1"]: full Haar decomposition {cA_n, cD_n, ..., cD_1} (PyWavelets wavedec ordering, orthonormal 1/√2 scaling), exact; length must be a power of 2; other wavelets raise (legacy needed PyWavelets, which is not a dependency) |
| `ZTransform` | fixed | 5 | Wolfram ZTransform[f, n, z] = Σ_{n=0}^∞ f(n) z^(-n) via symbolic summation (convergence conditions dropped); unevaluated when the sum has no closed form; legacy always returned itself |

### types (T1)

| Function | Status | Cases | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TypeCheck` | added | 8 | TypeCheck[expr] evaluates expr with type checking in report mode and gives <\|"Result" -> value, "Mismatches" -> {<\|"Function" -> f, "Position" -> i, "Value" -> v, "Expected" -> type\|>, ...}\|>. A mismatch is a concrete argument (or result) of the wrong kind; symbolic arguments never are. Position is the argument number, "Result" or "Count" (wrong number of arguments for a declared function). MikoshiLang extension. Check: exact; seeded mismatches in tests. |
| `TypeMatchQ` | added | 27 | TypeMatchQ[value, type] is True when value is known to have the type (Integer, Real, ListOf[t], t1 \| t2, ...) and False otherwise, including for symbolic values whose type is not known yet (x is not an Integer). An unknown type raises ValueError. MikoshiLang extension. Check: exact; reference values for every type in tests. |
| `TypeSignature` | added | 15 | TypeSignature[f] gives the signatures of f as {{t1, t2, ...} -> result, ...}: user declarations in this session first, otherwise the library signature read from the Python annotations of each implementation (RepeatedNull[t] stands for any number of further arguments); {} if none is known. TypeSignature[f, {t1, ...} -> r] declares a signature for a user function (repeat it for overloads) and returns it; RepeatedNull[t] is allowed last. The declaration is checked only when checking is on (Session(check_types=...) or TypeCheck). A non-symbol f raises TypeError; an unknown type raises ValueError. MikoshiLang extension. Check: exact; the signatures of library heads are compared with their annotations in tests. |

## Quarantine decision for the remaining names

The legacy `extended*.py` modules defined about 5,508 further names. 148 of them were reviewed during the audit:

* those with a real Wolfram meaning and a correct implementation were promoted or recorded under their area;
* 6 that the kernel already evaluates correctly (`AtomQ`, `ListQ`, `StringQ`, `ToString`, `ReplaceAll`,
  `Distance2D`) were recorded as kernel heads;
* `YoungTableaux` was briefly quarantined because the legacy version returned the size of a single tableau. It is now properly implemented and promoted (see above).

**Decision for the remaining names: keep them quarantined.** These are the other names on the list in
`mikoshilang/quarantined.json`. They raise
`NotImplementedError: <name> belongs to the quarantined, unreviewed library`.

Sampling during the audit showed why. The great majority are one-line rules that:

* hard-code a placeholder under a broad name. For example, `ANOVAOneWay[...]` always returns `1.0`,
  `AFTModel[...]` returns `{0}`, `ARCH[q]` returns the string `"ARCH(q)"`, and `AGCSignal[x]` is the identity;
* duplicate a reviewed head under a near-synonym.

Promoting any of them requires an actual implementation plus at least 3 differential cases, as with the 26
promoted heads. `tests/library/test_coverage.py::test_every_quarantined_name_raises` calls every remaining
quarantined name and fails if any returns a value.
